# Agentic AI Coding Loop

Agentic AI Coding Loop is a local orchestration project for running a controlled, phase-gated build-review-fix workflow between Codex and Claude Code.

Codex acts as the planner, task-state manager, reviewer, validator, and fix-prompt generator. Claude Code acts as the implementation agent for the active phase only. A local orchestrator manages prompts, artifacts, validation logs, and loop state. A human approves movement between phases and separately approves any eventual commit.

## Goal

Reduce manual copy/paste between Codex and Claude Code while keeping the workflow auditable, scoped, and safe.

## MVP

The first version focuses on a small local loop:

- read a human-provided objective
- let Codex maintain `TASK.md` and active phase state
- generate a focused Claude implementation prompt
- let Claude implement the requested phase
- capture Claude's structured summary
- capture `git diff` and `git status`
- run validation commands and save logs
- let Codex review the prompt, summary, diff, and logs
- generate a fix prompt if needed
- stop after each approved phase until the human starts the next one

## Workflow

This section describes the end-to-end workflow the shipped orchestrator at `scripts/agent_loop.py` (Phase 3A-3E) drives on a clean clone. New operators should start with [docs/usage.md](docs/usage.md) for the clean-clone walkthrough; the `## Running The Loop Manually (Phase 1)` section below is preserved as a hand-driven fallback against the same artifact formats.

1. Human provides the project objective or updates the desired outcome.
2. Codex updates `TASK.md`, `.agent-loop/current-task.md`, and `.agent-loop/current-phase.md` for the active phase.
3. Codex writes `.agent-loop/claude-prompt.md`.
4. Claude Code implements only the active phase and writes `.agent-loop/claude-summary.md`.
5. The orchestrator captures diff, status, and validation logs.
6. Codex writes `.agent-loop/codex-review.md` with one verdict:
   - `APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW`
   - `NEEDS_FIXES`
   - `FAILED_REQUIRES_HUMAN`
7. If fixes are required, Codex classifies review findings by ownership: supported Codex-owned follow-up is handled directly, and Claude-owned fixes are synchronized into `.agent-loop/fix-prompt.md`.
8. Claude applies targeted fixes within the same phase.
9. The loop repeats until the phase is approved, escalated, or reaches max cycles.
10. When a phase is approved, the system stops until the human starts the next phase.
11. The system never commits without separate human approval.

Run-mode selection, run controls, and the plain-English progress console remain queued for later Fix Phase C slices.

## Repository Files

Top-level project files:

- `README.md`: public project overview
- `AGENTS.md`: operating rules, review standards, and artifact formats
- `CLAUDE.md`: Claude Code implementation contract
- `ROADMAP.md`: phased delivery plan
- `TASK.md`: Codex-maintained task and phase record derived from the human objective

Loop artifacts:

```text
.agent-loop/
  phase-plan.md
  current-task.md
  current-phase.md
  claude-prompt.md
  claude-summary.md
  git-diff.patch
  git-status.log
  test-output.log
  lint-output.log
  typecheck-output.log
  build-output.log
  codex-review.md
  fix-prompt.md
  loop-state.json
```

## Safety

- no auto-commit
- no auto-push
- no hidden validation failures
- no trusting summary text over actual diffs and logs
- no phase advancement without human approval
- no final commit without human approval

## Ownership Rules

- Claude Code is the default implementation editor for code, tests, scripts, configuration, and other non-state repository files during an active phase
- Codex should normally route implementation changes to Claude Code and edit directly only when the issue has been intentionally assigned to Codex
- planning-state hygiene issues should be treated as Codex-resolved and fixed directly by Codex
- `.agent-loop/loop-state.json`, `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, and the evidence files are owned by the orchestrator or the scripts that generate them; they must not be fabricated by hand outside explicitly assigned implementation work
- if ownership is ambiguous, the change should be routed to Claude Code or escalated for human clarification rather than guessed
- when continuing an active implementation phase, Codex should first summarize the remaining implementation gap, classify it as Claude-resolved or Codex-resolved, and if it is Claude-resolved write the precise Claude Code task prompt before implementation proceeds
- when the human starts a new phase or sub-phase, Codex should first provide a rundown of what will be implemented, split the work into Codex-resolved and Claude-resolved responsibilities, send the Claude-resolved portion as a precise Claude Code task prompt, and then perform only the Codex-resolved portion directly

## Running The Loop Manually (Phase 1)

Phase 1 documented the manual file-based loop. The by-hand workflow below is preserved as a fallback even though the shipped orchestrator (Phase 3A-3E) is now the canonical driver and is documented in [docs/usage.md](docs/usage.md). Run the manual loop against the artifact formats defined in `AGENTS.md` when the orchestrator cannot be invoked (hand-driven cycle reproduction, audit walk-throughs). One full manual cycle:

1. Codex updates `TASK.md`, `.agent-loop/current-task.md`, and `.agent-loop/current-phase.md` for the active phase.
2. Codex writes the implementation prompt to `.agent-loop/claude-prompt.md` using the required Claude task format.
3. A human pastes that prompt into Claude Code.
4. Claude Code implements only the active phase and writes `.agent-loop/claude-summary.md` using the required Claude implementation summary format.
5. A human captures evidence into `.agent-loop/`:
   - `git diff > .agent-loop/git-diff.patch`
   - `git status > .agent-loop/git-status.log`
   - test, lint, typecheck, and build output to `.agent-loop/test-output.log`, `.agent-loop/lint-output.log`, `.agent-loop/typecheck-output.log`, `.agent-loop/build-output.log` (or record "Not run" explicitly).
6. A human pastes the prompt, summary, diff, and logs into Codex.
7. Codex writes `.agent-loop/codex-review.md` with exactly one verdict: `APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW`, `NEEDS_FIXES`, or `FAILED_REQUIRES_HUMAN`.
8. If the verdict is `NEEDS_FIXES`, Codex-owned follow-up is applied first where supported, `.agent-loop/fix-prompt.md` is regenerated from the Claude-owned review issues, and the cycle repeats from step 3 within `max_cycles`.
9. The cycle stops for human approval before any commit, and again before the next phase begins.

`.agent-loop/loop-state.json` is an orchestrator-owned runtime artifact in the shipped system: the activator initializes it at phase activation and `scripts/agent_loop.py` updates the per-cycle fields (`status`, `cycle_count`, `last_verdict`, `last_verdict_phase`, `claude_version`, `codex_version`, `orchestrator_version`, `approval_mode`, `awaiting_human_for`) during a run, while the Codex- or human-owned fields (`phase`, `sub_phase`, `task`, `max_cycles`, `contract_version`) come from the Phase 4C activation step. In the manual-fallback flow above a human edits the same orchestrator-writable fields against the Phase 3A Orchestrator Contract write-ownership rules; the file is never recorded by hand as the default workflow.

Fix Phase C3 (PRD Intake UX) is now the active sub-phase in the canonical phase/task artifacts. It ships the second runtime slice under the Fix Phase C1 contract: a guided PRD section (visible by default, second in the ordered top-level vocabulary) where a non-technical operator picks a PRD file and sees a plain-English state (no PRD selected / PRD required before running / PRD file could not be read / PRD ready) plus a bounded PRD preview (title + short summary excerpt, char-limited) without dumping the full PRD body into the default view. Run controls and the plain-English progress console remain queued for later Fix Phase C slices. All three Phase 5A approval modes (`review`, `strict`, `autonomous`) remain shipped runtime behavior, and the local adapter path remains available for automatic Claude/Codex invocation on a configured machine.

Phase 10T (MCP Read-Only Assistance In Desktop App, complete) ships the first MCP runtime slice and the first user-selectable read-only MCP assistance surface in the desktop app. The new `build_desktop_mcp_assistance_view(controller_root: Path) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator (HaltError is soft-failed to `loop_state=None` per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule), and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10t-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, `current_loop_state_status`, `controller_loop_state_approval_mode`, `phase_10t_runtime_available=True`, `phase_10u_runtime_available=False` (mutation-capable MCP runtime is deferred to Phase 10U+ per `ROADMAP.md`), closed mirrors of the Phase 10S `permission_classes` / `capability_categories` / `enablement_states` / `approval_requirements` enumerations, a `servers` list assembled from the closed `_DESKTOP_MCP_ASSISTANCE_REGISTRY` (one descriptor per MCP server matching the [Phase 10S MCP server selection UX contract](docs/mcp-server-selection-ux-contract.md) descriptor shape), and the literal `DESKTOP_MCP_ASSISTANCE_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. The initial registry ships three entries spanning every closed permission class so the renderer / refusal-path tests can exercise every closed branch: a `local_repo_docs` read-only advisory server (capability_categories `[read_only_advisory]`), a `local_browser_inspector` read-only browser/app inspection server (capability_categories `[browser_app_inspection]`), and a `github_pr_comment_poster` deferred-mutating server (capability_categories `[read_only_advisory, deferred_mutating]`) that ANCHORS the Phase 10S `deferred_mutating_class` refusal short-circuit. Every descriptor passes the pure `_desktop_mcp_assistance_validate_descriptor(...)` validator which refuses fail-closed via HaltError on any missing required field, wrong-typed value, unknown `permission_class`, unknown `capability_categories` member, unknown `approval_requirements` member, or unknown enablement-step type. Per-server enablement state is computed by `_desktop_mcp_assistance_compute_enablement_state(...)` which short-circuits every `deferred_mutating_class` server to `refused_deferred_runtime` (routing the operator at Phase 10U+ via the per-server `deferred_runtime_marker`) and otherwise returns `enabled_pending_runtime` only when every Phase 10S approval requirement is satisfied (`operator_acknowledged_safety_copy`, `operator_supplied_identity`, `approval_mode_supports_enablement` (review / autonomous; strict mode refuses fail-closed per Phase 5C), `phase_10t_runtime_available`, `policy_rule_permits_enablement`). Per-server approval state is computed by `_desktop_mcp_assistance_compute_approval_state(...)` which returns a per-requirement `{satisfied, reason}` dict so a renderer can show exactly which requirements remain unmet without collapsing them into a single boolean. The Phase 10T fix cycle wires per-session operator-input plumbing through the view builder so the shipped bounded surface actually exposes a selectable read-only MCP assistance path: `build_desktop_mcp_assistance_view(controller_root, *, operator_inputs=None)` now accepts an optional `operator_inputs` dict (`identity` per-session operator-supplied string, `acknowledged_server_ids` per-session per-server safety-copy acknowledgement set, `policy_refused_server_ids` per-session additive policy-refusal set; normalized by `_desktop_mcp_assistance_normalize_operator_inputs(...)` which refuses fail-closed via HaltError on a non-dict / wrong-typed value) that threads each per-server requirement to the descriptor builder. Per the Phase 10S contract these inputs are held in-memory only, NEVER persisted to disk, NEVER auto-filled from `$USER`, `whoami`, an MCP server session, a browser session, a packaging-time-configured identity, any persistent MCP-side identity store, or any saved preference; NEVER carried across sessions. The `cmd_view_desktop_mcp_assistance(args)` CLI exposes three new optional flags that thread the operator inputs explicitly per invocation: `--operator-identity <NAME>` (sets `identity`), `--acknowledge-server <ID>` (repeatable; appends to `acknowledged_server_ids`), `--policy-refuse-server <ID>` (repeatable; appends to `policy_refused_server_ids`). The `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)` Tk surface exposes an `Operator identity` `tk.Entry` and a per-server `Acknowledge safety copy` `tk.Checkbutton` bag rebuilt each poll; the `_refresh()` callback reads the live `StringVar` / `BooleanVar` state, calls `build_desktop_mcp_assistance_view(controller_root, operator_inputs=...)` with the per-session inputs, and re-renders the per-server action buttons so the operator can actually flip a read-only server to `enabled_pending_runtime` mid-session without restarting the window. Even with every operator input satisfied the deferred-mutating server stays `refused_deferred_runtime` (the Phase 10S short-circuit is NOT bypassable via operator inputs), and a dedicated regression test anchors that boundary exactly. Each server descriptor carries the Phase 10S `enablement_steps` tuple drawn from the closed `DESKTOP_RUN_PROFILE_STEP_TYPES = ("cli", "manual_edit")` enumeration plus a derived `command` summary (each step tagged with `[cli]` / `[manual-edit]` and joined with ` && `, intentionally NOT runnable as-is so a single-line scan still sees every step) and a multi-line `clipboard_payload`. The view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `mcp_assistance_view` sub-view alongside the existing `status_view` / `controls_view` / `dashboard_view` / `setup_view` / `run_profiles_view` / `project_start_view`, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders an `MCP Assistance (Phase 10T)` section that reuses `render_desktop_mcp_assistance_text(...)` verbatim - the per-server lines surface each safety-copy sentence, each capability label, each per-requirement approval state, the enablement reason, the `deferred_runtime_marker`, and each enablement step on its own indented line with explicit `[mcp-server]` / `[mcp-safety]` / `[mcp-capability]` / `[mcp-approval]` / `[mcp-enablement]` / `[refused]` / `[deferred-runtime]` / `[cli]` / `[manual-edit]` tags so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10T slice wires a third real Tkinter button row beneath the existing Phase 10Q and Phase 10R control panels inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: each MCP server entry gets a real `tk.Button` rendered via the new `build_desktop_mcp_assistance_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function (each control descriptor carries `id` / `label` (display name plus the closed enablement-state tag) / `enabled` (True only when `enablement_state == enabled_pending_runtime`; `deferred_mutating_class` servers are always disabled) / `permission_class` / `enablement_state` / `enablement_reason` / `clipboard_payload` / `deferred_runtime_marker` / `refusal_reason_template` / `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` / `category='advisory_selection_ux'`), and a click on a button is a PURE clipboard-copy operation that NEVER spawns a subprocess and NEVER contacts the MCP server (the Tk shell shares the Phase 10Q `_copy_to_clipboard(...)` helper and the same non-canonical status-caption widget). The three Tk button rows (Phase 10Q + Phase 10R + Phase 10T) are re-rendered every poll cycle from the latest view so eligibility transitions (driven by canonical loop-state.status / approval_mode changes and per-server descriptor updates) propagate to button `state="disabled"` / `"normal"` between polls. The new `view-desktop-mcp-assistance` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_mcp_assistance(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds; `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow (matching the Phase 10M / 10N / 10P / 10Q / 10R contracts) and omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-mcp-assistance] REFUSED: ...` stderr line. Per the [Phase 10O MCP integration contract](docs/mcp-integration-contract.md) and the [Phase 10S MCP server selection UX contract](docs/mcp-server-selection-ux-contract.md) this handler NEVER mutates any canonical artifact, NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, NEVER advances loop-state, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawns a subprocess (a dedicated regression test patches all six Python entry points and asserts zero calls during a render), NEVER opens a network socket (a dedicated regression test patches `socket.socket` and asserts zero opens during a render), NEVER contacts any MCP server in-process, NEVER auto-fills any operator-identity field, NEVER widens the Phase 10I three-control library-callable cap (a dedicated regression test anchors the cap exactly at `view-external-status` / `view-external-controls` / `inspect-external-target`), and NEVER introduces an MCP-side database / preference store / recents list / identity token / session token. This slice does NOT introduce: any actual networked MCP transport, in-process MCP fetch, browser inspection runtime, or app inspection runtime (the descriptor registry surfaces the selection UX only; actual MCP fetch transport is deferred to a later Phase 10 runtime slice); mutation-capable MCP actions (Phase 10U+); RAG runtime, GitHub integration, packaging / code-signing / auto-update pipelines, system-tray surfaces, or controlled-concurrency runtime; automatic next-phase activation; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, the Phase 10O MCP integration contract, the Phase 10P desktop setup runtime, the Phase 10Q run-profiles runtime, the Phase 10R project-start runtime, or the Phase 10S MCP server selection UX contract; or any Git automation.

Phase 10U (MCP Action Guardrails And Per-Tool Approval Policies, complete) layers the bounded mutation-action guardrails contract on top of the shipped Phase 10T read-only assistance surface. The new `build_desktop_mcp_action_guardrails_view(controller_root: Path, *, operator_inputs: dict | None = None) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator (HaltError is soft-failed per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule), and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10u-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, `current_loop_state_status`, `controller_loop_state_approval_mode`, `phase_10t_runtime_available=True`, `phase_10u_runtime_available=False` (this slice ships the CONTRACT only; the mutation-dispatch runtime is deferred to a future Phase 10 runtime slice tracked in `ROADMAP.md`), closed enumeration mirrors of `effect_classes`, `mutation_scopes`, `approval_policies`, `enablement_states`, `approval_requirements`, `audit_required_fields`, `refusal_reasons`, an `operator_inputs` per-poll mirror, an `actions` list assembled from the closed `_DESKTOP_MCP_ACTION_GUARDRAILS_REGISTRY`, and the literal `DESKTOP_MCP_ACTION_GUARDRAILS_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. The initial registry ships three concrete mutation-capable actions spanning every closed effect class (`github_post_pr_comment` -> `posts_external_artifact`; `github_edit_pr_metadata` -> `mutates_external_state`; `github_trigger_workflow` -> `triggers_remote_workflow`), all targeting the Phase 10T `github_pr_comment_poster` deferred-mutating server so the descriptor cross-check (every action MUST reference a `deferred_mutating_class` parent server in `_DESKTOP_MCP_ASSISTANCE_REGISTRY`) is exercised end-to-end. The pure `_desktop_mcp_action_guardrails_validate_descriptor(...)` validator refuses fail-closed via HaltError on any missing required field, wrong-typed value, unknown `effect_class`, unknown `mutation_scope`, unknown `approval_policy`, unknown `default_enablement_state`, unknown `approval_requirements` member, unknown `audit_required_fields` member, unknown enablement-step type, or unknown / non-`deferred_mutating_class` parent server id. Per-action approval state is computed by `_desktop_mcp_action_guardrails_compute_approval_state(...)` which returns a per-requirement `{satisfied, reason}` dict spanning seven closed requirements: `operator_per_action_acknowledgement`, `operator_supplied_identity`, `approval_mode_supports_action` (review / autonomous; strict mode refuses fail-closed per Phase 5C), `phase_10u_runtime_available`, `policy_pack_permits_action` (operator-side additive allow-list; the policy pack is NOT a refusal set, it is an explicit opt-in per-session list per the Phase 10U fail-closed default), `audit_log_appendable`, and `dry_run_payload_reviewed`. Per-action enablement state is computed by `_desktop_mcp_action_guardrails_compute_enablement_state(...)` which surfaces three closed states (`disabled_by_default` / `enabled_pending_runtime` / `refused_until_policy_update`): runtime-side requirements (`phase_10u_runtime_available`, `audit_log_appendable`) gate `refused_until_policy_update`; operator-side requirements gate `disabled_by_default`; promotion to `enabled_pending_runtime` requires every requirement satisfied. Since `phase_10u_runtime_available` is hard-coded `False` in this slice every action ALWAYS surfaces as `refused_until_policy_update` regardless of operator input - the headline boundary is anchored by a dedicated regression test that supplies every operator-side approval input and still asserts the fail-closed state. Each action descriptor carries the closed `audit_required_fields` tuple plus a `_desktop_mcp_action_guardrails_audit_entry_shape(...)`-computed `audit_entry_canonical_shape` dict (one `field: None` placeholder per required audit field; a future runtime slice MUST persist this exact envelope when dispatching the action). The closed `MCP_ACTION_REFUSAL_REASONS` vocabulary pins the seven legal refusal reasons (`approval_mode_strict`, `runtime_not_available`, `policy_pack_refuses_action`, `operator_identity_missing`, `operator_acknowledgement_missing`, `dry_run_unreviewed`, `audit_log_unappendable`) so a future runtime slice cannot invent ad-hoc refusal reasons. Operator inputs flow through `_desktop_mcp_action_guardrails_normalize_operator_inputs(...)` which accepts `{identity, acknowledged_action_ids, session_acknowledged_action_ids, dry_run_reviewed_action_ids, policy_permitted_action_ids}` (a non-dict / wrong-typed value is refused fail-closed via HaltError); per the Phase 10S source-of-truth preservation rule these inputs are held in-memory only, NEVER persisted to disk, NEVER auto-filled from any OS / packaging / browser-session source, NEVER carried across sessions. The Phase 10U fix cycle splits acknowledgement into two non-overlapping surfaces so the approval-policy enum models distinct executable contract logic rather than only descriptive text: `acknowledged_action_ids` carries per-INVOCATION acknowledgements and only satisfies actions whose `approval_policy == "per_action_explicit_approval"`; `session_acknowledged_action_ids` carries SESSION-SCOPED acknowledgements and only satisfies actions whose `approval_policy == "per_session_explicit_approval"`. Actions whose `approval_policy == "refused_until_policy_update"` are NEVER satisfiable at the policy level regardless of operator input. The new `cmd_view_desktop_mcp_action_guardrails(args)` CLI exposes five optional per-session operator-input flags (`--operator-identity <NAME>`, `--acknowledge-action <ID>` repeatable, `--session-acknowledge-action <ID>` repeatable, `--dry-run-reviewed-action <ID>` repeatable, `--policy-permit-action <ID>` repeatable) that thread the inputs through to the view builder explicitly per invocation. The view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `mcp_action_guardrails_view` sub-view alongside the existing seven sub-views, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders an `MCP Action Guardrails (Phase 10U)` section that reuses `render_desktop_mcp_action_guardrails_text(...)` verbatim - the per-action lines surface each closed enumeration value, each per-requirement approval state, the enablement reason, the `deferred_runtime_marker`, the per-action audit-required-field list, the per-action enablement steps, and the per-action refusal reason on its own indented line with explicit `[mcp-action]` / `[mcp-effect]` / `[mcp-mutation]` / `[mcp-policy]` / `[mcp-approval]` / `[mcp-enablement]` / `[mcp-audit]` / `[refused]` / `[deferred-runtime]` / `[cli]` / `[manual-edit]` tags so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10U slice wires a fourth real Tkinter button row beneath the existing Phase 10Q + Phase 10R + Phase 10T control panels inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: each MCP action gets either an `Acknowledge action (per-invocation): <name>` Checkbutton (rendered when `approval_policy == per_action_explicit_approval`) OR a `Session-acknowledge action: <name>` Checkbutton (rendered when `approval_policy == per_session_explicit_approval`) so the policy-relevant acknowledgement surface matches the executable approval-policy contract exposed by the view builder (the Tk surface cannot accidentally surface a per-invocation acknowledgement that does not actually satisfy a per-session policy and vice versa), plus a `Dry-run reviewed: <name>` Checkbutton and a `Policy pack permits: <name>` Checkbutton (per-session `tk.BooleanVar` only; closing the window discards every value), plus a real `tk.Button` rendered via the new `build_desktop_mcp_action_guardrails_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function (each control descriptor carries `id` / `label` (display name plus the closed enablement-state tag) / `enabled` (always False in this slice; promoted only when `enablement_state == enabled_pending_runtime`) / `effect_class` / `mutation_scope` / `approval_policy` / `enablement_state` / `enablement_reason` / `clipboard_payload` / `deferred_runtime_marker` / `refusal_reason_template` / `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` / `category='mutation_action_guardrail'`). The operator identity Entry is reused from the Phase 10T panel so the operator types it once per session. Each `_refresh()` poll reads live `StringVar` / `BooleanVar` state, calls `build_desktop_mcp_action_guardrails_view(controller_root, operator_inputs=...)` with the per-session inputs, and re-renders the per-action Checkbuttons + button row so eligibility transitions propagate to button `state="disabled"` / `"normal"` between polls. Even with every operator input satisfied every button stays disabled (the Phase 10U fail-closed default is NOT bypassable via operator inputs), and a dedicated regression test anchors that boundary exactly. The new `view-desktop-mcp-action-guardrails` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_mcp_action_guardrails(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds; `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow and omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-mcp-action-guardrails] REFUSED: ...` stderr line. Per the [Phase 10O MCP integration contract](docs/mcp-integration-contract.md), the [Phase 10S MCP server selection UX contract](docs/mcp-server-selection-ux-contract.md), and the Phase 10T read-only assistance surface this handler NEVER mutates any canonical artifact, NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, NEVER advances loop-state, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawns a subprocess (a dedicated regression test patches all six Python entry points and asserts zero calls during a render), NEVER opens a network socket (a dedicated regression test patches `socket.socket` and asserts zero opens during a render), NEVER contacts any MCP server in-process, NEVER auto-fills any operator-identity field, NEVER widens the Phase 10I three-control library-callable cap (a dedicated regression test anchors the cap exactly at `view-external-status` / `view-external-controls` / `inspect-external-target`), NEVER introduces an MCP-side database / preference store / recents list / identity token / session token, and NEVER actually dispatches a mutation. This slice does NOT introduce: any actual networked MCP transport, in-process MCP fetch, mutation-capable MCP runtime, GitHub API request, dry-run mutation payload generation, audit-log writer, or policy-pack persistence (the registry surfaces the per-action approval-policy contract only; actual dispatch is deferred to a later Phase 10 runtime slice); RAG runtime, packaging / code-signing / auto-update pipelines, system-tray surfaces, or controlled-concurrency runtime; automatic next-phase activation; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, the Phase 10O MCP integration contract, the Phase 10P desktop setup runtime, the Phase 10Q run-profiles runtime, the Phase 10R project-start runtime, the Phase 10S MCP server selection UX contract, or the Phase 10T MCP read-only assistance surface; or any Git automation.

Phase 10V (RAG Source Selection Contract And Desktop UX, complete) ships the first bounded RAG source selection contract and desktop UX surface so operators can BROWSE repo-local docs, PRDs, notes, standards, and evidence sources from the desktop app and see per-source provenance, freshness, and the advisory-vs-canonical mirror rule before any retrieval runtime ships. The contract is pinned at [docs/rag-source-selection-contract.md](docs/rag-source-selection-contract.md). The new `build_desktop_rag_source_selection_view(controller_root: Path, *, operator_inputs: dict | None = None) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator (HaltError is soft-failed per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule), and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10v-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, `current_loop_state_status`, `controller_loop_state_approval_mode`, `phase_10v_runtime_available=False` (this slice ships the CONTRACT and the SELECTION UX only; the retrieval runtime is deferred to a future Phase 10 runtime slice tracked in `ROADMAP.md`), `freshness_stale_threshold_seconds=30*24*3600`, closed enumeration mirrors of `source_types`, `provenance_categories`, `advisory_labeling_rules`, `freshness_states`, `enablement_states`, `approval_requirements`, an `operator_inputs` per-poll mirror, a `sources` list assembled from the closed `_DESKTOP_RAG_SOURCE_SELECTION_REGISTRY`, and the literal `DESKTOP_RAG_SOURCE_SELECTION_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. The initial registry ships five entries that together cover every closed `source_type` (`repo_local_standards_agents_md` -> `repo_local_standards` / `AGENTS.md`; `repo_local_docs_readme` -> `repo_local_docs` / `README.md`; `repo_local_prds_task_md` -> `repo_local_prds` / `TASK.md`; `repo_local_notes_codex_review` -> `repo_local_notes` / `.agent-loop/codex-review.md`; `repo_local_evidence_git_diff` -> `repo_local_evidence` / `.agent-loop/git-diff.patch`); none of the shipped entries carries `advisory_label_rule == "refused_until_policy_update"` (every shipped entry uses `advisory_only_no_canonical_substitution` or `advisory_with_provenance_required`), so the labeling-rule short-circuit refusal branch is exercised by a focused test that constructs a mutated descriptor with the refused rule rather than by a shipped registry entry. The pure `_desktop_rag_source_selection_validate_descriptor(...)` validator refuses fail-closed via HaltError on any missing required field, wrong-typed value, unknown `source_type`, unknown `provenance`, unknown `advisory_label_rule`, unknown `approval_requirements` member, or unknown selection-step type, AND (Phase 10V fix cycle) on any `path_canonical_rel` that is not a POSIX-style repo-relative path bounded inside the controller root: a backslash, a leading `/`, a Windows-style drive prefix (`C:` / `C:/foo`), or any `..` parent-directory segment is refused fail-closed so a future malformed registry entry cannot silently widen the bounded repo-local source model. Per-source freshness is derived per-poll via the pure `_desktop_rag_source_selection_probe_freshness(...)` helper that consults `Path.stat()` ONLY (the surface NEVER reads source CONTENT; a dedicated regression test writes a unique sentinel to every registered source path on disk and asserts the sentinel does NOT appear in either the view dict or the rendered text); the four closed freshness states (`fresh`, `stale`, `missing`, `unknown`) are gated on the `RAG_SOURCE_FRESHNESS_STALE_THRESHOLD_SECONDS = 30 * 24 * 3600` constant. Per-source approval state is computed by `_desktop_rag_source_selection_compute_approval_state(...)` which returns a per-requirement `{satisfied, reason}` dict spanning five closed requirements: `operator_acknowledged_advisory_labeling`, `operator_supplied_identity`, `approval_mode_supports_selection` (review / autonomous; strict mode refuses fail-closed per Phase 5C), `phase_10v_runtime_available`, and `source_path_present_in_repo` (derived per-poll from the freshness probe). Per-source enablement state is computed by `_desktop_rag_source_selection_compute_enablement_state(...)` which surfaces three closed states (`disabled_by_default` / `enabled_pending_runtime` / `refused_until_policy_update`): the labeling-rule short-circuit refuses fail-closed when `advisory_label_rule == "refused_until_policy_update"`; runtime-side requirements (`phase_10v_runtime_available`, `source_path_present_in_repo`) gate `refused_until_policy_update`; operator-side requirements gate `disabled_by_default`; promotion to `enabled_pending_runtime` requires every requirement satisfied. Since `phase_10v_runtime_available` is hard-coded `False` in this slice every source ALWAYS surfaces as `refused_until_policy_update` regardless of operator input - the headline boundary is anchored by a dedicated regression test that supplies every operator-side approval input and still asserts the fail-closed state. The closed `RAG_ADVISORY_LABELING_RULES` enum pins the three legal labeling rules (`advisory_only_no_canonical_substitution`, `advisory_with_provenance_required`, `refused_until_policy_update`) so the future runtime slice cannot silently downgrade a source whose retrieval result was supposed to require provenance into a bare advisory result. Operator inputs flow through `_desktop_rag_source_selection_normalize_operator_inputs(...)` which accepts `{identity, acknowledged_source_ids}` (a non-dict / wrong-typed value is refused fail-closed via HaltError); per the Phase 10S source-of-truth preservation rule these inputs are held in-memory only, NEVER persisted to disk, NEVER auto-filled from any OS / packaging / browser-session source, NEVER carried across sessions. The new `cmd_view_desktop_rag_source_selection(args)` CLI exposes two optional per-session operator-input flags (`--operator-identity <NAME>`, `--acknowledge-source <ID>` repeatable) that thread the inputs through to the view builder explicitly per invocation. The view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `rag_source_selection_view` sub-view alongside the existing eight sub-views, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders a `RAG Source Selection (Phase 10V)` section that reuses `render_desktop_rag_source_selection_text(...)` verbatim - the per-source lines surface the source description, provenance, advisory-labeling rule, freshness (state + last_modified UTC + size), each per-requirement approval state, the enablement reason, the `deferred_runtime_marker`, and each selection step on its own indented line with explicit `[rag-source]` / `[rag-provenance]` / `[rag-freshness]` / `[rag-advisory]` / `[rag-approval]` / `[rag-enablement]` / `[refused]` / `[deferred-runtime]` / `[cli]` / `[manual-edit]` tags so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10V slice wires a fifth real Tkinter button row beneath the existing Phase 10Q + Phase 10R + Phase 10T + Phase 10U control panels inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: each RAG source gets a per-session `Acknowledge advisory-labeling: <name>` `tk.Checkbutton` (per-session `tk.BooleanVar` only; closing the window discards every value), plus a real `tk.Button` rendered via the new `build_desktop_rag_source_selection_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function (each control descriptor carries `id` / `label` (display name plus the closed enablement-state tag) / `enabled` (always False in this slice; promoted only when `enablement_state == enabled_pending_runtime`) / `source_type` / `provenance` / `advisory_label_rule` / `freshness_state` / `enablement_state` / `enablement_reason` / `clipboard_payload` / `deferred_runtime_marker` / `refusal_reason_template` / `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` / `category='rag_source_selection_ux'`). The operator identity Entry is reused from the Phase 10T panel so the operator types it once per session. Each `_refresh()` poll reads live `StringVar` / `BooleanVar` state, calls `build_desktop_rag_source_selection_view(controller_root, operator_inputs=...)` with the per-session inputs, and re-renders the per-source Checkbuttons + button row so eligibility transitions propagate to button `state="disabled"` / `"normal"` between polls. The new `view-desktop-rag-source-selection` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_rag_source_selection(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds; `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow and omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-rag-source-selection] REFUSED: ...` stderr line. Per the Phase 10S source-of-truth preservation rule and the Phase 10O advisory-vs-canonical mirror rule this handler NEVER mutates any canonical artifact, NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, NEVER advances loop-state, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawns a subprocess (a dedicated regression test patches all six Python entry points and asserts zero calls during a render), NEVER opens a network socket (a dedicated regression test patches `socket.socket` and asserts zero opens during a render), NEVER reads source CONTENT (the dedicated sentinel-write regression test anchors this exactly), NEVER builds an embeddings index, NEVER persists a retrieval cache, NEVER subscribes to a background watcher, NEVER auto-fills any operator-identity field, NEVER widens the Phase 10I three-control library-callable cap (a dedicated regression test anchors the cap exactly at `view-external-status` / `view-external-controls` / `inspect-external-target`), and NEVER introduces a RAG-side database / preference store / recents list / identity token / session token. This slice does NOT introduce: any actual retrieval transport, chunking pipeline, ranking, snippet extraction, embeddings pipeline, vector index, or background watcher (the descriptor registry surfaces the selection UX only; actual retrieval is deferred to a later Phase 10 runtime slice); GitHub integration, packaging / code-signing / auto-update pipelines, system-tray surfaces, or controlled-concurrency runtime; automatic next-phase activation; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, the Phase 10O MCP integration contract, the Phase 10P desktop setup runtime, the Phase 10Q run-profiles runtime, the Phase 10R project-start runtime, the Phase 10S MCP server selection UX contract, the Phase 10T MCP read-only assistance surface, or the Phase 10U MCP action guardrails surface; or any Git automation.

Fix Phase B3 (Desktop Bootstrap Dispatch And Post-Bootstrap Handoff, complete) wires the Fix Phase B2 validated desktop bootstrap form into the shipped `attach_external_target(..., bootstrap=True, ...)` runtime path so the operator can bootstrap a new empty target directly from the desktop app without leaving to run the CLI manually. The new pure `_primary_desktop_dispatch_bootstrap_attach(controller_root, *, target_path, attached_by, approval_mode, bootstrapped_by, human_objective, project_intent, log_path=None) -> Path` wrapper delegates verbatim to the shipped runtime so every Phase 10C / 10D / 10E refusal path (canonical-artifact-write atomicity, rollback on failure, orchestrator.log audit line, controller-vs-target boundary) fires unchanged; the desktop shell adds NO parallel bootstrap logic, NO second desktop state store, and NO hidden control plane. The dispatch does NOT auto-fill any identity or free-text field (per the Fix Phase B1 no-auto-fill identity boundary); every one of the five closed `PRIMARY_DESKTOP_BOOTSTRAP_FIELD_NAMES` values comes verbatim from the operator's typed Entry. The Tk bootstrap dialog's submit callback runs the Fix Phase B2 pure classifier first so obvious form refusals never reach the runtime; on classifier refusal the dialog surfaces the reason and focuses the offending Entry while preserving typed context in every other Entry (dialog does NOT destroy itself). On a passing classifier the callback invokes the dispatch wrapper; on runtime HaltError the dialog surfaces `Bootstrap dispatch refused: <reason>` in the red status Label and keeps typed context intact for retry (no focus_set because runtime refusals are not always field-scoped). On dispatch success the dialog destroys itself, the primary attached-target Label refreshes to show the newly-attached target, and the main status caption surfaces the new pure `_primary_desktop_format_post_bootstrap_success(*, target_path, attach_record_name) -> str` message which explicitly names three shipped anchors so the operator sees that bootstrap is distinct from first-phase activation: the target's loop-state lands at `awaiting_first_activation`, the shipped Phase 4C activator is the only path forward, and the shipped `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` human approval gates that activator. Both helpers refuse fail-closed via `HaltError("halted_input_missing", ...)` on missing / empty / whitespace / non-string inputs. The primary "Select Project Folder" flow's other three UX modes are unchanged: `attach_existing_project` still surfaces the shipped attach CLI as copy-paste-ready guidance (because the primary control column does not collect an operator identity for the attach path); `refused_partial_target` and `refused_malformed_target` still surface an explicit refusal citing the shipped Phase 10C bootstrap-never-overwrites guarantee. This slice does NOT introduce: a second bootstrap runtime; a hidden desktop-only state plane; any auto-fill of the closed 5-field bootstrap vocabulary; any weakening of the shipped `partial_target` / `malformed_target` refusal behavior; any first-phase activation from the desktop UI (the shipped Phase 4C activator + `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` human approval remain the only path); any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; or any Git automation.

Fix Phase B2 (Desktop Bootstrap Form And Validation, complete) is the second slice of the Fix Phase B remediation series: it deepens the shipped Fix Phase B1 desktop bootstrap UX contract with a bounded desktop-side form-and-validation layer so the operator can enter the five closed bootstrap fields (`attached_by`, `approval_mode`, `bootstrapped_by`, `human_objective`, `project_intent`) safely inside the desktop app before any later dispatch slice. The new closed refusal-category vocabulary `PRIMARY_DESKTOP_BOOTSTRAP_REFUSAL_CATEGORIES = ("input_non_dict", "field_missing", "field_wrong_type", "field_empty", "field_embedded_double_quote", "approval_mode_not_in_shipped_enum", "bootstrapped_by_does_not_match_attached_by")` resolves every desktop-side refusal to exactly one closed enum value so the Tk callback branches on a closed classifier return rather than a scattered set of regex-matched reason strings. The new pure classifier `_primary_desktop_classify_bootstrap_form_refusal(fields) -> Optional[dict]` returns `None` on a passing form else a dict `{field, category, reason}` naming the offending field, the closed refusal category, and a human-readable reason string. Check precedence is deterministic and pinned by dedicated regression tests: non-dict shape -> per-field (missing -> wrong-type -> empty -> embedded double quote) -> approval_mode outside the shipped closed `EXTERNAL_TARGET_APPROVAL_MODES` enum -> `bootstrapped_by != attached_by` (whitespace-stripped comparison, matching the shipped Phase 10C single-operator-identity invariant). The existing `_primary_desktop_validate_bootstrap_form(fields) -> None` is refactored as a thin fail-closed wrapper that delegates to the classifier and raises `HaltError("halted_input_missing", result["reason"])` on any non-None classifier return so downstream call sites and their existing tests continue to work verbatim. Two shipped Phase 10B / 10C invariants are hoisted up into the desktop form validator: the closed `EXTERNAL_TARGET_APPROVAL_MODES` enum (`review` / `strict` / `autonomous` / `phase_9_autonomous_prd`) and the `bootstrapped_by == attached_by` single-operator-identity invariant, so the operator sees the refusal at form-validation time instead of after copy-pasting the CLI. Typed operator context is preserved across every refusal: the Tk bootstrap dialog now tracks each `tk.Entry` widget by field name in an `entry_widgets` dict alongside the existing `entry_vars`, calls the pure classifier directly on submit, surfaces `refusal["reason"]` in the status Label, AND calls `entry_widgets[refusal["field"]].focus_set()` when the offending field is field-scoped so the operator's caret lands on the field that needs correction. The dialog does NOT destroy itself on refusal; other Entries retain their text so the operator can correct the one field and re-submit. On a passing form the dialog builds the shipped `attach-external-target --bootstrap ...` CLI via the existing `_primary_desktop_format_bootstrap_cli_guidance(...)` helper (unchanged) and surfaces it in the dedicated Text widget for copy-paste (UX-only; no dispatch, matching the Fix Phase B1 fix cycle boundary). The shipped `attach-external-target --bootstrap ...` CLI remains the sole canonical-mutation path; the desktop shell adds NO parallel dispatch, NO second state store, and NO hidden background control plane. This slice does NOT introduce: any direct desktop-side dispatch to `attach_external_target(...)`, any auto-fill of the closed 5-field bootstrap vocabulary, any weakening of the shipped `partial_target` / `malformed_target` refusal behavior, any first-phase activation from the desktop UI (the shipped Phase 4C activator + `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` human approval remain the only path), any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics, any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts, or any Git automation.

Fix Phase B1 (Desktop Bootstrap UX Contract, complete) is the first slice of the Fix Phase B remediation series that turns the shipped Phase 10C pre-bootstrap target-state classification into an operator-visible desktop UX so selecting a brand-new folder no longer stops at the raw `empty_target` refusal wall. The slice ships a closed 4-mode UX vocabulary `PRIMARY_DESKTOP_FOLDER_UX_MODES = ("attach_existing_project", "bootstrap_new_project", "refused_partial_target", "refused_malformed_target")` mapping each of the four shipped Phase 10C classifications to exactly one deterministic desktop behavior, a closed 5-field bootstrap form vocabulary `PRIMARY_DESKTOP_BOOTSTRAP_FIELD_NAMES = ("attached_by", "approval_mode", "bootstrapped_by", "human_objective", "project_intent")` that the desktop UI MUST NOT widen or auto-fill from OS state / environment variables / hidden defaults, an operator-facing `PRIMARY_DESKTOP_BOOTSTRAP_NEXT_STEP_COPY` explaining that bootstrap is distinct from first-phase activation (a bootstrapped target lands at `awaiting_first_activation`; the shipped Phase 4C activator + `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` human approval remain the only path that advances a bootstrapped target past that state), and two pure UX-guidance CLI formatters (`_primary_desktop_format_attach_cli_guidance(*, target_path, approval_mode) -> str` and `_primary_desktop_format_bootstrap_cli_guidance(*, target_path, approval_mode, attached_by, bootstrapped_by, human_objective, project_intent) -> str`) that surface the shipped `attach-external-target` and `attach-external-target --bootstrap ...` CLIs as copy-paste-ready text with the operator-selected fields filled in (the attach guidance leaves `--attached-by <NAME>` as a placeholder so the operator supplies their own identity; the bootstrap guidance fills every field from the operator-supplied form). Both formatters refuse fail-closed via `HaltError("halted_input_missing", ...)` on missing / empty / whitespace / non-string / embedded-double-quote inputs. The desktop shell does NOT dispatch canonical mutation from the Tk callback; the operator runs the surfaced CLI in their own terminal so the shipped `attach_external_target(...)` runtime remains the sole audit-metadata write path (no auto-fill of `attached_by` from a synthetic desktop identity). Full-target folder selections surface attach CLI guidance in the primary status caption; empty-target selections open a modal Tk `Toplevel` bootstrap dialog with one Entry per closed field and the shipped next-step copy; partial-target and malformed-target selections surface an explicit refusal citing the shipped Phase 10C bootstrap-never-overwrites guarantee.

Fix Phase C3 (PRD Intake UX, active) ships the second runtime slice under the Fix Phase C1 contract. A new `PRD` `tk.LabelFrame` packed directly under the desktop app's control column (visible by default, second in the ordered top-level section vocabulary, NOT hidden behind the Advanced toggle) exposes a `Choose PRD file` button that opens `_filedialog.askopenfilename(...)` plus five plain-English Labels: the selected PRD file path (grey, "No PRD file selected" in the empty state), a bold state Label (`No PRD selected` / `PRD required before running` / `PRD file could not be read` / `PRD ready`), a plain-English summary, a BOUNDED preview Label (`Title: ...\nSummary: <<=200 chars>`) that identifies the PRD without dumping the full body, and a bold next-action Label + plain-English help hint. The new closed constants `FIX_PHASE_C3_SIGNAL_VERSION = "fix-phase-c3-v1"`, `FIX_PHASE_C3_STATE_IDS = ("no_prd_selected", "prd_missing_after_project", "prd_invalid", "prd_ready")`, `FIX_PHASE_C3_STATE_DISPLAY_MAP` (per-state `display_label` + `plain_english_summary` + `next_action_label` + `next_action_help` + `ready_to_run`), `FIX_PHASE_C3_EMPTY_STATE_PAYLOAD`, `FIX_PHASE_C3_REFUSAL_CATEGORIES = ("refused_invalid_prd_path", "refused_invalid_prd_content", "cancelled_prd_picker")`, `FIX_PHASE_C3_ATTRIBUTION = "[prd-intake]"`, and `FIX_PHASE_C3_SUMMARY_PREVIEW_MAX_CHARS = 200` pin the shipped surface verbatim. The new pure Tk-free helpers `_fix_phase_c3_format_no_prd_display_payload()`, `_fix_phase_c3_format_missing_after_project_payload()`, `_fix_phase_c3_format_invalid_payload(*, prd_path, plain_english_reason)`, `_fix_phase_c3_bound_summary_preview(summary)`, `_fix_phase_c3_format_prd_display_payload(*, prd_path)`, and `_fix_phase_c3_format_audit_line(*, state_id, epoch_seconds, refusal_category=None)` route every PRD classification through the shipped Phase 9B `_load_prd_intake_input(...)` + `_validate_prd_intake_common(...)` primitives (NO duplicated PRD parsing in the UI) and refuse fail-closed on missing / non-string / empty / non-file paths, on invalid JSON, on missing required PRD fields, and on unknown refusal / state vocabulary. The Tk callback `_fix_phase_c3_choose_prd_click()` opens the shipped file picker, formats the payload via the pure formatter, renders the six Labels (path, state, summary, preview, next action, help), and emits a `[desktop-first-run-prd]` audit line through the shipped `_log_note(controller_root/.agent-loop/orchestrator.log, ...)` writer. Every operator gesture is auditable: successful PRD pick emits with `state_id="prd_ready"`; content-refusal emits with `state_id="prd_invalid"` + `refusal_category="refused_invalid_prd_content"`; disappeared-path refusal emits with `refusal_category="refused_invalid_prd_path"`; picker cancellation emits with `state_id=None` + `refusal_category="cancelled_prd_picker"` and preserves the current payload verbatim (no re-render, no re-classification, no dispatch). The panel refreshes ONLY on the explicit Choose-PRD gesture; there is NO poll-tick refresh, NO cross-session cache of the picked PRD path or content, NO staging file, NO recent-file list, NO background thread / timer / watcher, and NO silent attach / bootstrap / advance on PRD selection. This slice does NOT introduce: run-mode selection, Start / Stop / Resume controls, run-console behavior, automatic execution, PRD decomposition redesign, a hidden PRD cache or staging plane, a UI-only settings plane, a new library-callable control (the Phase 10I three-control cap is preserved), a new canonical artifact, any auto-fill of the PRD path from OS state, any raw shipped-runtime vocabulary in the default (non-Advanced) surface, or any Git automation.

Fix Phase C2 (Project Folder Picker And Classification Surface, complete) ships the first runtime slice under the Fix Phase C1 contract. The new `Project` LabelFrame packed directly under the desktop app's control column (visible by default, NOT hidden behind the Advanced toggle) exposes a `Choose Folder` button that opens the OS-native folder picker (no typed paths, no auto-fill from OS state) plus four plain-English display Labels: the selected folder path, a bold display label (`Existing project` / `Empty folder` / `Partial setup detected` / `Setup file needs repair` / `No folder selected` in the empty state), a plain-English one-to-two-sentence summary, a bold next-action label, and a plain-English next-action help hint. The new closed constants `FIX_PHASE_C2_SIGNAL_VERSION = "fix-phase-c2-v1"`, `FIX_PHASE_C2_CLASSIFICATION_IDS = ("existing_project", "empty_folder", "partial_target", "malformed_target")`, `FIX_PHASE_C2_UX_MODE_TO_CLASSIFICATION_MAP` (bridge from the shipped Fix Phase B1 `PRIMARY_DESKTOP_FOLDER_UX_MODES` closed vocabulary), `FIX_PHASE_C2_CLASSIFICATION_DISPLAY_MAP` (per-classification `display_label` + `plain_english_summary` + `next_action_label` + `next_action_help` + `ready_to_run`), `FIX_PHASE_C2_EMPTY_STATE_PAYLOAD`, and `FIX_PHASE_C2_REFUSAL_CATEGORIES = ("refused_invalid_folder_path", "refused_unknown_shipped_ux_mode", "refused_missing_plain_english_display_entry")` pin the shipped surface verbatim. The new pure Tk-free helpers `_fix_phase_c2_derive_classification_from_ux_mode(ux_mode) -> str`, `_fix_phase_c2_format_no_folder_display_payload() -> dict`, `_fix_phase_c2_format_folder_display_payload(*, target_path) -> dict`, and `_fix_phase_c2_format_audit_line(*, classification_id, epoch_seconds, refusal_category=None) -> str` route every folder classification through the shipped `_primary_desktop_classify_folder_for_ux(...)` + `_primary_desktop_derive_folder_ux_mode(...)` chain (NO duplicated classification logic in the UI) and refuse fail-closed on missing / non-string / empty / non-directory paths, on unknown shipped UX modes, and on unknown refusal categories. The Tk callback `_fix_phase_c2_choose_folder_click()` opens `_filedialog.askdirectory(...)`, normalises the result via the shipped `_primary_desktop_normalize_selected_folder(...)`, formats the payload via the pure formatter, renders the four plain-English Labels, and emits a `[desktop-first-run-project]` audit line through the shipped `_log_note(controller_root/.agent-loop/orchestrator.log, ...)` writer. The audit line follows the shipped Phase 10AG / Phase 10AI convention (`signal_version` + `classification_id` + `refusal_category` + `epoch_seconds`). The panel refreshes ONLY on the operator's explicit Choose-Folder gesture; there is NO poll-tick refresh, NO cross-session cache of the selected path, NO background thread / timer / watcher, and NO silent bootstrap dispatch. This slice does NOT introduce: PRD selection, run-mode selection, Start / Stop / Resume controls, a run console, a progress console, a UI-only cache of the selected folder, an autofill of the folder path from OS state or environment variables, a new library-callable control (the Phase 10I three-control cap is preserved), a new canonical artifact, any bypass of the shipped Phase 10C `partial_target` / `malformed_target` refusal, any raw runtime vocabulary in the default (non-Advanced) surface, or any Git automation.

Fix Phase C1 (First-Run Setup Contract, complete) defines the guided single-user desktop UX contract for the "pick a folder, load a PRD, choose run behavior, and run" workflow, aimed at one non-technical local operator who dislikes terminals. The new [docs/desktop-first-run-setup-contract.md](docs/desktop-first-run-setup-contract.md) enumerates the closed ordered top-level section vocabulary (`Project`, `PRD`, `Run Mode`, `Run`, `Progress`), the closed plain-English state vocabulary (`setup`, `ready`, `running`, `waiting`, `blocked`, `approval_required`, `complete`), the guided step-by-step workflow (choose folder -> load PRD -> choose run mode -> Start / Stop -> monitor progress; every step gated on an explicit operator gesture, no auto-advance), the advanced-detail hiding rules (raw shipped runtime vocabulary is hidden by default and only reachable through a single shipped "Advanced" toggle matching the shipped Phase 10AG / Phase 10AI convention), the closed nine-category refusal vocabulary, the preserved approval gates (Phase 5A/5C/5D approval-mode, Phase 4C activator + `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION`, Phase 9G human acceptance, Phase 10U MCP per-tool approval, Phase 5F post-review prompt bootstrap), the audit expectations (`.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` receives the per-gesture audit line via the shipped Phase 10AG / Phase 10AI audit-line convention; no parallel first-run-history file), the source-of-truth preservation rule (no UI-only JSON / SQLite / MessagePack store; no session state file shadowing `loop-state.json`; no project-selection cache; no PRD staging area; no run-mode preference file; no browser-session identity cache; no recents list; no autofill from OS state; no background watcher beyond the shipped Phase 10L / 10M cadence rules), and the explicit deferral of the folder picker / PRD intake / run-mode selector / Start-Stop control / plain-English run console / review-and-approval surface / completion-and-handoff summary to future runtime slices (Fix Phase C2 through C8). No first-run setup runtime, folder picker, PRD intake surface, run-mode selector, run console, progress console, second controller, background watcher, or UI-only state plane ships in this slice; implementation is deferred to Fix Phase C2 through C8.

Phase 10AI (Orchestration Graph And Performance View Initial Slice, complete) materialises the shipped Phase 10AH contract into the first bounded desktop orchestration-visualization runtime. The new closed constants `PHASE_10AI_VISUALIZATION_SIGNAL_VERSION = "phase-10ai-v1"`, `PHASE_10AI_CANONICAL_MIRROR_KEYS` (the ten canonical-mirror keys `phase`, `sub_phase`, `task`, `loop_state_status`, `approval_mode`, `cycle_count`, `max_cycles`, `awaiting_human_for`, `last_verdict`, `last_verdict_phase`), `PHASE_10AI_ADVISORY_DERIVED_KEYS` (the five advisory-derived keys `review_branch_active`, `fix_branch_active`, `human_gate_pending`, `blocked_or_halted`, `artifact_backed_progress`), `PHASE_10AI_VISUALIZATION_VOCABULARY` (their concatenation, fifteen total), `PHASE_10AI_SOURCE_CATEGORIES = ("canonical_mirror", "visualization_advisory")`, `PHASE_10AI_STATUS_CATEGORIES = ("in_progress", "awaiting_review", "awaiting_human", "halted", "complete")`, `PHASE_10AI_REFUSAL_CATEGORIES` (the nine-category refusal set), `PHASE_10AI_KEY_SOURCE_CATEGORY_MAP`, and `PHASE_10AI_KEY_SOURCE_ARTIFACTS_MAP` pin the closed shipped vocabulary verbatim. The new pure `_desktop_orchestration_visualization_derive_advisory_state(*, loop_state, overlap_state, fix_prompt_mtime, claude_summary_mtime, artifact_backed_progress) -> dict` computes every advisory-derived key from the shipped canonical mirrors (`review_branch_active` from `PHASE_10AI_NORMAL_CYCLE_STATUSES` or `last_verdict == "APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW"`; `fix_branch_active` from `PHASE_10AI_FIX_CYCLE_STATUSES` or `fix-prompt.md.mtime > claude-summary.md.mtime`; `human_gate_pending` from `awaiting_human_for` non-empty or `STRICT_GATE_HALT_STATUSES`; `blocked_or_halted` from `status.startswith("halted_")` or overlap aggregate `refused_pending_recovery`; `artifact_backed_progress` passed through verbatim). The new pure `_desktop_orchestration_visualization_classify_status_category(*, loop_state, overlap_state) -> str` returns one of the closed five-category status-icon values with deterministic precedence (`halted` > `complete` > `awaiting_human` > `awaiting_review` > `in_progress`). The new pure `build_desktop_orchestration_visualization_view(controller_root) -> dict` assembles a bounded view dict shaped exactly to the closed vocabulary (fifteen nodes, each with `id`, `source_category`, `source_artifacts`, `current_value`, `attribution_tag`) plus the per-tick classified `status_category` and its `status_category_source_mirror`. The new pure `render_desktop_orchestration_visualization_text(view) -> list` emits one header line plus one line per node, every body line carrying either the `[canonical mirror]` tag (ten canonical-mirror keys) or the `[visualization-advisory]` tag (five advisory-derived keys) matching the shipped Phase 10AH attribution convention verbatim. The new pure `_desktop_orchestration_visualization_format_audit_line(*, epoch_seconds, refusal_category=None) -> str` reuses the shipped Phase 10AG audit-line convention (`[desktop-orchestration-visualization] signal_version=... refusal_category=... epoch_seconds=...`). The Tk panel wired under the Advanced toggle in `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)` exposes an `Orchestration View (Phase 10AI)` heading, a status-category label naming the classified status + its `.agent-loop/loop-state.json` source, and a read-only `tk.Text` widget rendering the fifteen-line attribution-tagged body. The refresh is invoked BOTH from `_refresh_orchestration_visualization()` at panel creation and from the main `_refresh(...)` poll callback so the shipped Phase 10L / 10M poll cadence remains the sole refresh clock. Every per-tick refresh emits the shipped `[desktop-orchestration-visualization]` audit line through the shipped `_log_note(controller_root/.agent-loop/orchestrator.log, ...)` writer. This slice does NOT introduce: a hidden UI-only orchestration graph store / progress cache / layout cache / animation state store; a background thread, separate timer, or watcher beyond the shipped poll cadence; automatic phase progression, verdict authorship, or downstream action dispatch from the visualization panel; a graph-side canonical write path (the panel is READ-ONLY); a new library-callable control (the Phase 10I three-control cap is preserved); a new canonical artifact; any auto-fill of operator identity; any weakening of the shipped Phase 5A/5C/5D approval-mode gates, the shipped Phase 10AC overlap-safe detection aggregate, the shipped Phase 10AB `_DESKTOP_CONCURRENCY_OWNERSHIP_MAP`, the shipped Phase 10AG `DESKTOP_CODEX_CONVERSATION_INTENT_IDS` vocabulary, or the shipped `attach_external_target(...)` boundary; a graph-rendering library dependency (the initial slice renders the shipped vocabulary as attribution-tagged text lines; a richer graph / icon renderer is a future slice concern per the Phase 10AH contract's `## Out Of Scope` section).

Phase 10AH (Orchestration Graph And Phase-State Visualization Contract, complete) defines the bounded contract for a future desktop visualization surface that should show the live orchestration state as a graph or icon-based flow.
