Phase 10AG (Desktop Codex Conversation Surface Initial Slice, complete) materialises the shipped Phase 10AF contract into the first bounded desktop runtime. The new pure classifier `_desktop_codex_conversation_classify_request(*, intent_id, operator_identity, message_body, in_flight, overlap_state, strict_mode_gate_pending) -> Optional[dict]` returns `None` on a passing request or a `{intent_id, refusal_category, reason}` dict for refusals, enforcing the shipped Phase 10AF closed refusal vocabulary in this order: unknown-intent -> missing operator identity -> missing message body -> shipped overlap-safe aggregate `refused_pending_recovery` -> shipped Phase 5C strict-mode gate pending -> serial-invocation in-flight flag. The new pure `_desktop_codex_conversation_format_request(*, intent_id, operator_identity, message_body) -> dict` shapes the operator-composed request payload with `signal_version = "phase-10ag-v1"`, `intent_id`, whitespace-stripped `operator_identity` + `message_body`, the closed per-intent `target_artifact` (looked up from `DESKTOP_CODEX_CONVERSATION_INTENT_TARGET_MAP`), and the `[codex-conversation-advisory]` attribution tag. The new pure `_desktop_codex_conversation_read_canonical_response_mirror(controller_root, intent_id) -> dict` returns the READ-ONLY canonical mirror of the per-intent primary target artifact with a `[canonical mirror]` attribution tag, or a `present=False` + explanatory `error` payload when the file is missing or unreadable (NEVER raises; NEVER writes). The new pure `_desktop_codex_conversation_build_dispatch_command(*, intent_id, controller_root) -> list` returns `[sys.executable, str(<agent_loop.py>), "resume"]` for `request_codex_review`; every other closed-vocabulary intent is recognised but refuses fail-closed with an explicit deferred-to-later-slice HaltError. The new pure `_desktop_codex_conversation_format_audit_line(*, intent_id, operator_identity, refusal_category=None, epoch_seconds) -> str` formats the Phase 3A orchestrator.log audit line so the Tk callback appends through the shipped audit writer (NEVER writes a parallel audit file). The Tk panel wired under the Advanced toggle in `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)` exposes a `Codex Conversation (Phase 10AG)` heading, an intent `_ttk.Combobox` (six closed values), an operator-identity `tk.Entry` (no auto-fill), an advisory message-body `tk.Text` (in-memory only; NEVER persisted; NEVER carried across sessions), a Send button that runs the classifier -> formatter -> dispatch-command builder chain and spawns `subprocess.Popen(cmd, cwd=controller_root)` for the review intent, a red status Label surfacing classifier / dispatch refusals with the operator's typed context intact, and a read-only canonical mirror `tk.Text` refreshing on every poll tick with the `[canonical mirror]` attribution and the per-intent target path. This slice does NOT introduce: a hidden UI-only request queue, a reply cache, a session state plane, a background chat driver, a networked Codex server / WebSocket / SSE stream / MCP-side "codex chat" endpoint, a second orchestrator, any auto-fill of the operator identity, any weakening of the shipped Phase 5A/5C/5D approval-mode gates, the shipped Phase 10AC overlap-safe detection aggregate, the shipped Phase 10AB `_DESKTOP_CONCURRENCY_OWNERSHIP_MAP`, or the shipped `attach_external_target(...)` boundary; and it does NOT ship a dispatch path for the other five closed-vocabulary intents (deferred to a later slice per the Phase 10AF contract).

Phase 10AF (Desktop Codex Conversation And Intervention Contract, complete) is the contract-definition slice that pins how a future desktop panel MUST let the operator route an operator-to-Codex request from inside the shipped desktop app without inventing a competing control plane, a hidden UI-only request queue, or a UI-only reply cache. The new [docs/desktop-codex-conversation-contract.md](docs/desktop-codex-conversation-contract.md) enumerates a CLOSED six-intent operator vocabulary (`request_codex_review`, `request_codex_issue_classification`, `request_codex_roadmap_update`, `request_codex_targeted_repo_change`, `request_codex_claude_prompt_authorship`, `request_codex_fix_prompt_authorship`), a CLOSED per-intent routing map back to the shipped canonical artifacts (`codex-review.md`, `claude-prompt.md`, `fix-prompt.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `TASK.md`, `.agent-loop/phase-plan.md`, `.agent-loop/current-phase.md`, `.agent-loop/current-task.md`, `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`), the advisory-vs-canonical mirror rule (in-progress operator drafts are held ONLY in-memory in the per-session Tk surface and NEVER persisted; canonical responses are surfaced as READ-ONLY mirrors of the on-disk file with explicit `[canonical mirror]` / `[codex-conversation-advisory]` attribution tags matching the shipped Phase 10K / 10L convention), the Claude-owned-vs-Codex-owned follow-up rule (Codex-owned target -> Codex writes through the shipped adapter; Claude-owned target -> Codex authors a `claude-prompt.md` / `fix-prompt.md` naming the follow-up and Claude picks it up next cycle; orchestrator-owned target -> refuse fail-closed), a CLOSED eight-category refusal vocabulary (`refused_intent_outside_closed_vocabulary`, `refused_orchestrator_owned_target`, `refused_claude_owned_target`, `refused_overlap_unsafe`, `refused_strict_mode_gate`, `refused_auto_fill_operator_identity`, `refused_in_flight_codex_invocation`, `refused_advisory_persistence`), the preserved approval gates (Phase 5A/5C/5D approval-mode, Phase 4C activator + `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION`, Phase 9G human acceptance, Phase 10U MCP per-tool approval, Phase 5F post-review prompt bootstrap), the audit expectations (`.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` receives the audit line per Phase 3A; the shipped canonical artifacts receive the request/response through the shipped adapter; NO parallel conversation-history file, per-session log, or UI-only event stream is introduced), and the explicit non-widening boundaries (no UI-only JSON / SQLite / MessagePack store; no session state file shadowing `loop-state.json`; no drafts persisted to disk; no pending-responses queue file; no browser-session identity cache; no recents list; no autofill from OS state; no background watcher beyond the shipped Phase 10L / 10M cadence rules; no new library-callable control beyond the shipped Phase 10I three-control cap; no framework-side integration beyond the shipped Phase 10AE evaluation surface). No desktop conversation runtime, chat/intervention panel, request queue, reply cache, or UI-only state plane ships in this slice; implementation is deferred to Phase 10AG.

Phase 10AE (Framework Evaluation Beyond The Native Loop, complete) ships a bounded EVALUATION-ONLY surface that compares the shipped native Codex/Claude loop against three named delegated-role frameworks (`crewai`, `langgraph`, `langchain`) across a closed set of criteria, without swapping any framework runtime into the shipped native loop. The new `build_desktop_framework_evaluation_view(controller_root: Path, *, operator_inputs: dict | None = None) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator (HaltError is soft-failed per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule) and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10ae-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, canonical mirrors of `current_loop_state_status` / `controller_loop_state_approval_mode` / `current_loop_state_phase` / `current_loop_state_sub_phase` / `current_loop_state_cycle_count`, `phase_10ae_runtime_available=False` (this slice is EVALUATION-ONLY; no framework runtime is imported / dispatched, and any attempted framework-runtime dispatch through the shipped `evaluate_framework_runtime_availability(repo_root, framework_id)` helper is refused fail-closed via `HaltError(HALTED_FRAMEWORK_EVALUATION_UNSAFE, ...)` for any framework_id other than `native_loop`), closed enumeration mirrors of `framework_ids` (`native_loop`, `crewai`, `langgraph`, `langchain`), `criterion_categories` (`shipped_boundary_preservation`, `native_loop_strength`, `framework_leverage_opportunity`), `verdicts` (`preserves_shipped_boundary`, `could_help_bounded`, `would_conflict_with_shipped_boundary`, `native_loop_only`, `not_applicable`), and `native_loop_statuses` (`shipped_boundary_preserved`, `native_loop_only`, `framework_leverage_opportunity_bounded`), an `operator_inputs` per-poll mirror, a `criteria` list assembled from the closed `_DESKTOP_FRAMEWORK_EVALUATION_REGISTRY` (ten shipped criteria: `codex_claude_ownership_boundary_preservation`, `approval_mode_gating_preservation`, `evidence_review_pipeline_preservation`, `canonical_artifact_first_source_of_truth`, `overlap_safe_detection_gate_preservation`, `desktop_ui_read_only_reporter_preservation`, `diff_and_evidence_first_review`, `structured_state_graph_orchestration`, `delegated_role_worker_coordination`, `prompt_and_tool_orchestration_leverage`), a `verdict_summary_counts` per-framework tally (`{framework_id: {verdict: count, ...}, ...}` so a reviewer can see at a glance which framework preserves the most shipped boundaries and which conflicts most often), and the literal `DESKTOP_FRAMEWORK_EVALUATION_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. The pure `_desktop_framework_evaluation_validate_descriptor(...)` validator refuses fail-closed via HaltError on any missing required field, wrong-typed value, unknown `criterion_category`, unknown `native_loop_status`, non-tuple `framework_verdicts`, `framework_verdicts` that omits or duplicates a shipped framework_id, a `framework_id` outside the closed enumeration, a `verdict` outside the closed enumeration, or a missing / empty `reason` string. Every registry criterion advertises the native-loop verdict as `preserves_shipped_boundary` because the shipped surface's entire point is to preserve the shipped boundary while comparing frameworks (a dedicated regression test anchors that invariant), and every criterion covers all four framework_ids (a dedicated regression test asserts the coverage). Operator inputs flow through `_desktop_framework_evaluation_normalize_operator_inputs(...)` which accepts `{identity, acknowledged_criterion_ids}` (a non-dict / wrong-typed value is refused fail-closed via HaltError); per the Phase 10S source-of-truth preservation rule these inputs are held in-memory only, NEVER persisted to disk, NEVER auto-filled from any OS / packaging / browser-session source, NEVER carried across sessions. The new `cmd_view_desktop_framework_evaluation(args)` CLI exposes two optional per-session operator-input flags (`--operator-identity <NAME>`, `--acknowledge-criterion <ID>` repeatable) that thread the inputs through to the view builder explicitly per invocation. The view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `framework_evaluation_view` sub-view alongside the existing seventeen sub-views, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders a `Framework Evaluation (Phase 10AE)` section that reuses `render_desktop_framework_evaluation_text(...)` verbatim - the per-criterion lines surface each criterion's `description`, `safety_copy`, `criterion_category`, `native_loop_status`, per-framework `verdict` + `reason`, `deferred_runtime_marker`, and `operator_acknowledged` on their own indented lines with explicit `[canonical mirror]` / `[advisory]` / `[framework-evaluation]` / `[framework-criterion]` / `[framework-verdict]` / `[framework-summary]` / `[deferred-runtime]` / `[refused]` tags so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10AE slice wires a fourteenth Tkinter panel beneath the existing Phase 10Q + Phase 10R + Phase 10T + Phase 10U + Phase 10V + Phase 10W + Phase 10X + Phase 10Y + Phase 10Z + Phase 10AA + Phase 10AB + Phase 10AC + Phase 10AD control panels inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: one real `tk.Button` per shipped criterion rendered via the new `build_desktop_framework_evaluation_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function that COPY-PASTES an operator-visible criterion acknowledgement TEMPLATE (not a shipped subcommand) to clipboard. Every descriptor carries `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` and `category='framework_evaluation_ux'`; ZERO new library-callable controls are introduced and the Phase 10I three-control library-callable cap is preserved exactly (a dedicated regression test asserts the Phase 10I registry still ships exactly `view-external-status` / `view-external-controls` / `inspect-external-target`). Matching the Phase 10Z / 10AA / 10AB / 10AC / 10AD fix-cycle affordance pattern, each button surfaces `enabled=True` so the shipped GUI can actually expose the copy path (the click ONLY copies the criterion acknowledgement TEMPLATE into the OS clipboard, never invokes a shipped subcommand and never mutates a hidden framework-evaluation cache), while `runtime_enabled` reflects whether a shipped framework runtime would allow the criterion to dispatch (always False in this slice because no framework runtime is shipped). The new `view-desktop-framework-evaluation` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_framework_evaluation(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds; `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow and omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-framework-evaluation] REFUSED: ...` stderr line. Per the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 3A orchestrator contract, the Phase 4 planner / activator separation, the Phase 5A/5C/5D approval-mode contract, the Phase 2A/2B evidence-review pipeline, the Phase 6M runtime-adapter contract, and the Phase 10Z / 10AA / 10AB / 10AC / 10AD copy-only affordance pattern this handler is FRAMEWORK-EVALUATION-CONTRACT ONLY: it NEVER mutates any canonical artifact, NEVER writes a framework-evaluation cache on disk, NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, NEVER advances loop-state, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawns a subprocess, NEVER opens a network socket, NEVER launches or coordinates any delegated-worker runtime (a dedicated regression test patches `threading.Thread`, `multiprocessing.Process`, and `multiprocessing.Pool` and asserts zero calls during a full view build), NEVER imports crewai / langgraph / langchain at surface build time (a dedicated regression test snapshots `sys.modules` before / after a view build and asserts no `crewai` / `langgraph` / `langchain` prefix was added), NEVER schedules a background watcher, and NEVER introduces a framework-side database / preference store / recents list / identity token / session token. Phase 10AE ships a BOUNDED runtime-availability helper that turns the enumerated framework-id set into a real refusal path: `evaluate_framework_runtime_availability(repo_root: Path, framework_id: str) -> dict` returns `{framework_id, runtime_available: True, verdict_summary_counts, notes}` only when `framework_id == "native_loop"`; every other shipped framework_id (`crewai`, `langgraph`, `langchain`) raises `HaltError(HALTED_FRAMEWORK_EVALUATION_UNSAFE, ...)` because no framework runtime is shipped in this slice, and any unknown framework_id raises the same halt so callers cannot silently import / dispatch an unshipped framework runtime. This slice does NOT introduce: any full framework migration, silent runtime swap, hidden background orchestration, or delegated-worker runtime added under the banner of "evaluation"; any framework-backed path that bypasses the shipped ownership, approval, review, overlap-safety, or canonical-artifact boundaries; any automatic next-phase activation behavior that bypasses or rewrites the shipped Phase 4 planner / activation separation; any claim that fully autonomous PRD-to-product execution is already solved; any packaging work, hidden orchestration, or live delegated execution added under the banner of this evaluation slice; any rewrite of current shipped behavior just to make future autonomy work easier; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, the Phase 10O MCP integration contract, the Phase 10P desktop setup runtime, the Phase 10Q run-profiles runtime, the Phase 10R project-start runtime, the Phase 10S MCP server selection UX contract, the Phase 10T MCP read-only assistance surface, the Phase 10U MCP action guardrails surface, the Phase 10V RAG source selection contract, the Phase 10W RAG local index and retrieval controls, the Phase 10X autonomous run console, the Phase 10Y capacity recovery console, the Phase 10Z selection UX contract, the Phase 10AA memory-vault export contract, the Phase 10AB controlled-concurrency contract, the Phase 10AC overlap-safe detection surface, or the Phase 10AD Codex-owned concurrent-work surface; or any Git automation. The Phase 10AE auditability refinement adds two required fields to the shipped descriptor shape so every surfaced judgment is auditable from a canonical/advisory shipped artifact rather than hidden in code comments alone: a per-criterion `docs_anchor: str` (single repo-relative citation naming the shipped docs / contract that pins the boundary being evaluated) and a per-verdict `evidence_anchors: tuple[str, ...]` (non-empty tuple of repo-relative citations backing that specific verdict - the shipped code symbol the native_loop verdict cites, or the shipped docs / contract path the framework verdict would conflict with). The `_desktop_framework_evaluation_validate_descriptor(...)` validator refuses fail-closed on any missing / empty / non-string / non-tuple anchor, and a dedicated regression test asserts every shipped registry entry populates both fields. `render_desktop_framework_evaluation_text(...)` surfaces the new fields under two new attribution tags (`[framework-anchor]` per criterion, `[framework-evidence]` per verdict) so the CLI reporter and the desktop-panel text output both show the concrete navigation targets rather than hiding the judgment in code comments alone. The refinement does NOT change the closed vocabularies (`FRAMEWORK_EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK_IDS`, `FRAMEWORK_EVALUATION_CRITERION_CATEGORIES`, `FRAMEWORK_EVALUATION_VERDICTS`, `FRAMEWORK_EVALUATION_NATIVE_LOOP_STATUSES`), the runtime-availability helper's `HaltError(HALTED_FRAMEWORK_EVALUATION_UNSAFE, ...)` refusal, the `phase_10ae_runtime_available=False` boundary, or the Phase 10I three-control library-callable cap.

Phase 10AD (Codex-Owned Concurrent Work Initial Slice, complete) turns the shipped Phase 10AB Controlled Concurrent Operation Contract ownership map and the shipped Phase 10AC overlap-safe detection aggregate into a bounded enumeration of Codex-owned actions that may run concurrently with an in-flight Claude implementation cycle WITHOUT invalidating Claude's active task context, plus a shipped bounded eligibility runtime helper that gates every such action fail-closed AND a shipped bounded read runtime path that actually performs one real Codex-owned read concurrently with Claude implementation. The new `build_desktop_codex_concurrent_work_view(controller_root: Path, *, operator_inputs: dict | None = None) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator (HaltError is soft-failed per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule) and to the shipped `build_desktop_overlap_detection_view(...)` (HaltError is soft-failed to `overlap_overall_state=None`; the shipped Phase 10AD fix cycle strengthens the per-action evaluator so a `None` overlap_overall_state -- OR any state that is not one of the two known-clean states `no_signal` / `signal_detected` -- refuses fail-closed via `refused_overlap_unsafe`, so a structural HaltError from the shipped Phase 10AC view builder can NEVER let a Phase 10AD entry advance into `eligible_bounded_execution`), and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10ad-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, canonical mirrors of `current_loop_state_status` / `controller_loop_state_approval_mode` / `current_loop_state_phase` / `current_loop_state_sub_phase` / `current_loop_state_cycle_count`, `phase_10ad_runtime_available=True` (the shipped Phase 10AD slice ships a real bounded eligibility runtime helper -- `evaluate_codex_concurrent_work_eligibility(repo_root, action_id)` returns the closed eligibility outcome for a shipped Codex-owned action or raises `HaltError(HALTED_CODEX_CONCURRENT_WORK_UNSAFE, ...)` when the action_id is not in the closed registry OR when the outcome is anything other than `eligible_bounded_execution` -- AND, per the shipped Phase 10AD fix cycle, a real bounded read runtime path `perform_bounded_codex_concurrent_read(repo_root: Path, action_id: str, log_path: Path | None = None) -> dict | None` that is invoked from `_run_normal_cycle_from_increment(...)` at status=`claude_implementing` (right after loop-state records that Claude is implementing and BEFORE the Claude adapter boundary fires) for the shipped `PHASE_10AD_SHIPPED_CONCURRENT_READ_ACTION_ID = "codex_prd_intake_read"` read-only advisory action, so the loop actually executes one bounded Codex-owned read of the shipped Codex-owned canonical `TASK.md` concurrently with the in-flight Claude implementation cycle; the shipped read routes through `evaluate_codex_concurrent_work_eligibility(...)` FIRST so any refusal skips the read best-effort (never halts Claude implementation) and every invocation appends a best-effort audit line (`[phase-10ad] bounded Codex-owned concurrent read executed / refused / skipped ...`) to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` so the reviewer can verify the shipped concurrent read actually ran; the narrower deferrals -- actual concurrent Codex/Claude WORKER / SUBPROCESS execution, hidden background watchers, and network widening -- remain tracked in `ROADMAP.md`), closed enumeration mirrors of `action_types` / `effect_classes` / `eligibility_states` / `ownership_roles`, an `operator_inputs` per-poll mirror, an `overlap_overall_state` per-poll mirror sourced verbatim from the shipped Phase 10AC aggregate, an `actions` list assembled from the closed `_DESKTOP_CODEX_CONCURRENT_WORK_REGISTRY` (five shipped actions covering `codex_prd_intake_read`, `codex_plan_proposal_write`, `codex_artifact_dashboard_read`, `codex_memory_vault_read`, and `codex_current_task_write`), derived `eligible_action_ids` / `refused_action_ids` lists, and the literal `DESKTOP_CODEX_CONCURRENT_WORK_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. Every action descriptor's `target_artifact_canonical_rel` MUST resolve to a shipped canonical artifact in the Phase 10AB `_DESKTOP_CONCURRENCY_OWNERSHIP_MAP` and the descriptor's `expected_target_owner_role` MUST match the shipped ownership entry verbatim (a dedicated regression test asserts every registry entry passes both guards). The pure `_desktop_codex_concurrent_work_validate_descriptor(...)` validator refuses fail-closed via HaltError on any missing required field, wrong-typed value, unknown `action_type`, unknown `effect_class`, unknown `expected_target_owner_role` (must be a Phase 10AB `CONCURRENCY_OWNERSHIP_ROLES` member), or non-POSIX / absolute / drive-prefixed / parent-traversal `target_artifact_canonical_rel`. Per-action eligibility is derived per-poll via the pure `_desktop_codex_concurrent_work_evaluate_eligibility(...)` helper that walks a closed evaluation order: (1) if the shipped Phase 10AC aggregate is `refused_pending_recovery` the outcome short-circuits to `refused_overlap_unsafe`; (2) else if the target is not in the shipped Phase 10AB ownership map OR its owner_role does not match the descriptor's `expected_target_owner_role` the outcome is `refused_owner_role_violation`; (3) else if the descriptor's `would_invalidate_claude_context` is True the outcome is `refused_would_invalidate_claude_context`; (4) else if the shipped Phase 10AC aggregate is anything OTHER than one of the two known-clean states (`no_signal` / `signal_detected`) -- which covers `unknown`, `None` for a structural HaltError soft-fail from the Phase 10AC view builder, and any unrecognized state -- the outcome falls closed to `refused_overlap_unsafe` (Phase 10AD fix cycle: the previous evaluator refused only on the literal `unknown` string and let `None` fall through to eligibility; the fix-cycle predicate is fail-closed default so no structural Phase 10AC refusal can silently unlock a Phase 10AD entry); (5) otherwise the outcome is `eligible_bounded_execution`. The always-refused `codex_current_task_write` entry anchors the `refused_would_invalidate_claude_context` branch: current-task.md is Codex-owned and Claude reads it every cycle, so writing it concurrently WOULD invalidate the active Claude context and the shipped surface refuses fail-closed regardless of operator input (a dedicated regression test supplies every operator-side input and still asserts refusal). Operator inputs flow through `_desktop_codex_concurrent_work_normalize_operator_inputs(...)` which accepts `{identity, acknowledged_action_ids}` (a non-dict / wrong-typed value is refused fail-closed via HaltError); per the Phase 10S source-of-truth preservation rule these inputs are held in-memory only, NEVER persisted to disk, NEVER auto-filled from any OS / packaging / browser-session source, NEVER carried across sessions. The new `cmd_view_desktop_codex_concurrent_work(args)` CLI exposes two optional per-session operator-input flags (`--operator-identity <NAME>`, `--acknowledge-action <ID>` repeatable) that thread the inputs through to the view builder explicitly per invocation. The view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `codex_concurrent_work_view` sub-view alongside the existing sixteen sub-views, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders a `Codex-Owned Concurrent Work (Phase 10AD)` section that reuses `render_desktop_codex_concurrent_work_text(...)` verbatim - the per-action lines surface each action's `description`, `safety_copy`, `action_type`, `effect_class`, `target_artifact_canonical_rel`, `expected_target_owner_role` / `actual_target_owner_role`, `would_invalidate_claude_context`, derived `eligibility_state` / `eligibility_reason`, `overlap_overall_state`, `operator_acknowledged`, `deferred_runtime_marker`, and (when refused) `refusal_reason_template` on their own indented lines with explicit `[canonical mirror]` / `[advisory]` / `[codex-concurrent]` / `[codex-owner]` / `[codex-effect]` / `[codex-eligibility]` / `[codex-overlap]` / `[deferred-runtime]` / `[refused]` tags so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10AD slice wires a thirteenth Tkinter panel beneath the existing Phase 10Q + Phase 10R + Phase 10T + Phase 10U + Phase 10V + Phase 10W + Phase 10X + Phase 10Y + Phase 10Z + Phase 10AA + Phase 10AB + Phase 10AC control panels inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: one real `tk.Button` per shipped action rendered via the new `build_desktop_codex_concurrent_work_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function that COPY-PASTES an operator-visible eligibility acknowledgement TEMPLATE (not a shipped subcommand) to clipboard. Every descriptor carries `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` and `category='codex_concurrent_work_ux'`; ZERO new library-callable controls are introduced and the Phase 10I three-control library-callable cap is preserved exactly (a dedicated regression test asserts the Phase 10I registry still ships exactly `view-external-status` / `view-external-controls` / `inspect-external-target`). Matching the Phase 10Z / 10AA / 10AB / 10AC fix-cycle affordance pattern, each button surfaces `enabled=True` so the shipped GUI can actually expose the copy path (the click ONLY copies the eligibility acknowledgement TEMPLATE into the OS clipboard, never invokes a shipped subcommand and never mutates a hidden concurrent-work cache), while `runtime_enabled` reflects whether the shipped bounded eligibility runtime would allow the action (True only when `eligibility_state == "eligible_bounded_execution"`). The new `view-desktop-codex-concurrent-work` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_codex_concurrent_work(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds; `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow and omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-codex-concurrent-work] REFUSED: ...` stderr line. Per the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 3A orchestrator contract, and the Phase 4 planner / activator separation the `cmd_view_desktop_codex_concurrent_work(...)` reporter handler itself remains a Phase 7C read-only reporter (the shipped runtime eligibility helper is a separate orchestrator-owned surface): it NEVER mutates any canonical artifact, NEVER writes a concurrent-work cache on disk, NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, NEVER advances loop-state, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawns a subprocess, NEVER opens a network socket, NEVER launches or coordinates any actual concurrent Codex/Claude worker (a dedicated regression test patches `threading.Thread`, `multiprocessing.Process`, and `multiprocessing.Pool` and asserts zero calls during a full view build AND during a runtime-helper invocation), NEVER reads canonical artifact BODY content, NEVER schedules a background watcher, and NEVER introduces a concurrent-work-side database / preference store / recents list / identity token / session token. Phase 10AD ships a BOUNDED runtime eligibility helper that turns the enumerated safe set into a real refusal path: `evaluate_codex_concurrent_work_eligibility(repo_root: Path, action_id: str) -> dict` delegates verbatim to `build_desktop_codex_concurrent_work_view(...)` and raises `HaltError(HALTED_CODEX_CONCURRENT_WORK_UNSAFE, ...)` when `action_id` is not in the closed registry (so callers cannot silently execute an unrecognized concurrent action) OR when the outcome is anything other than `eligible_bounded_execution` (so callers cannot silently execute a refused action). The helper is bounded per the Phase 10AD contract exactly like the reporter path: it consults the same registry the desktop surface exposes; it does NOT spawn a subprocess, open a network socket, launch or coordinate any actual concurrent Codex/Claude worker, schedule a background watcher, widen the Phase 10I library-callable cap, or advance loop-state past the caller-owned refusal write. Layered on top of the eligibility helper, the shipped Phase 10AD fix cycle ships a real bounded READ runtime path `perform_bounded_codex_concurrent_read(repo_root: Path, action_id: str, log_path: Path | None = None) -> dict | None` that routes eligibility through the shipped helper FIRST (returning `None` on refusal so a routine advisory-read refusal never halts the calling cycle) and, on `eligible_bounded_execution`, refuses fail-closed via `HaltError(HALTED_CODEX_CONCURRENT_WORK_UNSAFE, ...)` when the descriptor's `effect_class` is anything other than `read_only_advisory` (widening guard: the shipped bounded read runtime path never performs a `codex_owned_write` / `codex_owned_write_invalidates_claude` effect), reads the target artifact BYTES exactly once, and returns a bounded observation envelope `{action_id, target_artifact_canonical_rel, bytes_read, modified_utc, eligibility_state, eligibility_reason}`. The wired call site is `_run_normal_cycle_from_increment(...)` at status=`claude_implementing` for the shipped `codex_prd_intake_read` action, so the loop actually executes one bounded Codex-owned read of `TASK.md` concurrently with each Claude implementation cycle; each invocation appends a best-effort audit line to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` (executed / refused / skipped) so the reviewer can verify the shipped read was invoked without inspecting the process trace. A dedicated regression test bank patches `perform_bounded_codex_concurrent_read(...)` and asserts `_run_normal_cycle_from_increment(...)` calls it exactly once with the shipped constant and the orchestrator log path; a companion test forces the helper to raise `HaltError(HALTED_CODEX_CONCURRENT_WORK_UNSAFE, ...)` and asserts the cycle continues (audits the widening-guard refusal to the log rather than halting) so a routine widening-guard refusal cannot silently halt the Claude implementation cycle. Focused regression coverage anchors the fail-closed `None` overlap branch end-to-end: with `build_desktop_overlap_detection_view(...)` mocked to raise HaltError, the view builder soft-fails `overlap_overall_state=None`, EVERY registry entry surfaces `refused_overlap_unsafe`, and no entry falls through to `eligible_bounded_execution`. This slice does NOT introduce: any broad or general-purpose overlapping Codex/Claude runtime, silent background orchestration, watcher farm, or parallel worker model; any Codex-owned concurrent work that mutates Claude-owned implementation artifacts, prompt/summarization artifacts, or any artifact whose mutation would invalidate Claude's active task context; any bypass of the shipped Phase 10AC refusal gate or the Phase 10AB ownership contract; any automatic next-phase activation behavior; any claim that fully autonomous PRD-to-product execution is already solved; any packaging work, hidden orchestration, or live concurrency added under the banner of this bounded concurrent-work slice; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, the Phase 10O MCP integration contract, the Phase 10P desktop setup runtime, the Phase 10Q run-profiles runtime, the Phase 10R project-start runtime, the Phase 10S MCP server selection UX contract, the Phase 10T MCP read-only assistance surface, the Phase 10U MCP action guardrails surface, the Phase 10V RAG source selection contract, the Phase 10W RAG local index and retrieval controls, the Phase 10X autonomous run console, the Phase 10Y capacity recovery console, the Phase 10Z selection UX contract, the Phase 10AA memory-vault export contract, the Phase 10AB controlled-concurrency contract, or the Phase 10AC overlap-safe detection surface; or any Git automation.

Phase 10AC (Overlap-Safe Detection Initial Slice, complete) turns the shipped Phase 10AB Controlled Concurrent Operation Contract vocabulary (`CONCURRENCY_INVALIDATION_TRIGGERS`, `CONCURRENCY_RECOVERY_ACTIONS`) into a bounded observable-mtime detection + refusal layer so the loop can tell when concurrent work would invalidate the active task context, WITHOUT introducing any actual overlapping runtime, background orchestration, parallel worker model, or hidden concurrency. The new `build_desktop_overlap_detection_view(controller_root: Path, *, operator_inputs: dict | None = None) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator (HaltError is soft-failed per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule) and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10ac-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, canonical mirrors of `current_loop_state_status` / `controller_loop_state_approval_mode` / `current_loop_state_phase` / `current_loop_state_sub_phase` / `current_loop_state_cycle_count`, `phase_10ac_runtime_available=True` (the shipped Phase 10AC fix cycle wired a real bounded runtime refusal path -- `enforce_overlap_safe_runtime_gate(...)` inside `_run_normal_cycle_from_increment(...)` raises `HaltError(HALTED_OVERLAP_UNSAFE_CONTEXT, ...)` on a `refused_pending_recovery` aggregate; the narrower deferrals -- actual concurrent Codex/Claude worker runtime, hidden background watchers, and subprocess / network widening -- remain tracked in `ROADMAP.md`), closed enumeration mirrors of `signal_states` / `severity_levels` / `invalidation_triggers` / `recovery_actions`, an `operator_inputs` per-poll mirror, a `signals` list assembled from the closed `_DESKTOP_OVERLAP_DETECTION_SIGNAL_REGISTRY` (five shipped signals covering `codex_review_supersedes_claude_summary`, `claude_prompt_replaced_after_summary`, `fix_prompt_replaced_after_summary`, `loop_state_advanced_past_summary`, and `phase_activation_advanced_past_summary`), an aggregate `overall` dict, and the literal `DESKTOP_OVERLAP_DETECTION_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. Every signal descriptor's `invalidation_trigger` and `recovery_action` are shipped members of the Phase 10AB closed vocabularies verbatim (a dedicated regression test asserts every registry entry passes that guard). The pure `_desktop_overlap_detection_validate_signal_descriptor(...)` validator refuses fail-closed via HaltError on any missing required field, wrong-typed value, unknown `invalidation_trigger`, unknown `recovery_action`, unknown `severity_when_triggered`, or non-POSIX / absolute / drive-prefixed / parent-traversal reference / target `path_canonical_rel`. Per-signal state is derived per-poll via the pure `_desktop_overlap_detection_probe_mtime_pair(...)` helper that consults `Path.stat()` ONLY (the surface NEVER reads canonical artifact BODY content; a dedicated regression test writes unique sentinels into every reference / target artifact and asserts the sentinel does NOT appear in the view dict OR the rendered text) and the pure `_desktop_overlap_detection_evaluate_signal(...)` helper that maps the observed mtime relationship onto one of four closed signal states (`no_signal`, `signal_detected`, `refused_pending_recovery`, `unknown`) and one of four closed severity levels (`info`, `warning`, `refusal`, `unknown`) via the descriptor's `severity_when_triggered`. The aggregate overall state is derived by `_desktop_overlap_detection_derive_overall_state(...)`: any triggered `refusal`-severity signal short-circuits the overall state to `refused_pending_recovery`; any triggered `warning`-severity signal without a refusal produces `signal_detected`; ANY `unknown` signal in the set (Phase 10AC fix cycle: whether every signal is `unknown` OR one or more required overlap-evidence signals are `unknown` alongside otherwise-`no_signal` peers) falls back to `unknown` so the surface can NEVER report `no_signal` while some overlap evidence is unknowable; the baseline `no_signal` requires every signal to have reported `no_signal` (a dedicated regression test bank anchors the mixed-known / mixed-unknown / refusal-short-circuit-over-partial-unknown / warning-wins-over-partial-unknown branches). Operator inputs flow through `_desktop_overlap_detection_normalize_operator_inputs(...)` which accepts `{identity, acknowledged_signal_ids}` (a non-dict / wrong-typed value is refused fail-closed via HaltError); per the Phase 10S source-of-truth preservation rule these inputs are held in-memory only, NEVER persisted to disk, NEVER auto-filled from any OS / packaging / browser-session source, NEVER carried across sessions. The new `cmd_view_desktop_overlap_detection(args)` CLI exposes two optional per-session operator-input flags (`--operator-identity <NAME>`, `--acknowledge-signal <ID>` repeatable) that thread the inputs through to the view builder explicitly per invocation. The view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds an `overlap_detection_view` sub-view alongside the existing fifteen sub-views, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders an `Overlap-Safe Detection (Phase 10AC)` section that reuses `render_desktop_overlap_detection_text(...)` verbatim - the per-signal lines surface each signal's `description`, `safety_copy`, `invalidation_trigger`, `reference_path_canonical_rel` / `target_path_canonical_rel`, per-artifact existence + mtime + `reference_newer_than_target`, `severity_when_triggered`, derived `signal_state` / `severity` / `reason`, `recovery_action`, `deferred_runtime_marker`, and (when refused) `refusal_reason_template` on their own indented lines with explicit `[canonical mirror]` / `[advisory]` / `[overlap-signal]` / `[overlap-severity]` / `[overlap-recovery]` / `[overlap-overall]` / `[deferred-runtime]` / `[refused]` tags so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10AC slice wires a twelfth Tkinter panel beneath the existing Phase 10Q + Phase 10R + Phase 10T + Phase 10U + Phase 10V + Phase 10W + Phase 10X + Phase 10Y + Phase 10Z + Phase 10AA + Phase 10AB control panels inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: one real `tk.Button` per shipped signal rendered via the new `build_desktop_overlap_detection_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function that COPY-PASTES an operator-visible recovery-acknowledgement TEMPLATE (not a shipped subcommand) to clipboard. Every descriptor carries `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` and `category='overlap_detection_ux'`; ZERO new library-callable controls are introduced and the Phase 10I three-control library-callable cap is preserved exactly (a dedicated regression test asserts the Phase 10I registry still ships exactly `view-external-status` / `view-external-controls` / `inspect-external-target`). Matching the Phase 10Z / 10AA / 10AB fix-cycle affordance pattern, each button surfaces `enabled=True` so the shipped GUI can actually expose the copy path (the click ONLY copies the recovery-acknowledgement TEMPLATE into the OS clipboard, never invokes a shipped subcommand and never mutates a hidden detection cache), while the underlying runtime-actionability of the button DESCRIPTOR itself is surfaced separately via `runtime_enabled` (kept False in this slice because the copy-paste button does NOT dispatch the shipped runtime refusal gate; the shipped runtime side of Phase 10AC lives in `enforce_overlap_safe_runtime_gate(...)` inside `_run_normal_cycle_from_increment(...)`, not on this button) plus the appended `[<signal_state>]` tag on the button label. The new `view-desktop-overlap-detection` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_overlap_detection(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds; `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow and omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-overlap-detection] REFUSED: ...` stderr line. Per the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 3A orchestrator contract, and the Phase 4 planner / activator separation the `cmd_view_desktop_overlap_detection(...)` reporter handler itself remains a Phase 7C read-only reporter (the shipped runtime refusal path is a separate orchestrator-owned helper, described further below): it NEVER mutates any canonical artifact, NEVER writes a detection cache on disk, NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, NEVER advances loop-state, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawns a subprocess, NEVER opens a network socket, NEVER launches or coordinates any actual concurrent Codex/Claude runtime (a dedicated regression test patches `threading.Thread`, `multiprocessing.Process`, and `multiprocessing.Pool` and asserts zero calls during a full view build), NEVER reads canonical artifact BODY content (only stat / mtime; a dedicated sentinel-write regression test writes unique sentinels into every reference / target artifact and asserts the sentinel does NOT leak into the view or rendered text), NEVER schedules a background watcher, and NEVER introduces a detection-side database / preference store / recents list / identity token / session token. Phase 10AC ships a BOUNDED runtime refusal gate wired into the shipped orchestrator so the slice does more than report the signal: the `enforce_overlap_safe_runtime_gate(repo_root: Path) -> None` helper delegates verbatim to `build_desktop_overlap_detection_view(...)` and raises `HaltError(HALTED_OVERLAP_UNSAFE_CONTEXT, ...)` when the aggregate `overall_signal_state` is `refused_pending_recovery`. The gate is wired into `_run_normal_cycle_from_increment(...)` at the pre-Codex-review point (after Claude has written a fresh `claude-summary.md` and evidence has been captured); at that point the summary is the newest canonical artifact for a clean cycle, so any reference-newer-than-summary refusal signal is a real observable overlap anomaly rather than a benign cycle-start pre-condition. When the gate raises the shipped orchestrator routes the refusal through the existing `_halt(...)` path so `.agent-loop/loop-state.json` records `status = "halted_overlap_unsafe_context"` and `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` records the audit line. The gate is bounded per the Phase 10AC contract exactly like the reporter path: it consults the same signal registry the desktop surface exposes; it does NOT spawn a subprocess, open a network socket, launch or coordinate any actual concurrent Codex/Claude runtime, schedule a background watcher, read canonical artifact BODY content, widen the Phase 10I library-callable cap, or advance loop-state past the caller-owned refusal write. A dedicated regression test bank exercises the gate directly (refusal state fires HaltError, `no_signal` / `signal_detected` / `unknown` return None silently, no subprocess / socket / thread / process / pool spawned, loop-state not mutated) and end-to-end through `run_normal_cycle(...)` (a synthetic `refused_pending_recovery` aggregate persists `HALTED_OVERLAP_UNSAFE_CONTEXT` to loop-state.json; `no_signal` / `signal_detected` / `unknown` aggregates NEVER produce that status). This slice does NOT introduce: any actual overlapping Codex/Claude runtime, silent background orchestration, or hidden parallel worker model; any Codex-owned concurrent work beyond bounded detection and refusal (that is deferred to Phase 10AD); any automatic next-phase activation behavior; any claim that fully autonomous PRD-to-product execution is already solved; any packaging work, hidden orchestration, or live concurrency added under the banner of this detection slice; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, the Phase 10O MCP integration contract, the Phase 10P desktop setup runtime, the Phase 10Q run-profiles runtime, the Phase 10R project-start runtime, the Phase 10S MCP server selection UX contract, the Phase 10T MCP read-only assistance surface, the Phase 10U MCP action guardrails surface, the Phase 10V RAG source selection contract, the Phase 10W RAG local index and retrieval controls, the Phase 10X autonomous run console, the Phase 10Y capacity recovery console, the Phase 10Z selection UX contract, the Phase 10AA memory-vault export contract, or the Phase 10AB controlled-concurrency contract; or any Git automation.

Phase 10AB (Controlled Concurrent Operation Contract, complete) ships the first bounded controlled-concurrency CONTRACT slice for the shipped Codex / Claude / orchestrator / human role model, WITHOUT introducing any actual overlapping runtime, background orchestration, parallel worker model, or hidden concurrency. The new `build_desktop_concurrency_view(controller_root: Path, *, operator_inputs: dict | None = None) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator (HaltError is soft-failed per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule) and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10ab-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, canonical mirrors of `current_loop_state_status` / `controller_loop_state_approval_mode` / `current_loop_state_phase` / `current_loop_state_sub_phase` / `current_loop_state_cycle_count`, `phase_10ab_runtime_available=False` (this slice ships the CONTRACT only; the actual overlap-detection runtime is deferred to a future Phase 10+ runtime slice tracked in `ROADMAP.md`), closed enumeration mirrors of `overlap_states` / `ownership_roles` / `staleness_states` / `invalidation_triggers` / `recovery_actions` / `refusal_reasons` / `enablement_states` / `approval_requirements`, an `operator_inputs` per-poll mirror, a `rules` list assembled from the closed `_DESKTOP_CONCURRENCY_RULE_REGISTRY` (six shipped rules covering `codex_review_verdict_supersedes_in_flight_claude_work`, `claude_prompt_replacement_invalidates_prior_run`, `fix_prompt_replacement_invalidates_prior_fix`, `loop_state_advancement_invalidates_context`, `shared_canonical_artifact_reads_stay_overlap_safe`, and `human_owned_artifacts_freeze_concurrent_write`), an `ownership_map` list assembled from the closed `_DESKTOP_CONCURRENCY_OWNERSHIP_MAP` (every shipped canonical artifact maps to exactly one closed `owner_role` -- `claude_owned` / `codex_owned` / `orchestrator_owned` / `human_owned` / `shared_read_only`), and the literal `DESKTOP_CONCURRENCY_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. The pure `_desktop_concurrency_validate_rule_descriptor(...)` and `_desktop_concurrency_validate_ownership_entry(...)` validators refuse fail-closed via HaltError on any missing required field, wrong-typed value, unknown closed-enumeration member, non-POSIX / absolute / drive-prefixed / parent-traversal ownership path, or unknown `owner_role`. Per-artifact staleness is derived per-poll via the pure `_desktop_concurrency_probe_artifact_staleness(...)` helper that consults `Path.stat()` ONLY (the surface NEVER reads canonical artifact BODY content; a dedicated regression test writes unique sentinels into every ownership-map artifact and asserts the sentinel does NOT appear in the view dict OR the rendered text); the five closed staleness states (`fresh`, `stale_cycle`, `stale_phase`, `missing`, `unknown`) are derived from the shipped loop-state mtime plus a 1-hour cycle-scale grace window and a 7-day phase-scale grace window. Per-rule approval state is computed by `_desktop_concurrency_compute_approval_state(...)` which returns a per-requirement `{satisfied, reason}` dict spanning five closed requirements: `operator_acknowledged_contract`, `operator_supplied_identity`, `approval_mode_supports_concurrency` (review / autonomous; strict mode refuses fail-closed per Phase 5C), `phase_10ab_runtime_available`, and `no_invalidation_trigger_active` (hard-coded False in this slice so the fail-closed default is exact). Per-rule enablement state is computed by `_desktop_concurrency_compute_enablement_state(...)` which surfaces three closed states (`disabled_by_default` / `enabled_pending_runtime` / `refused_until_policy_update`): runtime-side requirements gate `refused_until_policy_update`; operator-side requirements gate `disabled_by_default`; promotion to `enabled_pending_runtime` requires every requirement satisfied. Since `phase_10ab_runtime_available` is hard-coded `False` in this slice every rule ALWAYS surfaces as `refused_until_policy_update` regardless of operator input - the headline boundary is anchored by a dedicated regression test that supplies every operator-side approval input and still asserts the fail-closed state. Operator inputs flow through `_desktop_concurrency_normalize_operator_inputs(...)` which accepts `{identity, acknowledged_rule_ids}` (a non-dict / wrong-typed value is refused fail-closed via HaltError); per the Phase 10S source-of-truth preservation rule these inputs are held in-memory only, NEVER persisted to disk, NEVER auto-filled from any OS / packaging / browser-session source, NEVER carried across sessions. The new `cmd_view_desktop_concurrency(args)` CLI exposes two optional per-session operator-input flags (`--operator-identity <NAME>`, `--acknowledge-rule <ID>` repeatable) that thread the inputs through to the view builder explicitly per invocation. The view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `concurrency_view` sub-view alongside the existing fourteen sub-views, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders a `Controlled Concurrent Operation (Phase 10AB)` section that reuses `render_desktop_concurrency_text(...)` verbatim - the per-rule lines surface each rule's `description`, `safety_copy`, `overlap_state`, `owner_role_writer` / `owner_role_reader`, `invalidation_trigger`, `recovery_action`, per-requirement approval state, `enablement_reason`, `deferred_runtime_marker`, and (when refused) `refusal_reason_template` on their own indented lines with explicit `[canonical mirror]` / `[advisory]` / `[concurrency-rule]` / `[concurrency-overlap]` / `[concurrency-owner]` / `[concurrency-invalidation]` / `[concurrency-recovery]` / `[concurrency-approval]` / `[concurrency-enablement]` / `[deferred-runtime]` / `[ownership-map]` / `[refused]` tags so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10AB slice wires an eleventh Tkinter panel beneath the existing Phase 10Q + Phase 10R + Phase 10T + Phase 10U + Phase 10V + Phase 10W + Phase 10X + Phase 10Y + Phase 10Z + Phase 10AA control panels inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: one real `tk.Button` per shipped rule rendered via the new `build_desktop_concurrency_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function that COPY-PASTES an operator-visible contract acknowledgement TEMPLATE (not a shipped subcommand) to clipboard. Every descriptor carries `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` and `category='concurrency_contract_ux'`; ZERO new library-callable controls are introduced and the Phase 10I three-control library-callable cap is preserved exactly (a dedicated regression test asserts the Phase 10I registry still ships exactly `view-external-status` / `view-external-controls` / `inspect-external-target`). Phase 10AA (Human-Facing Memory Vault Export Contract And Initial Slice, complete) ships the first bounded human-facing memory-vault export contract slice so operators can browse a bounded READ-ONLY view of durable-memory entries plus selected canonical-artifact mirrors from the desktop app WITHOUT letting the export become a competing source of truth. The new `build_desktop_memory_vault_view(controller_root: Path, *, operator_inputs: dict | None = None) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator (HaltError is soft-failed per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule), and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10aa-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, canonical mirrors of `current_loop_state_status` / `controller_loop_state_approval_mode`, `phase_10aa_runtime_available=False` (this slice ships the CONTRACT and the export UX only; the actual export renderer is deferred to a future Phase 10+ runtime slice tracked in `ROADMAP.md`), closed enumeration mirrors of `export_categories` / `source_kinds` / `advisory_labels` / `freshness_states` / `enablement_states` / `approval_requirements` / `refusal_reasons`, `freshness_stale_threshold_seconds = 30*24*3600`, an `operator_inputs` per-poll mirror, an `exports` list assembled from the closed `_DESKTOP_MEMORY_VAULT_REGISTRY`, and the literal `DESKTOP_MEMORY_VAULT_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. The initial registry ships five concrete exports spanning every closed `export_category` and both closed `source_kind` members (`durable_memory_decision_index` -> `durable_memory_entry` / `shipped_memory_json` / `.agent-loop/memory/decision`; `durable_memory_summary_index` -> `durable_memory_entry` / `shipped_memory_json` / `.agent-loop/memory/summary`; `decision_summary_from_claude_summary` -> `decision_summary` / `canonical_artifact_mirror` / `.agent-loop/claude-summary.md`; `architecture_snapshot_from_agents_md` -> `architecture_snapshot` / `canonical_artifact_mirror` / `AGENTS.md`; `architecture_snapshot_from_phase_plan` -> `architecture_snapshot` / `canonical_artifact_mirror` / `.agent-loop/phase-plan.md`) so every descriptor validator branch is exercised end-to-end. The pure `_desktop_memory_vault_validate_descriptor(...)` validator refuses fail-closed via HaltError on any missing required field, wrong-typed value, unknown `export_category`, unknown `source_kind`, unknown `advisory_label_rule`, unknown `approval_requirements` member, non-tuple approval-requirements value, or (matching the Phase 10V fix cycle) on any `path_canonical_rel` that is not a POSIX-style repo-relative path bounded inside the controller root: a backslash, a leading `/`, a Windows-style drive prefix (`C:` / `C:/foo`), or any `..` parent-directory segment is refused fail-closed so a future malformed registry entry cannot silently widen the bounded repo-local export model. Per-export freshness is derived per-poll via the pure `_desktop_memory_vault_probe_freshness(...)` helper that consults `Path.stat()` for files and (for directory sources) counts the `.json` children via `Path.iterdir()` ONLY (the freshness probe NEVER reads source CONTENT; a dedicated regression test writes a unique sentinel to every registered source path on disk and asserts the sentinel does NOT appear on the probe dict); the four closed freshness states (`fresh`, `stale`, `missing`, `unknown`) are gated on the `MEMORY_VAULT_FRESHNESS_STALE_THRESHOLD_SECONDS = 30 * 24 * 3600` constant. Per the Phase 10AA fix cycle, the shipped desktop app and CLI actually render a readable human-facing memory-vault export body via a bounded second helper `_desktop_memory_vault_read_excerpt(...)`: canonical-artifact mirror sources surface a HEAD-BOUNDED excerpt truncated at `MEMORY_VAULT_EXCERPT_BYTE_LIMIT = 2000` bytes so the reviewer sees the opening context of `.agent-loop/claude-summary.md` / `AGENTS.md` / `.agent-loop/phase-plan.md` without the surface silently mirroring the full canonical file into a competing source of truth (dedicated regression tests write a sentinel PAST the byte cap and assert it does NOT leak into the view dict or the rendered text; a matching regression test asserts the excerpt is exactly bounded at the cap); shipped durable-memory JSON directory sources surface a NAME-ONLY index bounded at `MEMORY_VAULT_ENTRY_INDEX_LIMIT = 10` most-recent shipped `.json` filenames (sorted reverse-alphabetic == newest-first, matching the Phase 6A writer name format `<UTC-timestamp>-<hash>.json`) so the operator sees WHICH shipped decision / summary entries exist without letting the memory-vault surface become a competing durable-memory reader (a dedicated regression test writes a sentinel INSIDE a shipped memory JSON body and asserts the sentinel does NOT leak into the view or the rendered text; the shipped `read_memory_entry(...)` primitive remains the sole reader for the JSON body). The excerpt / entry-index rendering is separately tagged (`[vault-excerpt]` / `[vault-entry-index]`) so a downstream tool can identify the readable-body rows unambiguously. Per-export approval state is computed by `_desktop_memory_vault_compute_approval_state(...)` which returns a per-requirement `{satisfied, reason}` dict spanning five closed requirements: `operator_acknowledged_advisory_labeling`, `operator_supplied_identity`, `approval_mode_supports_export` (review / autonomous; strict mode refuses fail-closed per Phase 5C), `phase_10aa_runtime_available`, and `source_present_in_repo` (derived per-poll from the freshness probe). Per-export enablement state is computed by `_desktop_memory_vault_compute_enablement_state(...)` which surfaces three closed states (`disabled_by_default` / `enabled_pending_runtime` / `refused_until_policy_update`): the labeling-rule short-circuit refuses fail-closed when `advisory_label_rule == "refused_until_policy_update"`; runtime-side requirements (`phase_10aa_runtime_available`, `source_present_in_repo`) gate `refused_until_policy_update`; operator-side requirements gate `disabled_by_default`; promotion to `enabled_pending_runtime` requires every requirement satisfied. Since `phase_10aa_runtime_available` is hard-coded `False` in this slice every export ALWAYS surfaces as `refused_until_policy_update` regardless of operator input - the headline boundary is anchored by a dedicated regression test that supplies every operator-side approval input and still asserts the fail-closed state. Operator inputs flow through `_desktop_memory_vault_normalize_operator_inputs(...)` which accepts `{identity, acknowledged_export_ids}` (a non-dict / wrong-typed value is refused fail-closed via HaltError); per the Phase 10S source-of-truth preservation rule these inputs are held in-memory only, NEVER persisted to disk, NEVER auto-filled from any OS / packaging / browser-session source, NEVER carried across sessions. The new `cmd_view_desktop_memory_vault(args)` CLI exposes two optional per-session operator-input flags (`--operator-identity <NAME>`, `--acknowledge-export <ID>` repeatable) that thread the inputs through to the view builder explicitly per invocation. The view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `memory_vault_view` sub-view alongside the existing thirteen sub-views, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders a `Memory Vault Export (Phase 10AA)` section that reuses `render_desktop_memory_vault_text(...)` verbatim - the per-export lines surface each export's `description`, `safety_copy`, `source_kind`, `advisory_label_rule`, `path_canonical_rel`, per-requirement approval state, `enablement_reason`, `deferred_runtime_marker`, and (when refused) `refusal_reason_template` on their own indented lines with explicit `[canonical mirror]` / `[advisory]` / `[vault-export]` / `[vault-source]` / `[vault-freshness]` / `[vault-approval]` / `[vault-enablement]` / `[deferred-runtime]` / `[refused]` tags so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10AA slice wires a tenth Tkinter panel beneath the existing Phase 10Q + Phase 10R + Phase 10T + Phase 10U + Phase 10V + Phase 10W + Phase 10X + Phase 10Y + Phase 10Z control panels inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: one real `tk.Button` per shipped export rendered via the new `build_desktop_memory_vault_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function that COPY-PASTES an operator-visible enablement-request TEMPLATE (not a shipped subcommand) to clipboard so the operator raises the request through the shipped review channel instead of silently mutating a hidden export cache. Every descriptor carries `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` and `category='memory_vault_export_ux'`; ZERO new library-callable controls are introduced and the Phase 10I three-control library-callable cap is preserved exactly (a dedicated regression test asserts the Phase 10I registry still ships exactly `view-external-status` / `view-external-controls` / `inspect-external-target`). Matching the Phase 10Z fix-cycle affordance pattern, each button surfaces `enabled=True` so the shipped GUI can actually expose the copy path (the click ONLY copies the enablement-request TEMPLATE into the OS clipboard, never invokes a shipped subcommand and never mutates a hidden export cache), while the underlying export-runtime state is surfaced separately on each descriptor via `runtime_enabled` (True only when `enablement_state == "enabled_pending_runtime"`; always False in this slice because `phase_10aa_runtime_available=False`), `enablement_state` (canonical mirror), `refusal_reason_template`, and an appended `[<enablement_state>]` tag on the button label so an operator reading the shipped UI can see that the export runtime is still deferred even though the copy affordance is clickable; dedicated regression tests anchor that boundary end-to-end (every button clickable even when every operator input is supplied, every `runtime_enabled` still False, every clipboard payload a template rather than a shipped subcommand, and every button `command` limited to the non-mutating clipboard-copy). Each `_refresh()` poll re-renders the memory-vault export button row from the cached sub-view so error envelopes drop the buttons without crashing the widget. The new `view-desktop-memory-vault` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_memory_vault(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds; `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow (matching the other Phase 10 desktop subcommands) and omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-memory-vault] REFUSED: ...` stderr line. Per the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 6A/6B durable-memory ownership rules, the Phase 10Q existing run-profile / approval-mode semantics, and the Phase 10S source-of-truth preservation rule this handler is MEMORY-VAULT-CONTRACT ONLY: it NEVER mutates any canonical artifact (a dedicated regression test snapshots `loop-state.json` before / after and asserts byte-for-byte equality), NEVER writes an export file on disk (a dedicated regression test snapshots the controller root's directory listing before / after and asserts byte-for-byte equality), NEVER mutates any shipped durable-memory entry (a dedicated regression test asserts the memory / decision / summary directories are untouched), NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` (a dedicated regression test asserts the log file does not appear), NEVER advances loop-state, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawns a subprocess (a dedicated regression test patches all six Python entry points and asserts zero calls during a render), NEVER opens a network socket (a dedicated regression test patches `socket.socket` and asserts zero opens during a render), NEVER reads durable-memory JSON BODY content (only stat / mtime / size / directory entry names via `Path.iterdir()`; a dedicated sentinel-write regression test anchors this exactly and asserts the JSON body sentinel does NOT leak into the view or rendered text), NEVER reads MORE than `MEMORY_VAULT_EXCERPT_BYTE_LIMIT` bytes of any canonical artifact (a dedicated head-cap regression test writes a sentinel past the byte cap and asserts it never leaks; the shipped canonical file remains the sole source of truth for the full body), NEVER persists an export cache, NEVER schedules a background watcher, NEVER auto-activates the next phase (per the Phase 4 planner / activator separation), and NEVER introduces a memory-vault-side database / preference store / recents list / identity token / session token. This slice does NOT introduce: any hidden replacement memory store, silent durable-memory mutation, or background control plane that bypasses the shipped Python runtime; any automatic next-phase activation behavior; any claim that fully autonomous PRD-to-product execution is already solved; any concurrent Codex/Claude overlap execution, packaging work, or hidden orchestration; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, the Phase 10O MCP integration contract, the Phase 10P desktop setup runtime, the Phase 10Q run-profiles runtime, the Phase 10R project-start runtime, the Phase 10S MCP server selection UX contract, the Phase 10T MCP read-only assistance surface, the Phase 10U MCP action guardrails surface, the Phase 10V RAG source selection contract, the Phase 10W RAG local index and retrieval controls, the Phase 10X autonomous run console, the Phase 10Y capacity recovery console, or the Phase 10Z selection UX contract; or any Git automation.

Phase 10Z (Model, Policy Pack, And Template Selection UX, complete) ships the first bounded desktop selection-UX contract slice so operators can browse the shipped model / policy-pack / project-template presets from the desktop app WITHOUT letting those choices become a hidden second source of truth. The new `build_desktop_selection_view(controller_root: Path, *, operator_inputs: dict | None = None) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator (HaltError is soft-failed per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule), and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10z-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, canonical mirrors of `current_loop_state_status` / `controller_loop_state_approval_mode`, `phase_10z_runtime_available=False` (this slice ships the CONTRACT only; the actual selection-runtime is deferred to a future Phase 10+ runtime slice tracked in `ROADMAP.md`), closed enumeration mirrors of `preset_categories` / `enablement_states` / `approval_requirements` / `refusal_reasons`, an `operator_inputs` per-poll mirror, a `presets` list assembled from the closed `_DESKTOP_SELECTION_REGISTRY`, and the literal `DESKTOP_SELECTION_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. The initial registry ships three concrete presets spanning every closed `preset_category` (`shipped_default_model` -> `model`; `shipped_default_policy_pack` -> `policy_pack`; `shipped_default_project_template` -> `project_template`) so every descriptor validator branch is exercised end-to-end. The pure `_desktop_selection_validate_descriptor(...)` validator refuses fail-closed via HaltError on any missing required field, wrong-typed value, unknown `preset_category`, unknown `approval_requirements` member, or non-tuple approval-requirements value. Per-preset approval state is computed by `_desktop_selection_compute_approval_state(...)` which returns a per-requirement `{satisfied, reason}` dict spanning five closed requirements: `operator_acknowledged_preset_scope`, `operator_supplied_identity`, `approval_mode_supports_selection` (review / autonomous; strict mode refuses fail-closed per Phase 5C), `phase_10z_runtime_available`, `policy_rule_permits_selection` (operator-side additive allow-list; the policy pack is NOT a refusal set, it is an explicit opt-in per-session list per the Phase 10Z fail-closed default). Per-preset enablement state is computed by `_desktop_selection_compute_enablement_state(...)` which surfaces three closed states (`disabled_by_default` / `enabled_pending_runtime` / `refused_until_policy_update`): `phase_10z_runtime_available=False` short-circuits every preset to `refused_until_policy_update`; when the runtime does ship, promotion to `enabled_pending_runtime` requires every closed approval requirement to be satisfied. Since `phase_10z_runtime_available` is hard-coded `False` in this slice every preset ALWAYS surfaces as `refused_until_policy_update` regardless of operator input - the headline boundary is anchored by a dedicated regression test that supplies every operator-side approval input and still asserts the fail-closed state. Operator inputs flow through `_desktop_selection_normalize_operator_inputs(...)` which accepts `{identity, acknowledged_preset_ids, policy_permitted_preset_ids}` (a non-dict / wrong-typed value is refused fail-closed via HaltError); per the Phase 10S source-of-truth preservation rule these inputs are held in-memory only, NEVER persisted to disk, NEVER auto-filled from any OS / packaging / browser-session source, NEVER carried across sessions. The new `cmd_view_desktop_selection(args)` CLI exposes three optional per-session operator-input flags (`--operator-identity <NAME>`, `--acknowledge-preset <ID>` repeatable, `--policy-permit-preset <ID>` repeatable) that thread the inputs through to the view builder explicitly per invocation. The view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `selection_view` sub-view alongside the existing twelve sub-views, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders a `Selection UX (Phase 10Z)` section that reuses `render_desktop_selection_text(...)` verbatim - the per-preset lines surface each preset's `preset_summary`, `safety_copy`, per-requirement approval state, `enablement_reason`, and `deferred_runtime_marker` on their own indented lines with explicit `[canonical mirror]` / `[advisory]` / `[selection]` / `[preset-category]` / `[preset-approval]` / `[preset-enablement]` / `[deferred-runtime]` / `[refused]` tags so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10Z slice wires a ninth Tkinter panel beneath the existing Phase 10Q + Phase 10R + Phase 10T + Phase 10U + Phase 10V + Phase 10W + Phase 10X + Phase 10Y control panels inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: one real `tk.Button` per shipped preset rendered via the new `build_desktop_selection_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function that COPY-PASTES an operator-visible enablement-request TEMPLATE (not a shipped subcommand) to clipboard so the operator raises the request through the shipped review channel instead of silently mutating a hidden preset store. Every descriptor carries `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` and `category='selection_ux'`; ZERO new library-callable controls are introduced and the Phase 10I three-control library-callable cap is preserved exactly (a dedicated regression test asserts the Phase 10I registry still ships exactly `view-external-status` / `view-external-controls` / `inspect-external-target`). Each button surfaces `enabled=True` so the shipped GUI can actually expose the copy path (the click ONLY copies the enablement-request TEMPLATE into the OS clipboard, never invokes a shipped subcommand and never mutates a hidden preset store), while the underlying preset-runtime state is surfaced separately on each descriptor via `runtime_enabled` (True only when `enablement_state == "enabled_pending_runtime"`; always False in this slice because `phase_10z_runtime_available=False`), `enablement_state` (canonical mirror), `refusal_reason_template`, and an appended `[<enablement_state>]` tag on the button label so an operator reading the shipped UI can see that the runtime is still deferred even though the copy affordance is clickable; a dedicated regression test anchors that boundary end-to-end (every button clickable even when every operator input is supplied, every `runtime_enabled` still False, every clipboard payload a template rather than a shipped subcommand, and every button `command` limited to the non-mutating clipboard-copy). Each `_refresh()` poll re-renders the selection button row from the cached sub-view so error envelopes drop the buttons without crashing the widget. The new `view-desktop-selection` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_selection(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds; `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow (matching the other Phase 10 desktop subcommands) and omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-selection] REFUSED: ...` stderr line. Per the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10Q existing run-profile / approval-mode semantics, and the Phase 10S source-of-truth preservation rule this handler is SELECTION-CONTRACT ONLY: it NEVER mutates any canonical artifact (a dedicated regression test snapshots `loop-state.json` before / after and asserts byte-for-byte equality), NEVER writes a selection cache on disk (a dedicated regression test snapshots the controller root's directory listing before / after and asserts byte-for-byte equality), NEVER mutates a shipped model / policy-pack / template store, NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` (a dedicated regression test asserts the log file does not appear), NEVER advances loop-state, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawns a subprocess (a dedicated regression test patches all six Python entry points and asserts zero calls during a render), NEVER opens a network socket (a dedicated regression test patches `socket.socket` and asserts zero opens during a render), NEVER refreshes a preset from a hosted service, NEVER schedules a background watcher, NEVER auto-activates the next phase (per the Phase 4 planner / activator separation), and NEVER introduces a preset-side database / preference store / recents list / identity token / session token. Phase 10Y (Capacity Recovery And Resume Console, complete) ships the first bounded desktop capacity-recovery / resume-console surface so token or rate-limit halts, checkpoint presence, bounded automatic-resume policy, retry or backoff state, and operator override or resume actions are legible from the desktop app and reporters WITHOUT introducing a hidden second control plane. The new `build_desktop_resume_console_view(controller_root: Path) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator (HaltError is soft-failed per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule), and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10y-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, canonical mirrors of `current_loop_state_status` / `controller_loop_state_approval_mode` / `cycle_count` / `max_cycles` / `awaiting_human_for`, a derived `capacity_halt_state` label (closed enumeration), a bounded checkpoint mirror (`checkpoint_state` / `checkpoint_count` / `checkpoint_names` bounded to `RESUME_CONSOLE_CHECKPOINT_MIRROR_CAP = 25` file names), a derived `resume_policy_state` label (closed enumeration), a derived `retry_state` label (closed enumeration), the operator-facing `recovery_hint` mirrored verbatim from the shipped `STATUS_RECOVERY_HINTS` map, closed enumeration mirrors of every state vocabulary, and the literal `DESKTOP_RESUME_CONSOLE_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. Every field is derived from canonical artifacts; no field is invented; no hidden UI-only field is introduced. The closed `RESUME_CONSOLE_CAPACITY_HALT_STATES` vocabulary maps the shipped halt vocabulary onto one of five labels (`not_halted`, `awaiting_token_exhaustion_continuation`, `halted_capacity_recoverable`, `halted_capacity_terminal`, `unknown`) so a reviewer can see at a glance whether the loop is running, at the specific token-exhaustion halt the shipped Phase 6F/6G runtime supports, at a recoverable non-capacity halt, or at a human-required terminal halt.
