The closed `RESUME_CONSOLE_CHECKPOINT_STATES` vocabulary (`not_present`, `present`, `present_multiple`, `unknown`) is derived per invocation from a bounded read of `.agent-loop/memory/checkpoint/` (the surface reads only file NAMES; it never opens a checkpoint file body, never invokes `load_memory_entry`, never persists a snapshot). The closed `RESUME_CONSOLE_RESUME_POLICY_STATES` vocabulary (`manual_resume_only`, `bounded_auto_continue_available`, `autonomous_bounded`, `unknown`) is derived from the canonical `approval_mode` cross-referenced with `capacity_halt_state`: a token-exhaustion halt short-circuits to `bounded_auto_continue_available` (matching the shipped Phase 6G `auto-continue` command); the shipped Phase 5D autonomous mode surfaces as `autonomous_bounded` (a bounded strict-gate bypass, NOT the unshipped Phase 9 fully-autonomous mode); `review` and `strict` surface as `manual_resume_only`. The closed `RESUME_CONSOLE_RETRY_STATES` vocabulary (`not_in_retry`, `within_retry_budget`, `at_max_cycles`, `unknown`) is derived from the canonical `cycle_count` / `max_cycles` pair so an operator can immediately see whether the retry budget has been exhausted. The view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `resume_console_view` sub-view alongside the existing eleven sub-views, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders a `Capacity Recovery Console (Phase 10Y)` section that reuses `render_desktop_resume_console_text(...)` verbatim - the per-field lines surface each derived state on its own indented line with explicit `[canonical mirror]` / `[advisory]` / `[resume-console]` / `[capacity-halt]` / `[checkpoint]` / `[resume-policy]` / `[retry]` / `[refused]` tags so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10Y slice wires an eighth Tkinter panel beneath the existing Phase 10Q + Phase 10R + Phase 10T + Phase 10U + Phase 10V + Phase 10W + Phase 10X control panels inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: three real `tk.Button` widgets rendered via the new `build_desktop_resume_console_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function that COPY-PASTE shipped recovery commands to clipboard (`python scripts/agent_loop.py resume`, `python scripts/agent_loop.py auto-continue`, `python scripts/agent_loop.py check-state`). Every descriptor carries `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` and `category='resume_console_recovery'`; ZERO new library-callable controls are introduced and the Phase 10I three-control library-callable cap is preserved exactly (a dedicated regression test asserts the Phase 10I registry still ships exactly `view-external-status` / `view-external-controls` / `inspect-external-target`). The `resume` and `auto-continue` buttons re-derive their `enabled` bit from the live `capacity_halt_state` so the console never advertises a runnable path the shipped runtime would immediately refuse: `resume` is enabled only when the derived `capacity_halt_state` is a CONFIRMED halt member of the closed Phase 10Y allowlist (`awaiting_token_exhaustion_continuation`, `halted_capacity_recoverable`, `halted_capacity_terminal`); the `unknown` state (produced when the view builder soft-fails an unreadable / malformed `loop-state.json`) fails closed so the operator is never invited to paste a `resume` invocation the shipped runtime could not confirm applies; `auto-continue` is enabled only when the capacity halt is specifically `awaiting_token_exhaustion_continuation` (per the shipped Phase 6G contract); `check-state` is always enabled because it is a pure Phase 7C reporter. Each `_refresh()` poll re-renders the resume-console button row from the cached sub-view so error envelopes drop the buttons without crashing the widget. The new `view-desktop-resume-console` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_resume_console(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds; `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow (matching the other Phase 10 desktop subcommands) and omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-resume-console] REFUSED: ...` stderr line. Per the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 3A orchestrator contract, and the Phase 6E/6F/6G checkpoint / resume / continuation contract this handler is VISIBILITY-ONLY: it NEVER mutates any canonical artifact (a dedicated regression test snapshots `loop-state.json` before / after and asserts byte-for-byte equality), NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` (a dedicated regression test asserts the log file does not appear), NEVER advances loop-state, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawns a subprocess (a dedicated regression test patches all six Python entry points and asserts zero calls during a render), NEVER opens a network socket (a dedicated regression test patches `socket.socket` and asserts zero opens during a render), NEVER polls a token or capacity endpoint, NEVER refreshes a token, NEVER retries a failed cycle, NEVER schedules a background watcher, NEVER persists a resume-console cache on disk (a dedicated regression test snapshots the controller root's directory listing before / after and asserts byte-for-byte equality), NEVER auto-resumes a halted cycle beyond the shipped Phase 6F/6G checkpoint / auto-continue budget, and NEVER auto-activates the next phase (per the Phase 4 planner / activator separation). This slice does NOT introduce: any unbounded autonomy widening, hidden auto-resume behavior, or token-refresh polling outside shipped checkpoint / continuation semantics; any hidden background control plane or second orchestrator that bypasses the shipped Python runtime; any automatic next-phase activation behavior that bypasses or rewrites the shipped Phase 4 planner / activation separation; any claim that fully autonomous PRD-to-product execution is already solved; any concurrent Codex/Claude overlap execution, model-policy extensibility, or hidden orchestration added under the banner of capacity recovery; any GitHub integration, packaging / code-signing / auto-update pipeline, system-tray surface, or controlled-concurrency runtime; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, the Phase 10O MCP integration contract, the Phase 10P desktop setup runtime, the Phase 10Q run-profiles runtime, the Phase 10R project-start runtime, the Phase 10S MCP server selection UX contract, the Phase 10T MCP read-only assistance surface, the Phase 10U MCP action guardrails surface, the Phase 10V RAG source selection contract, the Phase 10W RAG local index and retrieval controls, or the Phase 10X autonomous run console; or any Git automation.

Phase 10X (Autonomous Run Console And Completion Ledger, complete) ships the first bounded desktop autonomous run console and completion-ledger surface so PRD-to-completion mode exposes the active step, pending work, blocked or deferred work, fix-cycle state, and completion progress WITHOUT introducing a hidden second control plane. The new `build_desktop_run_console_view(controller_root: Path) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator (HaltError is soft-failed per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule), and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10x-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, canonical mirrors of `active_phase_id` / `active_sub_phase_id` / `active_task_summary` / `current_loop_state_status` / `controller_loop_state_approval_mode` / `cycle_count` / `max_cycles` / `last_verdict` / `last_verdict_phase`, first-line `current_phase_md_mirror` and `current_task_md_mirror` reads (bounded to 4 KiB per source; a fixed-cap read cannot balloon the surface), a per-phase `completion_ledger` derived from `.agent-loop/phase-plan.md` (bounded to the first `RUN_CONSOLE_PHASE_PLAN_HEADER_CAP = 200` `## Phase X - Title` headers so a malformed plan cannot balloon the surface), a `completion_progress` integer snapshot (`total_phases` / `complete_count` / `in_progress_count` / `pending_count` / `unknown_count`), closed enumeration mirrors of `activity_states` / `cycle_states` / `completion_states` / `fix_cycle_states` / `refusal_reasons`, and the literal `DESKTOP_RUN_CONSOLE_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. Every field is derived from canonical artifacts; no field is invented; no hidden UI-only field is introduced. The closed `RUN_CONSOLE_ACTIVITY_STATES` vocabulary maps the shipped loop-state status vocabulary onto one of nine labels (`running`, `awaiting_review`, `awaiting_claude`, `awaiting_fix`, `awaiting_human`, `phase_complete`, `halted_recoverable`, `halted_requires_human`, `unknown`) so a reviewer can see at a glance whether the loop is actively running, awaiting Codex review, awaiting the normal pre-implementation Claude turn (`last_verdict is None`), awaiting a Claude fix cycle (`last_verdict == "NEEDS_FIXES"`), awaiting a strict-mode human gate, in the human-approval terminal, or in a recoverable / human-required halt. The fix cycle mentioned in the fix-cycle vocabulary and the `awaiting_fix` activity label are gated on the same `last_verdict == "NEEDS_FIXES"` predicate so the two never disagree. The closed `RUN_CONSOLE_CYCLE_STATES` vocabulary (`within_budget`, `at_last_cycle`, `over_budget`, `unknown`) is derived from the canonical `cycle_count` / `max_cycles` pair. The closed `RUN_CONSOLE_FIX_CYCLE_STATES` vocabulary (`not_in_fix_cycle`, `in_fix_cycle`, `unknown`) is derived from the canonical `last_verdict` / `status` pair (a fix cycle is in flight only when `last_verdict == "NEEDS_FIXES"` AND the loop is awaiting Claude / actively running Claude / at a strict pre-fix / pre-codex-review-fix gate). The closed `RUN_CONSOLE_COMPLETION_STATES` vocabulary (`pending`, `in_progress`, `complete`, `unknown`) is derived per phase-plan entry from the active-phase / active-sub-phase lookup: any header listed BEFORE the active phase surfaces as `complete`, the active phase itself surfaces as `in_progress` (or `complete` when the activity state is `phase_complete`), any header listed AFTER surfaces as `pending`, and every header surfaces as `unknown` when the active phase does not appear in the plan (so the surface never silently guesses). The view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `run_console_view` sub-view alongside the existing ten sub-views, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders a `Run Console (Phase 10X)` section that reuses `render_desktop_run_console_text(...)` verbatim - the per-field lines surface each derived state on its own indented line with explicit `[canonical mirror]` / `[advisory]` / `[run-console]` / `[completion-ledger]` / `[cycle]` / `[fix-cycle]` / `[refused]` tags so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10X slice wires a seventh Tkinter panel beneath the existing Phase 10Q + Phase 10R + Phase 10T + Phase 10U + Phase 10V + Phase 10W control panels inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: three real `tk.Button` widgets rendered via the new `build_desktop_run_console_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function that COPY-PASTE shipped inspection commands to clipboard (`python scripts/agent_loop.py status`, `python scripts/agent_loop.py inspect-artifacts`, `python scripts/agent_loop.py check-state`). Every descriptor carries `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` and `category='run_console_visibility'`; ZERO new library-callable controls are introduced and the Phase 10I three-control library-callable cap is preserved exactly (a dedicated regression test asserts the Phase 10I registry still ships exactly `view-external-status` / `view-external-controls` / `inspect-external-target`). Each `_refresh()` poll re-renders the run-console button row from the cached sub-view so error envelopes drop the buttons without crashing the widget. The new `view-desktop-run-console` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_run_console(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds; `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow (matching the other Phase 10 desktop subcommands) and omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-run-console] REFUSED: ...` stderr line. Per the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 3A orchestrator contract, and the Phase 4 planner / activator separation this handler is VISIBILITY-ONLY: it NEVER mutates any canonical artifact (a dedicated regression test snapshots `loop-state.json` before / after and asserts byte-for-byte equality), NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` (a dedicated regression test asserts the log file does not appear), NEVER advances loop-state, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawns a subprocess (a dedicated regression test patches all six Python entry points and asserts zero calls during a render), NEVER opens a network socket (a dedicated regression test patches `socket.socket` and asserts zero opens during a render), NEVER contacts a remote service, NEVER persists a run-console cache or completion-ledger snapshot on disk (a dedicated regression test snapshots the controller root's directory listing before / after and asserts byte-for-byte equality), NEVER subscribes to a background watcher, NEVER auto-resumes a halted cycle, NEVER retries a failed cycle, NEVER refreshes a token, and NEVER auto-activates the next phase (per the Phase 4 planner / activator separation). This slice does NOT introduce: any capacity auto-resume, token-refresh detection, retry/backoff automation, or operator-override runtime beyond passive visibility; any hidden background control plane or second orchestrator that bypasses the shipped Python runtime; any automatic next-phase activation behavior that bypasses or rewrites the shipped Phase 4 planner / activation separation; any claim that fully autonomous PRD-to-product execution is already solved; any concurrent Codex/Claude overlap execution, model-policy extensibility, or hidden orchestration added under the banner of the run console; any GitHub integration, packaging / code-signing / auto-update pipeline, system-tray surface, or controlled-concurrency runtime; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, the Phase 10O MCP integration contract, the Phase 10P desktop setup runtime, the Phase 10Q run-profiles runtime, the Phase 10R project-start runtime, the Phase 10S MCP server selection UX contract, the Phase 10T MCP read-only assistance surface, the Phase 10U MCP action guardrails surface, the Phase 10V RAG source selection contract, or the Phase 10W RAG local index and retrieval controls; or any Git automation.

Phase 10W (RAG Local Index And Retrieval Controls, complete) layers the first bounded controller-local RAG indexing and retrieval-control runtime on top of the shipped Phase 10V source-selection contract. The new `build_desktop_rag_retrieval_controls_view(controller_root: Path, *, operator_inputs: dict | None = None) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator (HaltError is soft-failed per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule), and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10w-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, `current_loop_state_status`, `controller_loop_state_approval_mode`, `phase_10w_runtime_available=True` (this slice ships the first bounded controller-local retrieval runtime; the CLI provides the actual retrieval driver), closed bounded caps (`query_char_cap=512`, `per_source_byte_cap=8192`, `top_k_cap=5`, `top_k_default=3`, `excerpt_window_chars=240`), closed enumeration mirrors of `index_states` / `ranking_modes` / `approval_requirements` / `refusal_reasons`, an `operator_inputs` per-poll mirror, a `sources` list carrying per-source retrieval-eligibility state derived from the Phase 10V `_DESKTOP_RAG_SOURCE_SELECTION_REGISTRY` + operator inputs, and the literal `DESKTOP_RAG_RETRIEVAL_CONTROLS_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. This controls view is READ-ONLY: it NEVER reads source CONTENT (only the Phase 10V freshness probe is consulted for last_modified UTC / size_bytes / freshness_state); actual content reads happen only when the operator explicitly invokes the paired `retrieve_local_rag_excerpts(controller_root, *, operator_inputs, query, top_k=None) -> dict` library function or the `run-local-rag-retrieval` CLI. The retrieval library function is CONTROLLER-LOCAL: it reads at most `RAG_RETRIEVAL_PER_SOURCE_BYTE_CAP = 8192` bytes per acknowledged source (using `open(path, "rb").read(cap + 1)` so oversized files can be marked truncated without unbounded reads), decodes UTF-8 with `errors="replace"` for binary/mixed-encoding tolerance, tokenizes lowercase alphanumeric words via a compiled regex, ranks candidate sources by a bounded term-overlap score (distinct-matched-tokens divided by distinct-query-tokens), extracts a bounded excerpt of at most `RAG_RETRIEVAL_EXCERPT_WINDOW_CHARS = 240` characters centered on the first query-token match (newlines collapsed to spaces for a single-line-scannable reviewer read), and returns at most `RAG_RETRIEVAL_TOP_K_CAP = 5` excerpts. Every returned excerpt is tagged `provenance_tag="[rag-advisory]"` per the Phase 10O advisory-vs-canonical mirror rule and NEVER substitutes for canonical artifact content. The retrieval library REFUSES fail-closed via a returned `refusal_reason` field (drawn from the closed `RAG_RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_REASONS` vocabulary: `approval_mode_strict`, `operator_identity_missing`, `operator_acknowledgement_missing`, `query_empty`, `query_too_long`, `source_path_missing`, `source_path_outside_controller_root`, `runtime_not_available`) when: the query is empty / not a string / longer than `RAG_RETRIEVAL_QUERY_CHAR_CAP = 512` characters; the operator identity is missing; no source id has been acknowledged this session; the controller `loop-state.json.approval_mode` is `strict`; a selected source's path resolves OUTSIDE the controller root; or every acknowledged source is missing on disk (the fix cycle aligned this branch with the shipped enum: the runtime now emits `source_path_missing` on the "every acknowledged source is absent" refusal path so the closed refusal-reason vocabulary matches the actual runtime exactly and no enum member is dead). The controller-local scope check uses `_desktop_rag_retrieval_source_path_inside_controller_root(...)` which resolves both paths and checks the parent relationship - this preserves the controller-local scope boundary even if a future Phase 10V registry entry passes the descriptor validator via a symlink pointing outside the repo. The `_desktop_rag_retrieval_compute_approval_state(...)` helper returns a per-requirement `{satisfied, reason}` dict spanning eight closed requirements (`operator_acknowledged_advisory_labeling`, `operator_supplied_identity`, `approval_mode_supports_retrieval` [review / autonomous; strict mode refuses fail-closed per Phase 5C], `phase_10w_runtime_available`, `controller_local_scope_only`, `operator_query_supplied`, `query_within_char_cap`, `per_source_within_byte_cap`) so the renderer can show exactly which requirements remain unmet without collapsing them into a single boolean. The fix cycle added `operator_query_supplied` as a distinct closed approval requirement so the eligibility surface faithfully models the shipped runtime's `query_empty` refusal branch (a source is NEVER marked `retrieval_eligible=True` while the operator query is empty, and the paired `build_desktop_rag_retrieval_controls(...)` button descriptor therefore stays `enabled=False`). The view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `rag_retrieval_controls_view` sub-view alongside the existing nine sub-views, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders a `RAG Retrieval Controls (Phase 10W)` section that reuses `render_desktop_rag_retrieval_controls_text(...)` verbatim - the per-source lines surface provenance, advisory-labeling rule, freshness (state + last_modified UTC + size), each per-requirement approval state, and the derived `retrieval_eligible` boolean on its own indented line with explicit `[rag-source]` / `[rag-retrieval]` / `[rag-provenance]` / `[rag-freshness]` / `[rag-advisory]` / `[rag-approval]` / `[refused]` tags so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10W slice wires a sixth Tkinter panel beneath the existing Phase 10Q + Phase 10R + Phase 10T + Phase 10U + Phase 10V control panels inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: a per-session `Retrieval query` `tk.Entry` and a `Top-K` `tk.Entry` (both per-session `tk.StringVar` only; closing the window discards every value), plus a per-source `tk.Button` rendered via the new `build_desktop_rag_retrieval_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function that clicks-to-clipboard a fully-prefilled `python scripts/agent_loop.py run-local-rag-retrieval --controller-root <PATH> --operator-identity <NAME> --acknowledge-source <ID> --query "<QUERY>" --top-k <K>` command carrying the operator's live query + top-K values. The operator identity Entry + Phase 10V per-source acknowledgement Checkbuttons are reused from the Phase 10T / 10V widgets so the operator only types the identity once per session. Each `_refresh()` poll reads the live `StringVar` / `BooleanVar` state (including the `Retrieval query` `tk.StringVar` whose value is threaded into `operator_inputs["query"]`), calls `build_desktop_rag_retrieval_controls_view(controller_root, operator_inputs=...)` with the per-session inputs, re-derives `retrieval_eligible` per source (which now folds in `operator_query_supplied` and `query_within_char_cap`), and re-renders the per-source retrieval buttons so eligibility transitions - including live keystrokes into the query Entry - propagate to button `state="disabled"` / `"normal"` between polls. Per the fix cycle contract, a source retrieval button MUST NOT surface as enabled while the shipped runtime would refuse fail-closed with `query_empty` or `query_too_long`. Two new CLI subcommands surface the Phase 10W runtime through Phase 7C reporter-pattern handlers: `view-desktop-rag-retrieval-controls` (READ-ONLY controls view) and `run-local-rag-retrieval` (actual bounded retrieval driver). Both handlers require `--controller-root <PATH>` per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow (omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-rag-retrieval-controls]` / `[local-rag-retrieval] REFUSED: ...` stderr line); both always exit 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds. The retrieval CLI accepts `--operator-identity <NAME>`, `--acknowledge-source <ID>` (repeatable), `--query "<TEXT>"` (bounded to 512 chars), and `--top-k <N>` (defaults to 3; capped at 5). Per the Phase 10O advisory-vs-canonical mirror rule, the Phase 10S source-of-truth preservation rule, and the Phase 10V no-canonical-substitution rule these handlers NEVER mutate any canonical artifact (a dedicated regression test snapshots `loop-state.json` before / after and asserts byte-for-byte equality), NEVER append to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` (a dedicated regression test asserts the log file does not appear), NEVER advance loop-state, NEVER invoke `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawn a subprocess (a dedicated regression test patches all six Python entry points and asserts zero calls during a retrieval), NEVER open a network socket (a dedicated regression test patches `socket.socket` and asserts zero opens during a retrieval), NEVER contact a remote index / vector database / hosted retrieval service, NEVER persist a retrieval cache or hit log on disk (a dedicated regression test snapshots the controller root's directory listing before / after and asserts byte-for-byte equality), NEVER subscribe to a background watcher, NEVER auto-fill any operator-identity field, NEVER widen the Phase 10I three-control library-callable cap (a dedicated regression test anchors the cap exactly at `view-external-status` / `view-external-controls` / `inspect-external-target`), and NEVER let retrieved excerpts substitute for canonical artifact content (each excerpt is tagged `[rag-advisory]`). This slice does NOT introduce: any remote / vector database / hosted retrieval service / network-backed index (retrieval is CONTROLLER-LOCAL ONLY); any GitHub integration, packaging / code-signing / auto-update pipeline, system-tray surface, or controlled-concurrency runtime; any hidden daemon, crawler, or watcher farm outside the shipped Python runtime; automatic next-phase activation; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, the Phase 10O MCP integration contract, the Phase 10P desktop setup runtime, the Phase 10Q run-profiles runtime, the Phase 10R project-start runtime, the Phase 10S MCP server selection UX contract, the Phase 10T MCP read-only assistance surface, the Phase 10U MCP action guardrails surface, or the Phase 10V RAG source selection contract; or any Git automation.

Phase 10O is a documentation-first contract slice. Its job is to define how future MCP server support, scoped tool categories, browser/app inspection hooks, and tool-policy rules may assist planning, implementation, and review without bypassing canonical artifacts, evidence review, approval gates, or existing ownership boundaries. No MCP runtime integration ships in this slice; the contract is the prerequisite for later Phase 10T/10U implementation work.

Phase 10I (Minimal External UI Run/Resume Controls, complete) builds on the shipped Phase 10H read-only external UI status surface and ships a bounded eligibility-aware control affordance layer on top of it. The new `build_external_ui_control_view(controller_root: Path) -> dict` library function returns a structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10i-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, the current canonical `loop-state.status`, a closed `controls` list assembled from the new `_EXTERNAL_UI_CONTROL_REGISTRY` (8 entries covering the prompt's run/resume/inspect surface), and the literal `EXTERNAL_UI_CONTROL_DELEGATION_NOTE` reminder. Each control descriptor carries `id` / `label` / `command` / `dispatch_mode` (`library_call` for read-only library delegation, `copy_paste` for the Phase 10G CLI-only mutating commands) / `category` (`read_only` / `mutating`) / `delegated_library_function` (when applicable) / `currently_eligible` (derived from the canonical loop-state status against the control's `eligibility_states` frozenset) / `eligibility_reason` (one-line human-readable explanation). The new `view-external-controls` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_external_controls(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0; never mutates any canonical artifact; never writes to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`; never advances loop-state; never dispatches a CLI subprocess. The companion `invoke-external-control --control <ID>` CLI subcommand delegates a SINGLE read-only library-callable control (`view-external-status`, `view-external-controls`, `inspect-external-target`) via a direct library-function call (no CLI subprocess is spawned). Per the Phase 10G UI contract this surface NEVER writes `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, NEVER mutates `.agent-loop/loop-state.json`, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, and NEVER persists a canonical halt status. Success is surfaced through two `[external-ui]` stdout lines reporting the delegated library function name and the top-level keys of its return value; refusal raises a dedicated `ExternalUiControlRefusal` (NOT a `HaltError`, by design) which the CLI handler surfaces as a single `[external-ui] REFUSED: ...` stderr line plus exit code 2. Refusal fires when the requested control id is unknown or when its `dispatch_mode != 'library_call'` (mutating controls and copy-paste-only read-only controls MUST be operator-CLI-invoked per the Phase 10G "MUST NOT issue, dispatch, proxy" boundary) - the refusal message includes the shipped copy-paste invocation so the operator sees both why the refusal fired and the exact command to run manually. Mutating controls (`run`, `resume`, `auto-continue`) continue to be eligible-tagged copy-paste affordances; the eligibility hint is advisory only and does NOT bypass any shipped CLI subcommand's own preconditions. This slice does NOT enable: an artifact dashboard / analytics / diff viewer / history explorer (deferred to Phase 10J/10K); a native desktop-app shell or desktop-side action bridge (Phase 10L/10M/10N); controlled-concurrency or concurrent Codex/Claude overlap execution (Phase 10AB/10AC/10AD); MCP integration, RAG layer, GitHub integration, or model-policy extensibility; automatic next-phase activation behavior that bypasses the shipped Phase 4 planner / activator separation; any regression of the shipped Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; or any Git automation. Configuring a Fix-Phase-A-style local Claude/Codex wrapper continues to enable automatic local invocation within the existing phase loop; the Phase 10I controls layer is a read-side companion that surfaces which CLI subcommands are sensible to run right now, not a new control plane.

Phase 10J (Artifact Dashboard Contract, complete) defines, in documentation form only, the contract a future external artifact dashboard runtime must satisfy. The contract lives at [docs/artifact-dashboard-contract.md](docs/artifact-dashboard-contract.md) and pins six required dashboard surfaces (review summaries, diff views, progress history, approval actions, token/cost reporting, failure analytics), each with an explicit canonical-mirror-vs-advisory-derived-state breakdown and an enumeration of the on-disk source artifacts the dashboard MAY consume. The contract is layered on top of the binding Phase 10G UI contract: every read-set / advisory-mirror / CLI-only / no-auto-fill / refusal / source-of-truth / safety / approval / audit rule the Phase 10G contract pins continues to apply to the dashboard verbatim, and the dashboard MAY reuse the shipped Phase 10H `build_external_ui_status_view(...)` view model and the shipped Phase 10I `_EXTERNAL_UI_CONTROL_REGISTRY` + `invoke_external_ui_control(...)` library-call delegation surface without introducing additional library-callable controls. Mutating CLI subcommands (`attach-external-target`, `detach-external-target`, `verify-external-target`, `plan`, `activate`, `run`, `resume`, `auto-continue`, `record-final-acceptance`, and the full Phase 10G enumeration) remain copy-paste-only from the dashboard, and the contract explicitly forbids dashboard-side databases / session stores / event queues / identity tokens / approval workflows / cost-budget enforcement / external metrics pipelines that would compete with the canonical-artifact-first model. The contract distinguishes the FIRST shipped runtime slice (Phase 10K Artifact Dashboard Runtime Initial Slice) from later advanced capabilities (Phase 10L/10M/10N desktop-app shell work, then Phase 10AB/10AC/10AD concurrency / activity-stream refinements) so Phase 10K can implement against this contract without a further design pass. Phase 10J itself ships no dashboard runtime, no analytics backend, no diff viewer, no history explorer, no approval-action runtime, no new CLI subcommand, no new library function, no new halt status, no Git automation, and no rewrites of `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` / `ROADMAP.md` / the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts. The shipped Phase 1 - 9 runtime, the Phase 10A - 10F external-workspace runtime, the Phase 10H read-only status surface, the Phase 10I bounded run/resume controls layer, and the Fix Phase A local adapter contract are all preserved unchanged.

Phase 10R (Desktop App PRD Intake And Project Start Flow, complete) ships the first runtime that begins to satisfy the Phase 10L Desktop App Shell Contract's project-start surface. The new `build_desktop_project_start_view(controller_root: Path) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator and the shipped Phase 10F `inspect_external_target_attach(...)` aggregator (HaltError from either is soft-failed to `loop_state=None` / `attached=False` per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule), and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10r-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, `current_loop_state_status`, `attached`, `attach_record_refused` (True iff the attach record file is on disk but the Phase 10F inspector refused fail-closed because the JSON is unreadable / malformed / not a JSON object; added by the Phase 10R fix cycle so the surface no longer soft-fails a malformed attach record to `attached=False` and falsely re-advertises the attach path), `has_active_phase` (True iff the ATTACHED target's own `.agent-loop/loop-state.json` carries a non-empty `phase` / `sub_phase` / `task` triple; the Phase 10R fix cycle routes this gate input to the TARGET project's loop-state instead of the controller's own active-phase bookkeeping so the controller being mid-phase no longer locks out a fresh attached target), `controller_has_active_phase` (True iff the controller's own loop-state carries a non-empty `phase` / `sub_phase` / `task` triple; kept for audit visibility only and NOT a gate input), a closed `mirror` dict over the project-start dimensions enumerated by `DESKTOP_PROJECT_START_DIMENSION_IDS` (`target_attach` mirroring the Phase 10F inspector report; `target_loop_state` mirroring the attached target's resolved canonical path, the target's own loop-state.status, the target-side `has_active_phase` advisory, and any soft-failed error reason from the target-side `load_loop_state(...)` delegate; `task_md` / `prd_intake` / `proposed_phase` / `claude_prompt` / `current_task` / `current_phase` mirroring artifact presence + byte size via the closed `_desktop_project_start_artifact_mirror(...)` helper; `loop_state` mirroring the CONTROLLER's loop-state.phase / sub_phase / task / status / approval_mode plus the computed `controller_has_active_phase` advisory), an `affordances` list assembled from the closed `_DESKTOP_PROJECT_START_AFFORDANCES` registry (exactly six entries matching `DESKTOP_PROJECT_START_AFFORDANCE_IDS`), and the literal `DESKTOP_PROJECT_START_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. Each affordance carries the same structured `steps` tuple shape introduced by the Phase 10Q fix cycle (drawn from `DESKTOP_RUN_PROFILE_STEP_TYPES = ("cli", "manual_edit")`) so a multi-step recipe (intake-prd preparation, plan -> manual proposal edit -> activate) cannot be mistaken for a single runnable shell command. The six affordances are: `attach_or_select_target_project` (single-step `[cli] attach-external-target --target-path <PATH> --attached-by <NAME> --approval-mode <MODE>`; eligible only when no target is currently attached AND the attach record is not in a fail-closed refused state); `detach_target_project` (single-step `[cli] detach-external-target --detached-by <NAME>`; eligible only when a target IS currently attached AND the attach record is not in a fail-closed refused state); `intake_prd` (two-step recipe `[manual_edit] place PRD JSON at <PATH>` -> `[cli] intake-prd --input <PATH>`; writes ONLY the advisory `.agent-loop/prd-intake.json` artifact per the Phase 9B canonical-precedence-note - no canonical phase-activation artifact write); `bootstrap_prompt` (single-step `[cli] bootstrap-prompt`; writes `.agent-loop/claude-prompt.md` per the Phase 5F bootstrap contract so the operator does not have to hand-prepare the prompt artifact); `start_first_phase_via_plan_activate` (three-step `[cli] plan` -> `[manual_edit] set proposed-phase.md phase / sub_phase / task / max_cycles / approval_mode + paste the PRD-derived task body` -> `[cli] activate`; the Phase 10R fix cycle now requires a target to be attached (`requires_attached=True`) AND gates on the TARGET project's own `.agent-loop/loop-state.json` having NO active phase / sub_phase / task triple so the affordance is the FIRST-phase entry for the target rather than the controller; this means a controller mid-phase (e.g. on Phase 10R itself) no longer locks out the affordance when the operator has just attached a fresh target); `start_run` (single-step `[cli] run`; eligible only at `awaiting_claude_implementation`). Eligibility is computed per-call by `_desktop_project_start_eligibility(spec, status_value, attached, target_has_active_phase, attach_record_refused)` which combines four inputs (the closed `eligibility_states` frozenset, the `requires_attached` True/False/None tri-state, the TARGET-side active-phase-presence test for `start_first_phase_via_plan_activate`, and the new `attach_record_refused` short-circuit that fails BOTH attach and detach affordances closed when the attach record file is on disk but unreadable so neither canonical CLI is reachable); attach-vs-detach affordances surface mutually-exclusive eligibility so the operator sees exactly one viable path at any attach state. Every affordance carries `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` and `category='mutating'`; ZERO library-callable controls are introduced (the Phase 10I three-control cap is preserved with a dedicated regression test that anchors the cap exactly). The project-start view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `project_start_view` sub-view alongside the existing `status_view` / `controls_view` / `dashboard_view` / `setup_view` / `run_profiles_view`, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders a `Project Start (Phase 10R)` section that reuses `render_desktop_project_start_text(...)` verbatim - the per-affordance lines surface each step on its own indented line with explicit `[cli]` / `[manual-edit]` tags and an announced step count so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10R slice wires a real Phase 10R Tkinter button row beneath the existing Phase 10Q control panel inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: each affordance gets a real `tk.Button` rendered via the new `build_desktop_project_start_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function (each control descriptor carries `id` / `label` / `enabled` (mirrors `currently_eligible`) / `eligibility_reason` / `clipboard_payload` / `audit_note` / `dispatch_mode` / `category` / `requires_attached`), and a click on a button is a PURE clipboard-copy operation that NEVER spawns a subprocess and NEVER mutates a canonical artifact (the Tk shell shares the Phase 10Q `_copy_to_clipboard(...)` helper and the same non-canonical status-caption widget). The two Tk button rows (Phase 10Q + Phase 10R) are re-rendered every poll cycle from the latest view so eligibility transitions driven by canonical loop-state.status / attach-state / active-phase-presence changes propagate to button `state="disabled"` / `"normal"` between polls. The new `view-desktop-project-start` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_project_start(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds; `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow (matching the Phase 10M / 10N / 10P / 10Q contracts) and omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-project-start] REFUSED: ...` stderr line. Per the Phase 10L contract this handler NEVER mutates any canonical artifact, NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, NEVER advances loop-state, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawns a subprocess (a dedicated regression test patches all six Python entry points and asserts zero calls during a render), NEVER auto-runs `attach-external-target` / `detach-external-target` / `intake-prd` / `bootstrap-prompt` / `plan` / `activate` / `run` on the operator's behalf, NEVER auto-fills any `--*-by` operator-identity argument or `--approval-mode` value, NEVER rewrites `runtime-config.json` / `external-target.json` / `TASK.md` / `proposed-phase.md` / `claude-prompt.md` / `current-task.md` / `current-phase.md` / `prd-intake.json` (a dedicated regression test asserts the absence of every project-start artifact pre and post build), NEVER opens a network socket (a dedicated regression test patches `socket.socket` and asserts zero opens during a render), NEVER widens the Phase 10I library-callable control surface beyond its three-control cap, and NEVER introduces a hidden project-start state plane (no per-operator project recents list, no UI-only PRD store, no parallel intake queue). This slice does NOT introduce: MCP server selection / read-only MCP runtime (Phase 10S / 10T); MCP mutation-capable runtime (Phase 10U+); packaging, code-signing, auto-update pipelines, or native system-tray reminders (later Phase 10 sub-phases tracked in `ROADMAP.md`); multi-target desktop sessions or controlled-concurrency / overlap-safe detection / Codex-owned concurrent work (Phase 10AB / 10AC / 10AD); any claim that fully autonomous PRD-to-product execution ships (the affordance audit notes explicitly disclaim this); any persisted control plane parallel to the shipped `loop-state.json` / `runtime-config.json` / `external-target.json` / `proposed-phase.md` / `prd-intake.json` and the canonical Phase 4C activation path; automatic next-phase activation; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, the Phase 10O MCP integration contract, the Phase 10P desktop setup runtime, or the Phase 10Q run-profiles runtime; or any Git automation.

Phase 10Q (Desktop App Run Profiles And Approval Controls, complete) ships the first runtime that begins to satisfy the Phase 10L Desktop App Shell Contract's run-profile and approval-controls surface. The new `build_desktop_run_profiles_view(controller_root: Path) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator and the shipped Phase 6N `read_runtime_config(...)` validator (HaltError from either is soft-failed to `loop_state=None` / `runtime_adapter=RUNTIME_ADAPTER_DEFAULT` per the Phase 10L "MUST NOT propagate validator HaltErrors as canonical halt persistence" rule), and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10q-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, `current_loop_state_status`, a closed `mirror` dict over the run-profile dimensions enumerated by `DESKTOP_RUN_PROFILE_DIMENSION_IDS` (`approval_mode` mirroring loop-state.approval_mode plus the closed `ALLOWED_APPROVAL_MODES` allowed-values set; `max_cycles` / `cycle_count` mirroring the integer fields; `phase` / `sub_phase` / `task` / `status` mirroring the loop-state strings; `runtime_adapter` mirroring the persisted Phase 6N selection with `runtime_adapter_default` and `runtime_adapter_supported` advisories), an `affordances` list assembled from the closed `_DESKTOP_RUN_PROFILE_AFFORDANCES` registry (exactly six entries matching `DESKTOP_RUN_PROFILE_AFFORDANCE_IDS`), and the literal `DESKTOP_RUN_PROFILES_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. The Phase 10Q fix cycle replaced the single misleading `command` string on each affordance with a closed structured `steps` tuple drawn from `DESKTOP_RUN_PROFILE_STEP_TYPES = ("cli", "manual_edit")` so a multi-step recipe (plan -> manual proposal edit -> activate) cannot be mistaken for a single runnable shell command: each `select_approval_mode_review/strict/autonomous` affordance now carries a three-step recipe (`[cli] python scripts/agent_loop.py plan` -> `[manual_edit] in .agent-loop/proposed-phase.md, set the next-phase approval_mode line to review|strict|autonomous` -> `[cli] python scripts/agent_loop.py activate`) with the literal target value baked into the manual-edit step content, so a copy-paste is genuinely actionable instead of relying on an inline `#` shell comment to hide the edit; the `set_attach_approval_mode` affordance keeps its single-step `[cli] python scripts/agent_loop.py attach-external-target --target-path <PATH> --attached-by <NAME> --approval-mode <MODE>` recipe (with a dedicated regression test that feeds the placeholder-substituted CLI step through the real `build_parser()` and asserts the parse succeeds); `select_run_policy_bounded` is a single-step `[cli] python scripts/agent_loop.py run` recipe eligible only at `awaiting_claude_implementation`; `select_run_policy_prd_to_completion` is a single-step `[cli] python scripts/agent_loop.py auto-continue` recipe eligible only at `HALTED_TOKEN_EXHAUSTION`. Each affordance descriptor also carries a derived `command` summary (each step tagged with `[cli]` / `[manual-edit]` and joined with ` && `, intentionally NOT runnable as-is so a single-line scan still sees every step and its kind) and a multi-line `clipboard_payload` ready for desktop-side widget binding. Every affordance carries `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` and `category='mutating'`; ZERO library-callable controls are introduced (the Phase 10I three-control cap is preserved with a dedicated regression test that anchors the cap exactly). The run-profiles view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface in two complementary ways. First, `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `run_profiles_view` sub-view alongside the existing `status_view` / `controls_view` / `dashboard_view` / `setup_view`, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders a `Run Profiles (Phase 10Q)` section that reuses `render_desktop_run_profiles_text(...)` verbatim - the per-affordance lines now surface each step on its own indented line with an explicit `[cli]` / `[manual-edit]` tag and an announced step count (e.g. `steps (3 steps; copy-paste each in order):`) so the rendered text and the standalone CLI reporter agree on attribution. Second, the Phase 10Q fix cycle wires a real Phase 10Q Tkinter button row to the right of the existing `ScrolledText` widget inside `_launch_desktop_app_window(...)`: each affordance gets a real `tk.Button` rendered via the new `build_desktop_run_profile_controls(view: dict) -> list[dict]` library function (each control descriptor carries `id` / `label` / `dimension` / `target_value` / `enabled` (mirrors `currently_eligible`; the desktop shell MUST render the widget as `state="disabled"` when False) / `eligibility_reason` / `clipboard_payload` / `audit_note` / `dispatch_mode` / `category`), and a click on a button is a PURE clipboard-copy operation (`root.clipboard_clear()` + `root.clipboard_append(payload)` plus a non-canonical status caption widget update) that NEVER spawns a subprocess, NEVER evaluates the payload as shell, NEVER writes the payload to a persisted artifact, and NEVER widens the Phase 10I library-callable control cap. The Tk button row is re-rendered every poll cycle from the latest view so eligibility transitions (driven by canonical `loop-state.status` changes) propagate to button `state="disabled"` / `"normal"` between polls. The new `view-desktop-run-profiles` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_run_profiles(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds; `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow (matching the Phase 10M / 10N / 10P contracts) and omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-run-profiles] REFUSED: ...` stderr line that names the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow and enumerates the four forbidden default-root sources; supplying a folder missing any of the `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` / `TASK.md` / `.agent-loop/` markers also returns exit 2 with an explicit refusal naming the missing markers. Rendered output is tagged (`[canonical mirror]` attributed to `.agent-loop/loop-state.json` for the run-profile mirror dimensions, `[advisory]` for the persisted Phase 6N runtime-config mirror and the per-affordance eligibility_reason / audit_note advisories, `[affordance]` for eligible affordances, `[ineligible]` for refused affordances, plus the per-step `[cli]` / `[manual-edit]` tags within each affordance recipe) so a reviewer can tell which lines are canonical, which are operator-visible affordances, which affordances refused fail-closed, and which sub-steps are runnable shell vs human-supplied file edits. Per the Phase 10L contract this handler NEVER mutates any canonical artifact, NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, NEVER advances loop-state (the surface NEVER silently mutates `approval_mode` or any other canonical loop-state field outside the existing Phase 4C activation path and the existing `attach-external-target --approval-mode` write path), NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawns a subprocess (no `subprocess.Popen` / `subprocess.run` / `subprocess.call` / `subprocess.check_call` / `subprocess.check_output` / `os.system`; a dedicated regression test patches all six and asserts zero calls during a render), NEVER auto-runs `plan` / `activate` / `run` / `auto-continue` / `attach-external-target` on the operator's behalf, NEVER auto-fills any `--*-by` operator-identity argument, NEVER rewrites `runtime-config.json` / `external-target.json`, NEVER opens a network socket (a dedicated regression test patches `socket.socket` and asserts zero opens during a render), and NEVER widens the Phase 10I library-callable control surface beyond its three-control cap. This slice does NOT introduce: a guided PRD intake or project-start flow (Phase 10R); MCP server selection / read-only MCP runtime (Phase 10S / 10T); MCP mutation-capable runtime (Phase 10U+); packaging, code-signing, auto-update pipelines, or native system-tray reminders (later Phase 10 sub-phases tracked in `ROADMAP.md`); multi-target desktop sessions or controlled-concurrency / overlap-safe detection / Codex-owned concurrent work (Phase 10AB / 10AC / 10AD); any persisted control plane parallel to `loop-state.json` / `runtime-config.json` / `external-target.json` and the operator's own shell environment; automatic next-phase activation; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, the Phase 10O MCP integration contract, or the Phase 10P desktop setup runtime; or any Git automation.

Phase 10P (Desktop App Operator Setup And CLI Onboarding, complete) ships the first runtime that begins to satisfy the Phase 10L Desktop App Shell Contract's onboarding-side. The new `build_desktop_setup_view(controller_root: Path, *, environ: Optional[dict] = None) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped Phase 10L controller-root validator (`validate_desktop_controller_root(...)`), the shipped Phase 10F external-target inspector (`inspect_external_target_attach(...)`), and the shipped Phase 6N runtime-config validator (`read_runtime_config(...)`), and returns a per-call structured view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10p-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, six closed sections (`controller_root`, `target`, `adapter_env`, `runtime_config`, `wrapper_templates`, `local_tools`), and the literal `DESKTOP_SETUP_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. The setup view is integrated into the shipped Phase 10M desktop app surface: `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` adds a `setup_view` sub-view alongside the existing `status_view` / `controls_view` / `dashboard_view`, and `render_desktop_app_text(...)` renders a `Setup (Phase 10P)` section that reuses `render_desktop_setup_text(...)` verbatim so the standalone CLI reporter and the desktop window render the same attribution tags. Each section carries an explicit `status` field from a closed set (`ok` / `missing` / `default` / `stale` / `refused`) plus a human-readable `summary`; the `adapter_env` section reports `AGENT_LOOP_CLAUDE_CMD` / `AGENT_LOOP_CODEX_CMD` and the optional `AGENT_LOOP_CLAUDE_MODEL` / `AGENT_LOOP_CODEX_MODEL` identifiers from the Fix Phase A local adapter contract as a read-only snapshot of `os.environ` (the setup view NEVER mutates `os.environ`, NEVER writes a shell rc file, NEVER persists adapter commands to a hidden config plane), and now applies bounded structural validation via `_desktop_setup_validate_adapter_command(value)` (a pure `shlex.split` + `shutil.which` check, no subprocess spawn / shell evaluation / network IO / persisted-config write) so a command env var that is set but cannot realistically be invoked surfaces as `refused` rather than `ok`: an empty / non-string / whitespace-only value, an unparseable shell-quote string (e.g. `unterminated "quote`), or a first shell token that does not resolve via `shutil.which(...)` on PATH each surface fail-closed with an explicit `validation_reason`, and the overall `adapter_env` status is `refused` if either adapter is set-but-unresolvable, `missing` if either adapter is unset, and `ok` only when both adapter command env vars parse and resolve. The `wrapper_templates` section reports the presence of `scripts/claude-wrapper.sh.template` and `scripts/codex-wrapper.sh.template` so a fresh-clone operator can find the Fix Phase A starting points; the `local_tools` section reports required-local-tool resolvability on `PATH` via `shutil.which(...)` (Python and git in this initial slice; no version-string parsing of stdout from a spawned subprocess). The new `view-desktop-setup` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_setup(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content once the controller-root selection succeeds; `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow (matching the Phase 10M `launch-desktop-app` and Phase 10N `view-desktop-actions` contracts) and omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-setup] REFUSED: ...` stderr line that names the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow and enumerates the four forbidden default-root sources (auto-discovered repo root, OS-level current working directory, environment variable, packaging-time configured path); supplying a folder missing any of the `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` / `TASK.md` / `.agent-loop/` markers also returns exit 2 with an explicit refusal naming the missing markers. Rendered output is tagged (`[setup]` for `ok`, `[default]` for runtime-config-absent, `[stale]` for attached-but-stale target, `[missing]` for unset adapters / absent templates / missing tools, `[refused]` for fail-closed sections including set-but-unresolvable adapter commands, `[advisory]` for derived details like resolved tool paths, the executable_token / resolved_path / validation_reason fields, and the Python version) so a reviewer can tell which lines come from canonical artifacts and which are computed setup-state. Per the Phase 10L contract this handler NEVER mutates any canonical artifact, NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, NEVER advances loop-state, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER spawns a subprocess (no `subprocess.Popen` / `subprocess.run` / `subprocess.call` / `subprocess.check_call` / `subprocess.check_output` / `os.system`; a dedicated regression test patches all six and asserts zero calls during a render, and a separate regression test patches the same set during adapter-command validation to anchor that `_desktop_setup_validate_adapter_command(...)` is a structural reachability check only), NEVER writes to or rewrites `runtime-config.json` / `external-target.json` / the wrapper templates / the operator's shell rc (`set-runtime-config` and `attach-external-target` remain the only mutating paths), NEVER auto-fills any `--*-by` operator-identity argument, NEVER opens a network socket (a dedicated regression test patches `socket.socket` and asserts zero opens during a render), and NEVER widens the Phase 10I library-callable control surface beyond its three-control cap (`view-external-status`, `view-external-controls`, `inspect-external-target`; a dedicated regression test anchors the cap exactly). This slice does NOT introduce: a guided PRD intake or project-start flow (deferred to Phase 10R Desktop App PRD Intake And Project Start Flow); run-profile or approval-mode selection UX (Phase 10Q Desktop App Run Profiles And Approval Controls); MCP server selection / read-only MCP runtime (Phase 10S / 10T); MCP mutation-capable runtime (Phase 10U+); packaging, code-signing, auto-update pipelines, or native system-tray reminders (later Phase 10 sub-phases tracked in `ROADMAP.md`); multi-target desktop sessions or controlled-concurrency / overlap-safe detection / Codex-owned concurrent work (Phase 10AB / 10AC / 10AD); any persisted config plane parallel to `runtime-config.json` and the operator's own shell environment; automatic next-phase activation; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, or the Phase 10O MCP integration contract; or any Git automation.

Phase 10O (MCP Integration Contract And Safe Tool Boundary, complete) defines, in documentation form only, the contract every future MCP-assisted runtime slice must satisfy before any MCP server consumer ships. The contract lives at [docs/mcp-integration-contract.md](docs/mcp-integration-contract.md) and pins: ZERO MCP runtime integration currently shipped (no MCP client, no MCP server adapter, no MCP descriptor registry, no MCP policy engine, no browser/app inspection runtime); three closed MCP tool categories the future runtime MUST classify every tool into (`read_only_advisory` for documentation lookups / library reference / schema descriptions / code-search; `browser_app_inspection` for explicitly-authorized per-session DOM / screenshot / accessibility-tree reads; `deferred_mutating` for any tool that could mutate canonical artifacts, target-side state, external services, or external infrastructure - this category is REFUSED unconditionally in the Phase 10T initial slice and deferred to Phase 10U+); a per-tool descriptor schema matching the Phase 10I control-registry shape (`id` / `category` / `dispatch_mode` (`library_call` or `operator_invoked`) / `delegated_phase_10i_control_id` / `mutation_eligible` (ALWAYS `false` in the Phase 10T initial slice) / `audit_note` / `refusal_reason_template`); the read-only tool boundary (advisory derived state tagged `[mcp-advisory]` with source MCP tool id attributed, per-poll-cycle cache invalidation, bounded per-tool response size cap, bounded per-cycle MCP-call count cap, canonical record on disk remains source of truth); the deferred-mutation tool boundary (unconditional fail-closed refusal in Phase 10T; no silent downgrade from `deferred_mutating` to `read_only_advisory` based on heuristics); the browser/app inspection hook safety boundary (explicit per-session operator authorization, no credential / cookie / session-token / OS-level-credential / environment-variable / SSH-key / API-key / clipboard-auto-refresh capture, no synthetic mouse / keystroke / form-fill injection, no OS-level event-stream subscription, no cross-session persistence, no silent CLI dispatch based on inspection findings); the policy-rule hook additive-only boundary (a policy rule MAY refuse a tool the base contract would allow but MUST NOT approve a tool the base contract refuses, MUST NOT widen the read-only / deferred-mutation boundary, MUST NOT widen the Phase 10I library-callable three-control cap, MUST NOT widen the no-auto-fill identity boundary, MUST NOT silently dispatch any CLI subcommand); the same advisory-vs-canonical mirror rule the Phase 10G / 10J / 10L contracts pin (MCP-fetched values are advisory derived state tagged `[mcp-advisory]`, never canonical mirrors); the full CLI-only operation enumeration (`attach-external-target`, `detach-external-target`, `verify-external-target`, `plan`, `activate`, `run`, `resume`, `auto-continue`, `record-final-acceptance`, and every other shipped mutating subcommand remain operator-CLI-invoked; the Phase 10I library-callable surface caps at the three shipped controls and the MCP runtime MUST NOT introduce a fourth); the same no-auto-fill operator-identity invariants Phase 10B `attached_by` / Phase 10C `bootstrapped_by` / Phase 9G `accepted_by` pin (no auto-fill from MCP-fetched values, MCP server session, browser session, `$USER` / `whoami`, packaging-time-configured identity, or code-signing-bound identity); the refusal cases (descriptor missing/malformed/unknown category, `deferred_mutating` category refused unconditionally, response size cap exceeded, attempt to set canonical loop-state field or write canonical artifact or supply `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` token or `--*-by` argument from MCP, per-cycle call count cap exceeded, policy-rule refusal, transport failure, TLS / authentication failure with no silent unauthenticated downgrade); source-of-truth preservation (no MCP-side database / notification queue / identity token / memory store separate from `.agent-loop/memory/`); the no-Git-automation boundary in BOTH roots; every shipped Phase 5 review / strict / autonomous approval-mode boundary; every shipped Phase 6 memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support-layer boundary; the audit expectation that a reviewer reading the controller's and target's `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` MUST be able to reconstruct what the operator did without needing access to any MCP-side log, event stream, session record, or telemetry feed; and explicit evidence-review preservation (MCP-fetched test / lint / typecheck / build output is NEVER the canonical record Codex reviews, MCP-fetched diff state is NEVER the canonical `git-diff.patch` / `git-status.log`, MCP-fetched "summary" or "review" text is NEVER `claude-summary.md` or `codex-review.md`, no modification to `scripts/run_checks.sh`, no silent prompt-injection without the `[mcp-advisory]` provenance tag). The contract distinguishes the FIRST shipped runtime slice (Phase 10T MCP Read-Only Assistance In Desktop App, covering category 1 and category 2 tools only) from later mutation-capable work (Phase 10U+ bounded mutation-capable MCP actions, plus the per-action approval gates, audit-log entry shape, closed eligibility enumeration, and rollback behavior each later slice must add) so Phase 10T can implement against this contract without a further design pass. Phase 10O itself ships no MCP client library, MCP server adapter, MCP runtime, MCP descriptor registry, MCP policy engine, browser inspection runtime, app inspection runtime, RAG layer, packaging / code-signing / auto-update pipeline, system-tray surface, controlled-concurrency runtime, new CLI subcommand, new library function, new halt status, new canonical artifact, or Git automation, and no rewrites of `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` / `ROADMAP.md` / the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts. The shipped Phase 1 - 9 runtime, the Phase 10A - 10F external-workspace runtime, the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, the Phase 10M desktop runtime, the Phase 10N desktop action bridge, and the Fix Phase A local adapter contract are all preserved unchanged.

Phase 10N (Desktop App Action Bridge Initial Slice, complete) extends the Phase 10M read-only desktop runtime with the first bounded operator-decision action bridge over four desktop verbs (`attach`, `inspect`, `run`, `resume`). The new `build_desktop_action_bridge_view(controller_root: Path) -> dict` library function returns a structured per-call view carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10n-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, the canonical `current_loop_state_status` (loaded via the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator; `HaltError` soft-fails to `None`), an `affordances` list assembled from the closed `_DESKTOP_ACTION_BRIDGE_AFFORDANCES` registry (exactly four entries matching `DESKTOP_ACTION_BRIDGE_AFFORDANCE_IDS`), and the literal `DESKTOP_ACTION_BRIDGE_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. Each affordance descriptor carries `id` / `label` / `command` / `dispatch_mode` (`copy_paste` for every mutating affordance, `library_call` only for `inspect` which delegates to the shipped Phase 10I `inspect-external-target` library-callable control) / `category` (`mutating` / `read_only`) / `delegated_library_function` / `delegated_phase_10i_control_id` / `currently_eligible` (derived from canonical `loop-state.status` against the affordance's `eligibility_states` frozenset: `run` is eligible only when `awaiting_claude_implementation`; `resume` is eligible only at the four Phase 5C strict-mode halt statuses; `attach` and `inspect` carry `eligibility_states=None` so they remain operator-decision regardless of loop-state) / `eligibility_reason` / `audit_note` (audit-log visibility the operator-CLI invocation will produce, so the operator can see at a glance whether running the affordance leaves a canonical audit trail). The `attach` affordance renders the shipped CLI command exactly as the parser requires (`attach-external-target --target-path <PATH> --attached-by <NAME> --approval-mode <MODE>`) with all three required arguments surfaced as placeholder text - no auto-fill of any `--*-by` operator-identity argument or `--approval-mode` mode-selection value is permitted, and the rendered command is genuinely copy-pasteable against the shipped parser (a dedicated regression test feeds the affordance command through the actual `build_parser()` with placeholders substituted and asserts the parse succeeds). The new `view-desktop-actions` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_desktop_actions(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content; never mutates any canonical artifact; never appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`; never advances loop-state; never invokes `_halt(...)`; NEVER spawns a subprocess (no `subprocess.Popen` / `subprocess.run` / `subprocess.call` / `subprocess.check_call` / `subprocess.check_output` / `os.system`; a dedicated regression test patches all six and asserts zero calls during a render); never widens the Phase 10I library-callable control surface beyond its three-control cap (a dedicated regression test anchors the cap exactly). `--controller-root <PATH>` is REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow (matching the Phase 10M `launch-desktop-app` contract); omitting it returns exit 2 with an explicit `[desktop-action-bridge] REFUSED: ...` stderr line that names the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow and enumerates the four forbidden default-root sources. Rendered output is attributed (`[canonical mirror]` for `current_loop_state_status`, `[affordance]` for eligible affordances, `[ineligible]` for refused affordances, `[advisory]` for `eligibility_reason` and `audit_note`) so a reviewer can tell which lines are canonical, which are operator-visible affordances, and which affordances refused fail-closed. This slice does NOT introduce: silent mutating subprocess dispatch; a new library-callable control (the Phase 10I three-control cap is preserved); packaging, code-signing, auto-update pipelines, or native system-tray reminders (later Phase 10 sub-phases tracked in `ROADMAP.md`); multi-target desktop sessions or controlled-concurrency / overlap-safe detection / Codex-owned concurrent work (Phase 10AB / 10AC / 10AD); MCP / RAG / GitHub / model-policy extensibility; automatic next-phase activation; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, the Phase 10L desktop-app contract, or the Phase 10M desktop runtime; or any Git automation.

Phase 10M (Desktop App Read-Only Runtime Initial Slice, complete) ships the first local desktop app that satisfies the Phase 10L Desktop App Shell Contract's read-side. The new `assemble_desktop_app_view(controller_root: Path) -> dict` library function delegates verbatim to the shipped Phase 10H status view (`build_external_ui_status_view(...)`), the shipped Phase 10I controls view (`build_external_ui_control_view(...)`), and the shipped Phase 10K artifact dashboard view (`build_artifact_dashboard_view(...)`) and returns a per-poll assembled dict carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10m-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, the three sub-view dicts unmodified, and the literal `DESKTOP_APP_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. A `_desktop_safe_call_view(...)` wrapper converts `HaltError` (and any other exception raised by a shipped view library function) into a `{error, view: None}` soft-error envelope so the desktop shell can render an error state per the Phase 10L refusal vocabulary without re-raising as canonical halt persistence. The new `launch-desktop-app` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_launch_desktop_app(args)`: it validates the operator-selected controller root (`--controller-root <PATH>`; REQUIRED per the Phase 10L Controller-Root Selection Flow - no auto-discovered repo-root fallback, no OS-level current working directory, no environment variable, no packaging-time configured path) against the `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` / `TASK.md` / `.agent-loop/` markers and refuses fail-closed with exit 2 and a `[desktop-app] REFUSED: ...` stderr line when any marker is missing; in `--headless` (or `--once`) mode it renders `render_desktop_app_text(view)` to stdout once and exits 0 (Phase 7C reporter pattern); in default GUI mode it lazy-imports Tkinter from the Python stdlib and opens a `_DESKTOP_APP_WINDOW_TITLE`-titled ScrolledText window that re-polls `assemble_desktop_app_view(...)` every `cadence_seconds` seconds (clamped by `_desktop_clamp_cadence(...)` to the Phase 10L floor: `_DESKTOP_APP_MIN_IDLE_CADENCE_SECONDS = 2` or `_DESKTOP_APP_MIN_OPERATOR_CADENCE_SECONDS = 1` under `--operator-driven`). On `ImportError` (Tk unavailable, e.g. a headless server) the handler refuses fail-closed with exit 2 and a stderr message pointing the operator at `--headless`. Each rendered output line is tagged `[canonical mirror]` (attributed to the source artifact path), `[advisory]` (computed), `[structural summary]` (top-level keys of the shipped sub-view dict), or `[error]` (soft-failure envelope from the safe-call wrapper) so a reviewer can tell which fields are canonical and which are derived. Per the Phase 10L contract this handler NEVER mutates any canonical artifact, NEVER appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, NEVER advances loop-state, NEVER invokes `_halt(...)`, NEVER dispatches a CLI subprocess, and NEVER widens the Phase 10I library-callable control surface beyond its three-control cap (`view-external-status`, `view-external-controls`, `inspect-external-target`); a dedicated regression test (`NonMutationInvariantsTests.test_does_not_introduce_new_library_callable_control`) anchors the cap exactly. This slice does NOT introduce: mutating desktop action dispatch (deferred to Phase 10N Desktop App Action Bridge Initial Slice); packaging, code-signing, auto-update pipelines, or native system-tray reminders (later Phase 10 sub-phases tracked in `ROADMAP.md`); multi-target desktop sessions or controlled-concurrency / overlap-safe detection / Codex-owned concurrent work (Phase 10AB / 10AC / 10AD); MCP / RAG / GitHub / model-policy extensibility; automatic next-phase activation; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, or the Phase 10L desktop-app contract; or any Git automation.

Phase 10L (Desktop App Shell Contract, complete) defines, in documentation form only, the contract a future native desktop-app shell over the shipped Python runtime must satisfy. The contract lives at [docs/desktop-app-contract.md](docs/desktop-app-contract.md) and pins: a local-only native window process (no remote server, no hosted SaaS endpoint, no shared multi-user backend, no background mutating daemon) with the toolkit choice (Electron / Tauri / Qt / native AppKit / Win32 / GTK / etc.) intentionally NOT pinned at this layer; explicit operator selection of the controller root via a native folder picker that validates `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` / `TASK.md` / `.agent-loop/` presence before any render and treats a root switch as a clean reset (no cross-merge between controller roots); attach visibility through the shipped Phase 10F inspector report and Phase 10H status view (target-side artifacts surface as canonical mirrors ONLY when an attach is present and fresh); bounded refresh / polling rules (at least 2 seconds between idle polls of the same view library function, at least 1 second under operator-driven refresh, per-poll-cycle cache invalidation required, optional `.agent-loop/` file-system watch permitted as a polling optimization but MUST NOT bypass the shipped view library functions, no long-poll / SSE / WebSocket fan-out); artifact-opening behavior via the operating system's default file-open mechanism (`xdg-open` / `open` / Windows shell association) with no bundled editor, no auto-staging, no Git side effects, and no silent open of Codex-owned planning artifacts; the bridge to the shipped Python orchestrator / view surfaces pinned at exactly two modes (a library-call bridge that hosts a Python sub-process invoking the shipped `build_external_ui_status_view(...)` / `build_external_ui_control_view(...)` / `build_artifact_dashboard_view(...)` / `invoke_external_ui_control(...)` / `inspect_external_target_attach(...)` functions verbatim, and a CLI-spawn bridge that copy-pastes mutating subcommands to the operator's own shell - no re-implementing the shipped library functions in a non-Python language, no custom IPC / named-pipe / socket bypass, no auto-fill of any `--*-by` operator-identity argument); the same advisory-vs-canonical mirror rule the Phase 10G UI contract and the Phase 10J dashboard contract pin (canonical mirrors attributed to source artifacts and refreshed per poll; advisory derived state explicitly tagged); the full CLI-only operation enumeration (`attach-external-target`, `detach-external-target`, `verify-external-target`, `plan`, `activate`, `run`, `resume`, `auto-continue`, `record-final-acceptance`, and every other shipped mutating subcommand remain operator-CLI-invoked; the Phase 10I library-callable surface caps at the three shipped controls and the desktop shell MUST NOT introduce a fourth); the same no-auto-fill operator-identity invariants Phase 10B `attached_by` / Phase 10C `bootstrapped_by` / Phase 9G `accepted_by` already pin (no auto-fill from `$USER` / `whoami` / browser session / packaging-time-configured identity / code-signing-bound identity); the refusal cases (operator-selected folder missing `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` / `TASK.md` / `.agent-loop/`, unreadable canonical artifact, unrecognized signal-version marker, controller-vs-target boundary violation, missing/malformed `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` token, HaltError raised by a shipped view library function, Python sub-process failure to import `agent_loop`); source-of-truth preservation (no shell-side database / notification queue / identity token / packaged memory store beyond a per-poll-cycle in-memory rendering cache); the no-Git-automation boundary in BOTH roots; and the audit expectation that a reviewer reading the controller's and target's `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` MUST be able to reconstruct what the operator did without needing access to any shell-side log, event stream, session record, or telemetry feed. The contract distinguishes the FIRST two shipped runtime slices (Phase 10M Desktop App Read-Only Runtime Initial Slice and Phase 10N Desktop App Action Bridge Initial Slice) from later advanced capabilities (Phase 10AB / 10AC / 10AD controlled-concurrency, overlap-safe detection, Codex-owned concurrent work; plus the multi-target desktop sessions, packaging / code-signing / auto-update pipelines, and native system-tray reminders tracked separately in `ROADMAP.md`) so Phase 10M and Phase 10N can each implement against this contract without a further design pass. Phase 10L itself ships no desktop runtime, no native window, no packaging step, no code-signing pipeline, no auto-update pipeline, no system-tray surface, no new CLI subcommand, no new library function, no new halt status, no Git automation, and no rewrites of `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` / `ROADMAP.md` / the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts.
