The shipped Phase 1 - 9 runtime, the Phase 10A - 10F external-workspace runtime, the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, the Phase 10I controls layer, the Phase 10J dashboard contract, the Phase 10K dashboard runtime, and the Fix Phase A local adapter contract are all preserved unchanged.

Phase 10K (Artifact Dashboard Initial Slice, complete) ships the first runtime that satisfies the Phase 10J Artifact Dashboard Contract. The new `build_artifact_dashboard_view(controller_root: Path) -> dict` library function assembles a structured per-call view model carrying `view_signal_version = "phase-10k-v1"`, `controller_path_canonical`, a `surfaces` dict keyed by the closed `EXTERNAL_DASHBOARD_SURFACE_IDS` tuple (the six required Phase 10J surfaces: `review_summaries`, `diff_views`, `progress_history`, `approval_actions`, `token_cost`, `failure_analytics`), and the literal `EXTERNAL_DASHBOARD_PRECEDENCE_NOTE` reminder. Each surface descriptor separates `canonical_mirrors` (per-artifact `path` / `present` / `byte_size` / `error` entries verbatim from the on-disk file, loop-state field mirrors where applicable, and bounded per-category memory-entry enumerations - Phase 6I distillation summary/decision/failure entries on Progress History, Phase 6F token-exhaustion checkpoints + the Phase 6G/6H continuation context on Token / Cost Reporting, Phase 6L repeated-failure synthesis entries + the loop-state `last_verdict` / `status` failure context on Failure Analytics) from `advisory` derived state (computed values like `current_verdict` from the latest codex-review, `added_line_count` / `removed_line_count` from the diff tail, `cycle_progress_hint`, `approaching_cycle_cap`, `phase_plan_section_headers_tail`, `approved_for_activation_token_present`, `issue_count_in_latest_review`, `evidence_files_with_content`, `distillation_entry_total`, `token_exhaustion_checkpoint_count`, `repeated_failure_synthesis_count`, `loop_state_last_verdict`, `loop_state_status`). Approval-actions affordances are rendered inline as three copy-paste-only command cards (`plan`, `activate`, `record-final-acceptance --accepted-by <NAME>`), each tagged `dispatch_mode='copy_paste'` and `category='mutating'`, deliberately NOT widening the Phase 10I library-callable surface beyond its three-control cap. The new `view-artifact-dashboard` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_artifact_dashboard(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0; never mutates any canonical artifact; never appends to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`; never advances loop-state; never invokes `_halt(...)`; never dispatches a CLI subprocess; reuses the Phase 10I control registry classification without adding any new library-callable control id. Each rendered output line is either `[canonical mirror]`-tagged with the source artifact path or `[advisory]`-tagged so a reviewer can tell which fields are canonical and which are computed. Missing artifacts surface as `present=False` advisory state; unreadable artifacts surface as `error=<str>` (preserving the Phase 10J refusal contract's missing-vs-unreadable distinction without raising). This slice does NOT introduce: an analytics backend, a dashboard-side database / session store / event queue / identity token / approval workflow / cost-budget enforcement / external metrics pipeline; a native desktop-app shell or action bridge (deferred to Phase 10L/10M/10N); controlled-concurrency / overlap-safe detection / concurrent Codex/Claude execution (deferred to Phase 10AB/10AC/10AD); MCP / RAG / GitHub / model-policy extensibility; automatic next-phase activation; any regression of the Phase 5/6/9 review / strict-mode / autonomous / memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support semantics; any change to the Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contracts; any change to the Phase 10G UI contract, the Phase 10H status surface, or the Phase 10I controls layer; or any Git automation.

Fix Phase A (Automatic Local Claude/Codex Invocation Reliability, complete) is the targeted remediation slice that closed the gap between the shipped subprocess adapter seams and reliable real-world automatic Claude/Codex invocation on a configured local machine. The slice shipped three deliverables. First, a concrete operator-facing adapter contract at [docs/local-adapter-contract.md](docs/local-adapter-contract.md) pinning the cwd / stdin / artifact-write obligations of `AGENT_LOOP_CLAUDE_CMD` and `AGENT_LOOP_CODEX_CMD`, the success vs failure semantics the orchestrator computes per invocation, the explicit list of orchestrator-owned artifacts the wrapper MUST NOT mutate, the no-stdout-parsing rule preserving `.agent-loop/claude-summary.md` and `.agent-loop/codex-review.md` as the canonical source of truth, and an explicit "Distinction From Fully Autonomous Mode" section so an operator running an automatically-driven cycle does not conflate it with the still-deferred fully autonomous PRD-to-product mode. Second, first-party wrapper starting-point templates at [scripts/claude-wrapper.sh.template](scripts/claude-wrapper.sh.template) and [scripts/codex-wrapper.sh.template](scripts/codex-wrapper.sh.template). Third, a hardened freshness check in all four adapter paths (`ManualHandoffClaudeAdapter`, `ManualHandoffCodexAdapter`, `SubprocessClaudeAdapter`, `SubprocessCodexAdapter`): the orchestrator now requires the canonical artifact's mtime to STRICTLY ADVANCE past the pre-invocation snapshot (previously the check was `!= prev_mtime` which accepted backdated rewrites), with an explicit `mtime did not advance` stderr line emitted on refusal. Configuring the wrappers enables automatic local invocation within the existing phase loop; it does NOT enable automatic next-phase activation, multi-target orchestration, MCP / RAG / GitHub integration, model-policy extensibility, or concurrent Claude/Codex overlap execution.

Phase 10H (Minimal External UI Read-Only Status Surface, complete) is the first runtime slice satisfying the approved [docs/external-ui-contract.md](docs/external-ui-contract.md) contract. The new `build_external_ui_status_view(controller_root: Path) -> dict` library function assembles a structured per-call view model from the approved canonical artifact read set: the controller's `.agent-loop/loop-state.json` (parsed via the shipped `load_loop_state(...)` validator and exposed as a canonical mirror with fields `phase` / `sub_phase` / `task` / `status` / `cycle_count` / `approval_mode` / `awaiting_human_for` / `last_verdict`), the controller's `TASK.md` / `.agent-loop/current-task.md` / `.agent-loop/current-phase.md` (canonical mirrors with `present` / `byte_size` / `error` metadata), the Phase 10F aggregator inspector report (`attached` / `schema_valid` / `schema_violations` / `freshness` sub-report, reused verbatim so the view stays consistent with `inspect-external-target`), and the target-side `.agent-loop/loop-state.json` + `TASK.md` + planning artifacts ONLY when the inspector reports the attach as both present AND fresh. The view model carries `view_signal_version = "phase-10h-v1"`, a single `advisory_status_label` (a human-friendly mapping of `loop-state.status` to `in-flight` / `halted` / `phase complete` / `awaiting first Phase 4C activation` / `other` derived from the most-relevant loop-state - target when rendered, otherwise controller), an `advisory_notes` list (e.g. "attach is STALE; run `verify-external-target` or `detach-external-target`" when freshness drifts), a `cli_only_operations` list of copy-paste-ready command cards covering the full Phase 10G CLI-only boundary - every shipped mutating subcommand (`attach-external-target` with and without `--bootstrap`, `detach-external-target`, `verify-external-target`, `plan`, `activate`, `run`, `resume`, `auto-continue`, `run-long-run-continuation`, `run-capacity-reprobe`, `record-final-acceptance`) AND every shipped read-only reporter the contract pins as CLI-only (`inspect-external-target`, `inspect-artifacts`, `status`, `evaluate-final-acceptance`, `validate-artifacts`, `check-state`), each card tagged with a `mutating` or `read_only` category - and the literal `EXTERNAL_UI_CANONICAL_PRECEDENCE_NOTE` reminder. Every operator-identity field in the cards is a `<NAME>` / `<PATH>` / `<MODE>` / `<TEXT>` placeholder (no auto-fill from `$USER` / `whoami` / browser session / any UI-side identity store, mirroring the Phase 10B/10C/9G non-auto-fill invariants the contract pins). The new `view-external-status` CLI subcommand surfaces the view through `cmd_view_external_status(args)` following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: always exits 0 on report content (absence of attach record, observed staleness, schema violations are all REPORT content rather than orchestrator failures); never mutates the attach record; never writes to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`; never advances loop-state. Each rendered output line is either attributed to its source artifact (`(canonical mirror, source=.agent-loop/loop-state.json)`) or tagged `[advisory]` so a reviewer can tell which fields are canonical and which are computed. The `cli_only_operations` cards print under an explicit "the UI does NOT execute these" header. Hard failures (unreadable attach record JSON, non-dict top-level attach record) propagate through `HaltError -> _halt` matching the existing `inspect-external-target` behavior so the structural refusal vocabulary stays consistent. The shipped attach / detach / bootstrap / verify / inspect CLI surfaces and library APIs are unchanged; the new view library function is purely additive and call-only (no shipped surface invokes it as a side effect). Out of scope for this slice: any mutating UI control surface, run/resume from the UI, canonical-artifact writes from the UI process, alternate artifact stores, web/browser/HTTP surfaces, target-side cycle dispatch, autonomous multi-target orchestration, and any rewrite of the no-Git-automation boundary in either root - those each land in their own later Phase 10 sub-phases tracked in `ROADMAP.md`.

Phase 10G (Minimal External UI Contract, complete) defines, in documentation form only, the first external operator UI surface for external-workspace mode. The contract lives at [docs/external-ui-contract.md](docs/external-ui-contract.md) and pins the four load-bearing boundaries every external UI runtime slice (the shipped Phase 10H read-only status surface, the shipped Phase 10I bounded run/resume controls layer, and every later Phase 10J+ extension) must satisfy: (1) the canonical artifact read set the UI MAY consume (the controller-side `.agent-loop/external-target.json`, `.agent-loop/loop-state.json`, `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, `.agent-loop/proposed-phase.md`, the per-cycle prompt/summary/review/fix-prompt artifacts, the Codex-owned `current-task.md` / `current-phase.md` / `phase-plan.md`, `TASK.md`, and `.agent-loop/final-acceptance.json` when present; plus the target-side `loop-state.json` / `orchestrator.log` / `TASK.md` / `current-task.md` / `current-phase.md` only when an attach record is present and fresh); (2) the advisory-vs-canonical mirror rule (every UI-rendered value is either a canonical mirror that attributes the source artifact and refreshes per poll, OR advisory derived state that MUST be marked as advisory; the UI MUST NOT cross-merge canonical mirrors from different snapshots and MUST NOT cache values past a single poll cycle); (3) the CLI-only operation enumeration (`attach-external-target`, `detach-external-target`, `verify-external-target`, `plan`, `activate`, `run`, `resume`, `auto-continue`, `record-final-acceptance`, and every other shipped mutating subcommand MUST be operator-initiated through the shipped CLI; the UI MAY render copy-paste-ready commands but MUST NOT execute them on the operator's behalf); (4) the safety/approval boundary preservation (no Git automation in either root; no `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`/`ROADMAP.md`/`README.md`/contract-doc/source edits as a side effect; no canonical-artifact writes from the UI process; no auto-fill of `attached_by` / `bootstrapped_by` / `detached_by` / `accepted_by` operator-identity fields from browser session, OS username, or any UI-side identity store; no silent phase activation; no silent Phase 9G acceptance; no silent re-bootstrap; the Phase 4C activator + `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` token, the Phase 9G `record-final-acceptance` CLI, and the Phase 10E `attach-external-target --bootstrap` CLI remain the only paths). The contract also enumerates the UI refusal cases (missing/unreadable/schema-failing attach record, freshness probe drift, target-root permission denial, unrecognized signal-version markers, missing/malformed `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` token, controller/target same-root or nested-root violations) and pins the source-of-truth preservation rules (no UI-side database/cache/event-stream/identity-token beyond per-poll in-memory rendering; canonical artifacts on disk always win). The Phase 10H read-only status surface (`build_external_ui_status_view(...)` + `view-external-status`) and the Phase 10I bounded run/resume controls layer (`build_external_ui_control_view(...)` + `view-external-controls` + `invoke-external-control`) ship as the first two runtime slices satisfying this contract; the still-deferred advanced UI capabilities (artifact dashboard / analytics / diff viewer / history explorer, concurrent-attach awareness, per-target activity stream) each land in their own later Phase 10J+ sub-phases tracked in `ROADMAP.md`. Phase 10G itself ships no UI runtime, no dashboard, no alternate artifact store, no automatic mutation path outside the shipped CLI surfaces, no target-side cycle dispatch, no new halt status, no new CLI subcommand, and no new library function. The shipped Phase 1 - 9 runtime behavior and the Phase 10A - 10F contract / runtime surfaces are preserved unchanged.

The operator flow across Phase 4B + Phase 4C is:

1. Run `python scripts/agent_loop.py plan` to generate `.agent-loop/proposed-phase.md`. The planner enforces every Phase 4A refusal condition (unresolved `NEEDS_FIXES`, `FAILED_REQUIRES_HUMAN`, any `halted_*` status, orchestrator-in-flight statuses, `cycle_count >= max_cycles` on `NEEDS_FIXES`, stale evidence, unreadable evidence headers, prior approved proposal carrying `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION`, malformed `loop-state.json`) and the bounded-scope rules (max 10 files, max 1 contract revision, testable Definition-of-done bullet, no vague Objective language, cycle-size estimate <= 3 without justification, no `active-in-flight` / `pending` dependency, no label collision against historical sub-phases, no `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` token authored by the planner itself, `## Exclusions` enumerating later 4x / 5+ sub-phases). The planner writes only `.agent-loop/proposed-phase.md` and a `note:`-style line in `.agent-loop/planner.log`.
2. A human reviews the proposal and, if accepted, appends a `## Approval` section to `.agent-loop/proposed-phase.md`. The approval section must contain the literal `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` token on its own line within the section AND must reference the proposal's `## Label` text. Token forgery (extra words on the line, alternate capitalization, surrounding characters) and stale-label approvals are refused at activation time.
3. Run `python scripts/agent_loop.py activate` to consume the approved proposal. The activator parses the proposal, verifies the approval requirements exactly, refuses cleanly on every failure mode (missing `## Approval` section, malformed token, label mismatch, missing/unreadable proposal, missing/malformed `loop-state.json`, empty required-proposal-section bodies), and on success performs the activation write set plus the Phase 5F phase-start prompt bootstrap in one fail-closed path: rewriting `TASK.md`'s `## Active Phase` / `## Active Sub-Phase` / `## Phase Status` / `## Active Task` / `## Phase Outcome Required Now` / `## Next-Phase Gate` / `## Out Of Scope For Current Phase` (preserving `## Human Objective` and `## Project Intent` verbatim); rewriting `.agent-loop/current-task.md` and `.agent-loop/current-phase.md`; updating `.agent-loop/phase-plan.md`'s `## Active Phase` line, closing the previously-active sub-phase's `### Status` opening line as a transition note, and APPENDING a new sub-phase section that mirrors the activated proposal; resetting `.agent-loop/loop-state.json` to `status = awaiting_claude_implementation`, `cycle_count = 0`, `last_verdict = null`, `last_verdict_phase = null` with `phase` / `sub_phase` / `task` set from the approved proposal and `max_cycles` / `contract_version` / `claude_version` / `codex_version` / `orchestrator_version` preserved per the Phase 3A write-ownership rules; writing `.agent-loop/claude-prompt.md` inside the same atomic-or-rollback activation transaction; appending a `note:`-style approval-source line (file path, mtime, literal approval line) to `.agent-loop/planner.log`; and appending a `prompt bootstrap:` audit note to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`. The activator exits 0 on success and 2 on any refusal; refusals never partially write any activation file.
4. Phase 4D integration (shipped): after the orchestrator persists a terminal `APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW` verdict in `_handle_verdict_loop`, it invokes the standalone planner exactly once to refresh `.agent-loop/proposed-phase.md`, then logs a single `note:`-style line to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` recording the planner's exit code. The hook is fully contained: a planner refusal (exit 2) is logged but does not convert the already-approved cycle into a halt, and any unexpected exception from planner code is caught and logged as a `note:` line rather than allowed to crash or halt the cycle. The orchestrator still returns 0 after the terminal approval in all of those cases. The hook never auto-activates a proposal and never widens the planner's write boundary (only `.agent-loop/proposed-phase.md` and `.agent-loop/planner.log` may be written by the planner call); approval and activation remain the separate, human-controlled `activate` step described above.

Phase 4E adds a planner-adapter seam. Both the `plan` path (step 1) and the post-approval planner refresh (step 4) now dispatch planner execution through `make_planner_adapter().run(...)` instead of calling the planner in-process directly. The default adapter (`LocalPlannerAdapter`) runs the same in-process planner, so behavior, refusal handling, the exception-containment guarantees on the post-approval path, and the planner's write boundary (`.agent-loop/proposed-phase.md` and `.agent-loop/planner.log` only) are all unchanged; the seam exists so a later slice can supply an alternate planner adapter without touching the call sites. The adapter is purely a dispatch boundary: it never widens the planner write boundary and never activates a proposal. Activation stays the separate, human-controlled `activate` step - planning (proposal generation and refresh) and activation remain distinct.

Phase 5B (Review Mode Initial Slice, shipped) makes `review` the explicit default approval mode and adds the first machine-readable Claude completion handoff artifact. New / freshly activated Phase 5+ runtime state in `.agent-loop/loop-state.json` carries `approval_mode = "review"` and `awaiting_human_for = null` by default (a pre-existing non-review `approval_mode` is preserved across activation; `awaiting_human_for` is always reset by activation). During a normal cycle the orchestrator backfills the same defaults on any loaded state that pre-dates Phase 5B, and on the implemented `review` path it sets `awaiting_human_for = "phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval"` on `APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW` and leaves it `null` everywhere else. After Claude returns a fresh `claude-summary.md` for either an implementation or fix cycle and evidence succeeds, the orchestrator writes `.agent-loop/claude-done.json` (`signal_version`, `phase`, `sub_phase`, `task`, `cycle_count`, `mode` of `"implementation"` or `"fix"`, `source_prompt_path`, and `status = "ready_for_codex_review"`) as a routing/timing signal only; it never substitutes for `claude-summary.md`, the git diff, or validation evidence in review. Each new prompt or fix-prompt issuance clears any prior `claude-done.json` so a stale completion signal cannot drive the wrong review cycle. The shipped `review`-mode baseline is preserved unchanged by Phase 5C.

Phase 5C (Strict Mode Pauses, shipped) implements the three `strict`-mode human checkpoints defined in the Phase 5A contract. When `approval_mode = "strict"`, the orchestrator pauses at: (1) before a new implementation prompt dispatches to Claude (gate name `pre_claude_prompt`, halt status `halted_awaiting_human_pre_claude_prompt`); (2) before a new fix prompt dispatches to Claude (`pre_fix_prompt`, `halted_awaiting_human_pre_fix_prompt`); (3) after Claude completion and evidence validation but before Codex review begins (`pre_codex_review`, `halted_awaiting_human_pre_codex_review_normal` for the implementation cycle and `halted_awaiting_human_pre_codex_review_fix` for the fix cycle - two halt-status flavors so resume can route to the right continuation). At each gate the orchestrator persists `awaiting_human_for = <gate name>` plus the matching halt status in a single atomic write, logs a `note:` line to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, and exits cleanly with code 2. After the human approves the paused step they run `python scripts/agent_loop.py resume`, which refuses unless `status` is one of the four strict-gate halts, then clears `awaiting_human_for`, restores a ready-to-continue status, and dispatches to the continuation matched by the persisted gate halt-status so no earlier work is re-done and the next gate (if any) still fires.

Phase 5D (Autonomous Mode Initial Slice, shipped) implements the first narrow `autonomous` runtime continuation path. When `approval_mode = "autonomous"`, the orchestrator bypasses each of the three Phase 5C strict-mode human gates (`pre_claude_prompt`, `pre_fix_prompt`, and `pre_codex_review` - which fires once per cycle and persists in two halt-status flavors `halted_awaiting_human_pre_codex_review_normal` / `halted_awaiting_human_pre_codex_review_fix` so resume can route to the matching implementation-cycle or fix-cycle continuation) and continues directly through the existing implementation step, the existing Claude-completion-to-Codex-review handoff, and bounded `NEEDS_FIXES` fix cycles within the existing `cycle_count` / `max_cycles` threshold. Each gate bypass writes a single `autonomous mode: bypassing <gate> gate at <where>` note to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` so the mode is auditable from repo artifacts and not hidden in transient control flow. Every existing hard stop is preserved: `FAILED_REQUIRES_HUMAN` still halts immediately, malformed evidence or summaries still fail closed, the `cycle_count >= max_cycles` threshold on `NEEDS_FIXES` still halts, and the `APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW` terminal still persists `status = phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval` and `awaiting_human_for = phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval` because the Phase 5A contract requires human approval before phase progression. The shipped `review` (Phase 5B) and `strict` (Phase 5C) behavior is unchanged.

Phase 5E (Post-Review Artifact Reconciliation, shipped) makes the post-review follow-up step deterministic and ownership-aware. Every review issue is classified Codex-owned vs Claude-owned: an explicit `Owner:` field is honored if present, otherwise inference is based on the issue's `File(s)` partition. Inference returns Codex when every listed file is in `CODEX_OWNED_REVIEW_PATHS`, Claude when every listed file is outside that set, and refuses at parse time (`halted_review_parse_failed`) when the file list mixes Codex-owned planning paths with Claude-owned implementation paths (the contract's "refuse when ownership is ambiguous" rule). Each supported Codex auto-fix action declares a per-action allow-list of paths it may touch (`CODEX_ACTION_ALLOWED_FILES`); a Codex-owned issue whose `File(s)` list extends outside its action's allow-list is refused at reconciliation time (`halted_input_missing`) before any side-effect, which is the contract guarantee that a Codex auto-fix cannot overwrite Claude-owned implementation work. Reconciliation writes an up-front `review reconciliation: codex_owned_issues=N (actions=[...]) claude_owned_issues=N` summary to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, keeps the per-action and fix-prompt-synchronization notes from earlier phases, and now reloads loop-state from disk on the reconciliation halt path so a successful Codex auto-fix (e.g. `sync_phase5_runtime_defaults`) persists across the subsequent halt instead of being clobbered by the pre-reconciliation snapshot. The shipped `review` (Phase 5B), `strict` (Phase 5C), and `autonomous` (Phase 5D) runtime behavior is unchanged; Claude-owned findings still drive `.agent-loop/fix-prompt.md` regeneration in the required handoff format.

Phase 6G (Automatic Continuation Chaining Initial Slice, shipped) builds on the shipped 6D / 6E / 6F primitives by adding bounded automatic continuation chaining over the existing single-hop token-exhaustion resume. The operator runtime entry is the new `auto-continue` subcommand (`python scripts/agent_loop.py auto-continue`): while `.agent-loop/loop-state.json` is at `halted_awaiting_token_exhaustion_continuation`, the chain repeatedly applies the shipped Phase 6F `run_token_exhaustion_resume` hop until one of these stop conditions: (a) a hop's return code is non-zero (a refusal - budget exhausted, malformed checkpoint, unsupported saved stage, cycle-identity mismatch - or a non-token halt inside the dispatched continuation), in which case the chain propagates the rc unchanged; (b) the cycle has cleared the token-exhaustion halt after a successful hop (no fresh exhaustion event was recorded during the dispatched continuation), in which case the chain returns 0; or (c) `AUTO_CONTINUE_MAX_HOPS = 4` hops have run, in which case the chain refuses fail-closed as a defense-in-depth bound independent of the per-checkpoint `continuation_budget`. Each chain hop logs an `auto-continue chain hop N begin` audit line so the chain is reconstructable from `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` after the fact. After every successful hop the chain re-runs the same `validate_loop_state` and `check_contract_version` structural / contract checks the chain entry used BEFORE branching on the persisted halt status, so a dispatched continuation that left loadable-but-invalid loop-state on disk (missing required field, unsupported `contract_version`, etc.) is refused fail-closed via `_halt` rather than logged as a chain completion or silently advanced into another hop. The initial "status is not the token-exhaustion halt" check uses the recovery-preserving refusal pattern (NOT `_halt`), so running `auto-continue` against a strict-gate halt by mistake does not clobber the strict-gate recovery point. The chain is OPT-IN: the existing `resume` subcommand still dispatches the single-hop resume on a `HALTED_TOKEN_EXHAUSTION` halt; only the dedicated `auto-continue` subcommand enters the chain. Phase-boundary memory distillation, repeated-failure memory synthesis, and broader optional-context file loading remain deferred. Every shipped Phase 5 behavior (`review`, `strict`, `autonomous`, post-review reconciliation, and the phase-start prompt baseline) remains unchanged.

Phase 6H (Bounded Continuation Prompt Construction Initial Slice, shipped) builds on the shipped 6B (storage), 6C (advisory-only retrieval), 6D (checkpoint storage), 6E (checkpoint resume), 6F (token-exhaustion continuation), and 6G (auto-continuation chaining) primitives. The new `build_continuation_prompt_context(repo_root, *, memory_entry_limit=None, evidence_byte_limit=None, log_path=None) -> dict` library function assembles a structured continuation-context payload from canonical loop-state + the active Phase 6F/6G token-exhaustion checkpoint + bounded evidence excerpts + advisory durable-memory entries. The dict carries a top-level `context_signal_version = "phase-6h-v1"`, a `canonical_state` subset of loop-state (phase / sub_phase / task / cycle_count / approval_mode / halt_status / awaiting_human_for), a `checkpoint` subset of the active checkpoint (suspension_reason, active_prompt_path, continuation_budget, pre_suspension_status, pre_suspension_awaiting_human_for, source_path, source_artifact_path, created_at), an `evidence` map of each `EVIDENCE_FILES` rel path -> bounded excerpt metadata (`{absent: True}` for a missing file, otherwise the full readable-file block `{absent: False, byte_size_on_disk, excerpt, excerpt_byte_size, truncated}`; an evidence file that exists but cannot be read refuses fail-closed rather than emitting an undocumented `read_error` branch), a `memory_advisory` list of Phase 6C-retrieved entries (each carrying `advisory_only = True`; the `checkpoint` memory category is intentionally excluded because the active checkpoint is already on the dedicated `checkpoint` key), `memory_limit_applied` / `memory_truncated_at_limit` flags, `evidence_byte_limit_applied`, and an explicit `canonical_precedence_note` reminder that canonical artifacts win over advisory memory. The operator runtime entry is the new `build-continuation-context` subcommand which persists the JSON payload to `.agent-loop/continuation-context.json` (override with `--output`) so the construction is auditable from on-disk artifacts; it accepts `--memory-entry-limit` (default `CONTINUATION_CONTEXT_DEFAULT_MEMORY_LIMIT = 8`, capped at `MEMORY_RETRIEVAL_MAX_LIMIT = 32`) and `--evidence-byte-limit` (default `CONTINUATION_CONTEXT_DEFAULT_EVIDENCE_BYTE_LIMIT = 4096` per file, capped at `CONTINUATION_CONTEXT_MAX_EVIDENCE_BYTE_LIMIT = 65536`). Refusal modes (all fail-closed): missing / malformed `loop-state.json`, unsupported `contract_version`, halt status outside the eligible set (only `HALTED_TOKEN_EXHAUSTION` in this initial slice; strict-gate halts continue using their existing `resume` path), no active checkpoint on disk, cycle-identity mismatch between checkpoint and loop-state, `suspension_reason` other than `token_exhaustion`, out-of-bound memory or evidence limits (zero, negative, non-int, bool, above the cap), and an evidence file that exists on disk but cannot be read. The library function never writes to canonical artifacts and is byte-equivalent on loop-state and the checkpoint across a build call. Prompt string serialization, phase-boundary memory distillation, repeated-failure synthesis, and broader optional context-file loading beyond the existing `EVIDENCE_FILES` set remain deferred. Every shipped Phase 5 behavior remains unchanged.

Phase 6I (Phase-Boundary Memory Distillation Initial Slice, shipped) builds on the shipped 6B (storage), 6C (retrieval), 6D (checkpoint storage), 6E (resume), 6F (token-exhaustion continuation), 6G (auto-continue), and 6H (continuation context) primitives. The new `distill_phase_boundary_memory(repo_root, *, excerpt_byte_limit=None, log_path=None) -> list[Path]` library function writes append-mostly durable memory entries in three of the five Phase 6A categories - `summary`, `decision`, and conditionally `failure` - at a successfully APPROVED phase boundary, sourced from canonical loop-state plus bounded excerpts of the canonical source artifacts `.agent-loop/claude-summary.md` and `.agent-loop/codex-review.md`. Each entry body carries an explicit `distillation_signal_version = "phase-6i-v1"` marker plus a `source_artifacts` list pointing at the canonical sources so a later retrieval can trace provenance. The `summary` and `decision` entries always land; the `failure` entry lands only when `cycle_count > 1`, codifying "phase required N-1 fix cycle(s) before reaching APPROVED" without parsing internal markdown. The operator runtime entry is the new `distill-phase-boundary-memory` subcommand which accepts `--excerpt-byte-limit` (default `DISTILLATION_DEFAULT_EXCERPT_BYTE_LIMIT = 4096` per source file, capped at `DISTILLATION_MAX_EXCERPT_BYTE_LIMIT = 65536`) and prints the list of new memory-entry paths plus a `phase-boundary distillation:` audit note. Refusal modes (all fail-closed): missing / malformed `loop-state.json`, unsupported `contract_version`, status not `phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval`, `last_verdict` not `APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW`, missing or malformed `claude-summary.md` (`halted_summary_malformed` from `validate_claude_summary`), missing or malformed `codex-review.md` (`halted_review_malformed` / `halted_review_parse_failed` from `validate_codex_review_and_parse_verdict`), on-disk codex-review verdict disagreeing with loop-state `last_verdict`, an unreadable source artifact (raises `OSError` on `read_bytes`), an already-distilled-marker `summary` entry on disk for this exact `(phase, sub_phase, cycle_count)` identity (idempotency check), a summary entry whose envelope is unreadable / not JSON / not a dict (identity-unknown branch of the idempotency probe), a matching-identity summary entry whose body is unreadable / non-string / non-JSON / not a dict (matching-identity branch of the idempotency probe), and out-of-bound `excerpt_byte_limit`. The library function never mutates `loop-state.json` or any canonical source artifact; only `.agent-loop/memory/<category>/` is written. A re-run is refused fail-closed rather than silently producing duplicate entries. The multi-entry write is atomic: a failure during the `decision` or `failure` write rolls back any entries already written in that call before the exception propagates, so a failed distillation never leaves a partial set on disk and never blocks a clean retry. Broader optional context-file loading, repeated-failure synthesis beyond the narrow phase-boundary helper, and orchestrator-side automatic invocation from the verdict-handling path remain deferred. Every shipped Phase 5 behavior remains unchanged.

Phase 6L (Repeated-Failure Memory Synthesis Initial Slice, shipped) builds on the shipped 6B / 6C / 6D / 6E / 6F / 6G / 6H / 6I / 6J / 6K primitives by distilling recurring failure patterns into a NEW durable advisory `failure` memory entry. The new `synthesize_repeated_failures(repo_root, *, min_entries=None, max_source_entries=None, log_path=None) -> Path` library function reads ONLY the existing `failure`-category memory entries that match the active loop-state `(phase, sub_phase)` (bounded, on-disk, structurally validated by the shipped Phase 6B `read_memory_entry`), skips any entry already carrying the 6L body marker so the synthesis layer stays flat (no synthesis-of-syntheses), and writes a new `failure` memory entry through the shipped Phase 6B `write_memory_entry` plumbing carrying a `synthesis_signal_version = "phase-6l-v1"` body marker plus the ordered list of source memory entry paths so a later retrieval can trace provenance. The body also records `source_count`, `source_cycle_counts`, a per-source bounded excerpt list (`source_path` / `cycle_count` / `created_at` / `knowledge_type` / `failure`), `min_entries_applied`, `max_source_entries_applied`, `synthesized_at`, the literal `REPEATED_FAILURE_CANONICAL_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`, and the canonical task/phase/sub_phase/cycle_count/approval_mode passed through from loop-state. The operator runtime entry is the new `synthesize-repeated-failures` subcommand which accepts `--min-entries` (default `REPEATED_FAILURE_DEFAULT_MIN_ENTRIES = 2`, must be >= 2 and capped at `REPEATED_FAILURE_MAX_MIN_ENTRIES = 32`) and `--max-source-entries` (default `REPEATED_FAILURE_DEFAULT_MAX_SOURCE_ENTRIES = 8`, must be >= 2 and capped at `REPEATED_FAILURE_MAX_MAX_SOURCE_ENTRIES = 32`); when more than the cap match, the newest entries by `(cycle_count, created_at)` are kept. Refusal modes (all fail-closed): missing / malformed `loop-state.json`; unsupported `contract_version` (`halted_contract_version_mismatch`); fewer than `min_entries` matching non-synthesis source entries on disk for the active `(phase, sub_phase)`; an existing 6L synthesis entry already on disk for the same `(phase, sub_phase, source-set)` identity; out-of-bound `min_entries` or `max_source_entries` (zero, negative, below 2, non-int, bool, above the cap); `max_source_entries < min_entries` (contradictory); a matching-identity probe envelope whose body is 6L-marked but has a non-list `source_memory_entries` value (matching-identity malformed branch never silently skipped). The library function never reads arbitrary repo files, raw logs, transcripts, or anything outside `.agent-loop/memory/failure/`; never mutates `loop-state.json`; never mutates any source memory entry; and writes only to `.agent-loop/memory/failure/` through the Phase 6B append-mostly guarantee. A Phase 6I distillation `failure` entry (which carries the 6I `distillation_signal_version` marker but NOT the 6L marker) IS a valid source for the 6L synthesis. Arbitrary repo-file ingestion, raw-transcript-as-memory, framework-backed runtime paths, and orchestrator-side automatic invocation from the verdict-handling / continuation / prompt-bootstrap paths remain deferred. Every shipped Phase 5 behavior remains unchanged.

Phase 6M (Runtime Adapter Contract For Framework Evaluation, shipped) defined the adapter boundary any future framework-backed runtime must satisfy before it can be evaluated against the shipped local orchestrator. That contract pins the canonical source-of-truth artifact set, allowed reads through the shipped validators only, allowed writes through the shipped writer surfaces only, forbidden-write boundaries, Codex/Claude/human ownership preservation, approval-mode and halt/refusal vocabulary preservation, checkpoint/continuation rules, durable-memory boundaries, audit expectations, default-runtime/evaluation constraints, and the load-bearing rule that canonical repo artifacts always win over framework-managed state.

Phase 10F (External Target Validation And Refusal Hardening, complete) hardens the shipped external-workspace surfaces with stronger internal-coherence validation, a new freshness probe for on-disk drift detection, and a non-mutating inspector subcommand. The Phase 10B/10C attach-record schema validator (`_validate_external_target_attach_record_schema(...)`) now enforces five additional internal-coherence invariants the prior validator only required to be type-correct: (1) `mode_selection.selected_by == attached_by` (single operator identity); (2) `mode_selection.selected_at == attached_at` (single attach-session timestamp); (3) `stale_attach_detection.target_path_canonical_at_attach == target_path_canonical`; (4) `stale_attach_detection.controller_path_canonical_at_attach == controller_path_canonical`; (5) `bootstrap_state` extension-field coherence (a `target_canonical_set_present` record MUST carry null for every Phase 10C extension field; a `target_canonical_set_bootstrapped` record MUST carry non-null values plus `bootstrap_signal_version == "phase-10c-v1"`, `pre_bootstrap_target_state == "empty_target"`, and `initial_loop_state_status == "awaiting_first_activation"`). The existing detach path automatically inherits these checks because it already runs the schema validator before any side effect; a malformed-but-JSON record that contradicts itself now refuses fail-closed via `halted_input_missing` instead of being silently accepted. Two new library functions add the freshness layer: `probe_external_target_attach_freshness(controller_root) -> dict` is a non-throwing reporter that loads the attach record, compares `stale_attach_detection.target_path_canonical_at_attach` to the path the target now resolves to, checks every marker file from `target_marker_files_at_attach` is still present on disk, and compares `controller_path_canonical` to the running controller's canonical path; the report carries `is_fresh`, `reasons`, `missing_target_marker_files`, and `current_*_path_canonical` fields plus a `freshness_signal_version = "phase-10f-v1"` marker. `assert_external_target_attach_fresh(controller_root) -> None` is the throwing companion that raises a new `halted_external_target_stale_attach` halt status on any observed drift (and `halted_input_missing` when no attach record exists). The new `verify-external-target` CLI subcommand wires that assertion into a shipped operator-runtime path: it calls `assert_external_target_attach_fresh(...)`, routes any `HaltError` through the existing `_halt` so the halt status is persisted into `loop-state.json` and logged to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, exits 2 on `halted_external_target_stale_attach` (drift observed) or `halted_input_missing` (no attach record), and exits 0 with a one-line acknowledgement only on a verifiably fresh attach. The shipped attach / detach / bootstrap surfaces continue to behave exactly as they did before this slice; `verify-external-target` is the dedicated fail-closed surface where the operator opts into strict freshness enforcement. A third library function `inspect_external_target_attach(controller_root) -> dict` aggregates attach-record presence + the schema validator's verdict (captured as a list of violation reasons rather than re-raised) + the freshness probe into a single structured report. A new `inspect-external-target` CLI subcommand surfaces the aggregator report following the Phase 7C reporter pattern: it always exits 0 (absence, staleness, and schema violations are report content rather than orchestrator failures), never mutates the attach record, never writes to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, and never advances loop-state; hard failures (unreadable attach record JSON, non-dict top-level) propagate through `_halt` exactly like the existing detach refusals so the structural-failure vocabulary stays consistent. The new `halted_external_target_stale_attach` status is documented in [docs/halt-and-recovery.md](docs/halt-and-recovery.md). The shipped attach / detach / bootstrap runtime surfaces, CLI grammar, halt-status vocabulary on the no-bootstrap and bootstrap branches, source-of-truth boundary (controller-owned metadata stays controller-owned, target-side canonical artifacts stay target-owned), Phase 4C activator + `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` activation gate, approval-mode semantics, checkpoint/resume behavior, cycle thresholds, and no-Git-automation boundary in both roots are all preserved unchanged. Out of scope for this slice: target-side cycle dispatch (Phase 10G), external UI / dashboard / run-control, concurrent multi-target orchestration, and any rewrite of the shipped attach / detach / bootstrap library APIs or CLI surfaces.

Phase 10E (External Workspace Bootstrap Runtime Initial Slice, complete) implements the explicit bootstrap runtime path for `empty_target` external workspaces under the approved Phase 10C bootstrap contract. The new `bootstrap_external_target(target_root, *, bootstrapped_by, attached_by, approval_mode, human_objective, project_intent, controller_contract_version, controller_orchestrator_version, bootstrapped_at=None, bootstrap_log_line=None) -> dict` library function validates the operator inputs (`bootstrapped_by` matches the bounded-string vocabulary capped at 200 chars and MUST equal `attached_by` per the Phase 10C single-operator-identity invariant; `human_objective` is required, capped at 8000 chars in the body and 200 chars in the attach-record excerpt; `project_intent` is required, capped at 8000 chars; `approval_mode` is from the shipped Phase 10B closed enumeration), re-classifies the target's pre-bootstrap state, refuses fail-closed on anything other than `empty_target`, and writes exactly the five canonical bootstrap artifacts (`TASK.md`, `.agent-loop/current-task.md`, `.agent-loop/current-phase.md`, `.agent-loop/phase-plan.md`, `.agent-loop/loop-state.json`) with the Phase 10C-pinned initial contents through an atomic temp-write + rename sequence; a failure during either the temp-write phase or the rename phase rolls back every artifact already landed in the same call (plus the `.agent-loop/` directory if the bootstrap created it) so the target is left in the original `empty_target` state. The runtime returns the bootstrap-state payload the caller persists into the controller-owned attach record's Phase 10C extension fields (`status = "target_canonical_set_bootstrapped"`, `bootstrap_signal_version = "phase-10c-v1"`, `pre_bootstrap_target_state = "empty_target"`, the sorted list of `artifacts_written`, `initial_loop_state_status = "awaiting_first_activation"`, the bounded `initial_human_objective_excerpt`, and the `bootstrap_log_line` byte-for-byte matched to the controller's `orchestrator.log`). The Phase 10D `attach_external_target(...)` runtime gains a `bootstrap=False, bootstrapped_by=None, human_objective=None, project_intent=None` opt-in surface and the matching `--bootstrap` / `--bootstrapped-by` / `--human-objective` / `--project-intent` CLI flags on `attach-external-target`; passing `--bootstrap` against an `empty_target` invokes the bootstrap runtime before the attach record write (so a bootstrap refusal leaves no attach record on disk), passing `--bootstrap` against a `full_target` is a no-op honored by the runtime checking whether bootstrap is needed, and passing `--bootstrap` against a `partial_target` / `malformed_target` still refuses per the Phase 10C bootstrap-never-overwrites guarantee. An attach without `--bootstrap` against `empty_target` now refuses with an explicit forward-pointer to the opt-in flag. Two new halt statuses (`halted_external_target_bootstrap_input_missing` for missing / over-bounded / identity-mismatch / partial- or malformed-state refusals at bootstrap time; `halted_external_target_bootstrap_atomicity_failure` for atomic-write or rename failures after rollback) are added to the shipped vocabulary and documented in [docs/halt-and-recovery.md](docs/halt-and-recovery.md). Bootstrap never activates a target-side phase: the bootstrapped target sits at `loop-state.status = "awaiting_first_activation"` until the shipped Phase 4C activator + `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` token runs against the target. The Phase 2A / 3A / 4A contract bodies, the planner / activator separation, the Phase 5A approval modes, the Phase 5E reconciliation, the Phase 5F prompt bootstrap, the Phase 6 memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support, the Phase 9B-9G surfaces, and the Phase 10A-10D contract / runtime surfaces remain unchanged. Out of scope for this slice were target-side cycle dispatch, external UI / dashboard / run-control, concurrent attaches, multi-target operation, and any rewrite of the shipped no-Git-automation boundary in either root.

Phase 10C (External Workspace Bootstrap Contract, complete) defines, in documentation form only, how target-side `.agent-loop` initialization may occur in external-workspace mode before any attach or bootstrap runtime ships. The contract lives at [docs/external-workspace-bootstrap-contract.md](docs/external-workspace-bootstrap-contract.md) and satisfies the Phase 10B attach-record `bootstrap_state` sub-object's delegated field-level schema. The contract pins exactly five target-side canonical artifacts the bootstrap surface may write during a bootstrap event (and only when the operator has explicitly opted into bootstrap on an empty target): the target's `TASK.md`, `.agent-loop/current-task.md`, `.agent-loop/current-phase.md`, `.agent-loop/phase-plan.md`, and `.agent-loop/loop-state.json`. Those same canonical artifacts continue to be rewritten by normal target-side flows (the shipped Phase 4C activator on per-target phase activation, the shipped Phase 3A orchestrator on per-cycle `loop-state.json` updates, etc.); Phase 10C constrains bootstrap's own write boundary, not the long-term write ownership of those files. The contract pins minimum-required initial contents for each (the target's `TASK.md` carries the operator-supplied Human Objective and Project Intent plus explicit `(to be set by first Phase 4C activation)` placeholders for every other section; the three planning skeletons each carry a single header and an `Awaiting first Phase 4C activation` body; the target's `loop-state.json` carries `phase = null`, `sub_phase = null`, `task = null`, `status = "awaiting_first_activation"` (a new status the Phase 10E bootstrap runtime slice will introduce paired with a `docs/halt-and-recovery.md` update), `cycle_count = 0`, `max_cycles = 3`, `last_verdict = null`, `last_verdict_phase = null`, `contract_version` / `orchestrator_version` from the running controller, `claude_version = null`, `codex_version = null`, `approval_mode` from the operator's attach-time selection, `awaiting_human_for = null`). The contract pins a four-value `pre_bootstrap_target_state` enumeration that the attach runtime MUST classify every operator-supplied target root into: `empty_target` (the only state bootstrap may proceed against, and only with operator opt-in), `partial_target` (always refused fail-closed; bootstrap MUST NOT auto-complete a partial canonical set), `full_target` (no bootstrap needed; the attach runtime records `bootstrap_state.status = "target_canonical_set_present"`), and `malformed_target` (always refused fail-closed; bootstrap MUST NOT overwrite an existing canonical artifact even if it is malformed - the load-bearing "bootstrap never overwrites" guarantee). The contract pins six required extension fields the bootstrap runtime MUST write into the Phase 10B attach record's `bootstrap_state` sub-object when bootstrap was performed: the literal `bootstrap_signal_version = "phase-10c-v1"`; `pre_bootstrap_target_state` (MUST equal `"empty_target"` when bootstrap proceeded); `artifacts_written` (a sorted list of exactly the five canonical paths bootstrap is allowed to write; any other set refuses both at write time and at attach-record validation time); `initial_loop_state_status` (MUST equal `"awaiting_first_activation"`); `initial_human_objective_excerpt` (capped at 200 chars matching the shipped `attached_by` / `accepted_by` bounded-string vocabulary); and `bootstrap_log_line` (byte-for-byte matched to the controller's `orchestrator.log` mirroring the Phase 10B `audit.attach_log_line` pattern). Operator opt-in is via a separate `--bootstrap` CLI flag that defaults off; the operator must explicitly supply `bootstrapped_by` (never auto-filled from `$USER` / `whoami`); `bootstrapped_by` MUST equal `attached_by` so the attach and the bootstrap record the same operator identity in one attach session; and the operator must supply the initial `TASK.md.Human Objective` and `Project Intent` values (no defaults). The contract preserves the shipped artifact source-of-truth model (canonical artifacts on disk remain authoritative; the bootstrap surface writes only canonical artifacts and introduces no advisory state), the shipped CLI-first workflow, the shipped no-Git-automation boundary in BOTH roots, the shipped Phase 4 planner / Phase 4C activator + `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` token separation (bootstrap initializes the target's canonical set but does NOT activate the first phase; the bootstrapped target sits at `awaiting_first_activation` until the operator runs the shipped activator against the target), the shipped review / strict / autonomous approval-mode semantics (bootstrap honors whichever mode the operator selected at attach time and never widens autonomy past it), the shipped halt / refusal vocabulary (refusal cases include no-opt-in attach against non-`full_target`, opt-in attach against `partial_target` / `malformed_target`, missing / over-bounded `bootstrapped_by`, missing initial `Human Objective` / `Project Intent`, `bootstrapped_by != attached_by`, atomicity failure with rollback, controller-self / controller-nested target, non-writable target; each maps onto a `halted_external_target_bootstrap_*` status the Phase 10E runtime slice will introduce paired with a `docs/halt-and-recovery.md` update), the Phase 9G final human acceptance gate (bootstrap never records final acceptance), the shipped checkpoint / continuation behavior, and the shipped cycle-threshold halts. Phase 10C is documentation / contract only: no bootstrap runtime, no attach / detach runtime, no target-side cycle dispatch, no external UI / dashboard / run-control, no concurrent execution, no MCP / RAG / GitHub / model-policy work, no new CLI subcommand, no new library function, no new runtime constant, no new halt status, and no new signal-version-marker code change ships in this slice. The runtime that writes the target-side canonical set is deferred to Phase 10E; the attach / detach runtime that consumes this contract during attach is Phase 10D; target-side cycle dispatch over a fully-bootstrapped + activated target is Phase 10F; the external UI / dashboard / run-control advisory surface is Phase 10G; concurrent execution / MCP / RAG / GitHub / model-policy work each land in Phase 10H and later sub-phases tracked in `ROADMAP.md`.

Phase 10B (External Target Attach Record Contract, complete) defines, in documentation form only, the controller-owned attach-record schema that the future Phase 10D attach / detach runtime must persist before any target-side cycle begins. The contract lives at [docs/external-target-attach-record-contract.md](docs/external-target-attach-record-contract.md) and pins the on-disk shape, write boundary, and refusal behavior of `.agent-loop/external-target.json` (the path the Phase 10A contract assigned to the controller-owned attach record). Every required top-level field is schema-stable across releases: `attach_record_signal_version` (the literal `"phase-10b-v1"`); `attached_at` (UTC ISO-8601 attach timestamp); `attached_by` (operator-supplied non-empty string capped at 200 chars; never auto-filled from `$USER` / `whoami`); `target_path_canonical` (the operator-supplied target root AFTER `Path.resolve()` canonicalization; the load-bearing source of truth for "which directory the controller is currently attached to"); `target_path_raw` (the literal operator input for refusal-message reproducibility only; never participates in any safety check); `controller_path_canonical` (the controller root AFTER `Path.resolve()`, derived from the shipped `find_repo_root()`); `controller_identity` (a structured sub-object carrying `repo_signature` as a stable opaque controller identifier, `orchestrator_version` matching the shipped `ORCHESTRATOR_VERSION` vocabulary, and `contract_version` matching the shipped `loop-state.contract_version` vocabulary); `mode_selection` (sub-object carrying `approval_mode` from the closed enumeration `{"review", "strict", "autonomous", "phase_9_autonomous_prd"}` plus `selected_at` and `selected_by` with the same 200-char bounded vocabulary as `attached_by`); `bootstrap_state` (sub-object carrying `status` from the closed enumeration `{"target_canonical_set_present", "target_canonical_set_bootstrapped"}` plus the matched `bootstrapped_at` and `bootstrapped_by` fields, both `null` when no bootstrap was performed); `stale_attach_detection` (sub-object carrying `target_marker_files_at_attach` for the on-disk staleness probe plus the attach-time canonical snapshots of both target and controller paths so a later contract amendment can detect canonical-representation divergence); `audit` (sub-object carrying the literal `attach_log_line` byte-for-byte matched to the controller's `orchestrator.log` plus a bounded `refusal_history` list capped at 32 entries); and the literal `canonical_precedence_note` reminder string mirroring the shipped `FINAL_ACCEPTANCE_CANONICAL_PRECEDENCE_NOTE` / `CAPACITY_RETRY_CANONICAL_PRECEDENCE_NOTE` pattern. Every required field is canonical; the contract intentionally defines no advisory fields, so a future external UI / dashboard surfacing attach metadata MUST treat the on-disk record as authoritative and any in-process cache as advisory. The runtime that writes the record MUST canonicalize paths via `Path.resolve()` BEFORE storing / comparing / refusing, MUST write atomically (temp-write + rename), MUST refuse fail-closed on every contract-listed refusal case (record already exists without explicit detach, partially-written / malformed / unrecognized-signal-version record, target root same as / nested inside the controller root, missing or over-bounded `attached_by`, unknown `approval_mode`, inconsistent `bootstrap_state.status`/`bootstrapped_at`/`bootstrapped_by` combinations, missing or wrong-typed controller-identity sub-fields, target-side staleness from missing marker files or moved/renamed target, controller-side staleness from a different running controller), and MUST never write any field outside the schema this contract enumerates (undeclared fields are a contract change via signal-version bump, not a runtime extension). The contract preserves the shipped artifact source-of-truth model (canonical artifacts on disk win over any controller-side metadata or future external UI; `.agent-loop/loop-state.json` remains canonical for per-target phase / cycle / status / verdict; the target's `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` remains canonical for the per-target audit trail; the controller's `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` remains canonical for controller-side attach / refusal events), the shipped CLI-first workflow, the shipped no-Git-automation boundary in BOTH roots (controller and target), the shipped Phase 4 planner / Phase 4C activator + `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` token separation (attach never activates a target-side phase), the shipped review / strict / autonomous approval-mode semantics (applied per-target per-attach), the shipped halt / refusal vocabulary (every Phase 10B refusal maps onto a shipped halt-vocabulary term or onto a forward-referenced `halted_external_target_*` status the Phase 10C bootstrap contract or the Phase 10D attach runtime will introduce paired with a `docs/halt-and-recovery.md` update at that time), the Phase 9G final human acceptance gate (attach never records final acceptance and never bypasses Phase 9G), the shipped checkpoint / continuation behavior, and the shipped cycle-threshold halts. Phase 10B is documentation / contract only: no attach / detach runtime, no target bootstrap runtime, no external UI / dashboard / run-control, no concurrent Codex/Claude execution, no MCP integration, no RAG layer, no GitHub integration, no model-policy extensibility, no CLI / library / runtime constant / halt status / signal-version-marker code change ships in this slice. The runtime that writes / reads / refreshes the attach record is deferred to Phase 10D; the bootstrap contract the `bootstrap_state` sub-object delegates to is the work of Phase 10C; the target bootstrap runtime that satisfies the Phase 10C contract is the work of Phase 10E; target-side cycle dispatch / run-control / concurrent execution / MCP / RAG / GitHub / model-policy work each land in their own later Phase 10 sub-phases tracked in `ROADMAP.md`.

Phase 10A (External Workspace Controller Contract, complete) defined, in documentation form only, how this controller repository can safely target an EXTERNAL workspace or repository on the same machine without collapsing controller-owned and target-owned responsibilities, runtime state, or artifact truth. That baseline contract lives at [docs/external-workspace-contract.md](docs/external-workspace-contract.md) and remains the source document for controller-vs-target ownership, path-boundary rules, attach/bootstrap refusal expectations, and approval-gate preservation. The Phase 10B paragraph above ships the next layer on top of that baseline by pinning the attach record's on-disk schema; later Phase 10 slices (10C bootstrap contract, 10D attach/detach runtime, 10E bootstrap runtime, 10F target-side cycle dispatch, 10G external UI) build on both the Phase 10A and Phase 10B contracts and are tracked in `ROADMAP.md`.

Phase 9G (Final Human Acceptance And Polish Gate, complete) ships the explicit final human review, polish, and acceptance gate that prevents a fully autonomous Phase 9 PRD-to-product run from being treated as complete until human acceptance is recorded from canonical repo artifacts. The shipped Phase 9B/9C/9D/9E/9F surfaces can drive a PRD all the way to the Codex-approved terminal (`loop-state.status == "phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval"` and `last_verdict == "APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW"`); Phase 9G adds the load-bearing "but the human still has to look at it before we call it done" gate the Phase 9A autonomy contract requires. The library function `record_final_acceptance(repo_root, *, accepted_by, notes=None, output_path=None, log_path=None) -> Path` reads validated loop-state, requires the run to be at the Codex-approved terminal, validates `accepted_by` is a non-empty string (capped at `FINAL_ACCEPTANCE_MAX_ACCEPTED_BY_LENGTH = 200` chars; the canonical record of WHO accepted), validates optional `notes` is a bounded string (capped at `FINAL_ACCEPTANCE_MAX_NOTES_LENGTH = 8000` chars), and writes the canonical acceptance artifact at `.agent-loop/final-acceptance.json` carrying `acceptance_signal_version = "phase-9g-v1"`, `accepted_at` (UTC ISO-8601), `accepted_by`, a loop-state snapshot (`phase`, `sub_phase`, `task`, `cycle_count`, `last_verdict`) so the recorded acceptance is an auditable point-in-time decision, the operator-supplied `notes`, and the literal `FINAL_ACCEPTANCE_CANONICAL_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. A second library function `evaluate_final_acceptance(repo_root) -> dict` reads canonical artifacts only and returns one of three signals: `not_ready` (the run has not reached the Codex-approved terminal), `awaiting_final_human_acceptance` (Codex-approved terminal reached but no acceptance artifact yet, or the artifact is present but does not match the current loop-state), `final_acceptance_recorded` (artifact present and matches the current loop-state's phase / sub_phase / task / cycle_count / last_verdict / signal_version). The evaluator never mutates the artifact and reports a stale or corrupted artifact as `awaiting_final_human_acceptance` (with `acceptance_artifact_present=True` and `acceptance_artifact_matches_loop_state=False`) so a corrupt artifact never silently looks like a valid acceptance. The operator runtime entries are the new `record-final-acceptance` CLI subcommand (`python scripts/agent_loop.py record-final-acceptance --accepted-by NAME [--notes TEXT] [--output <repo-relative-path>]`) and the new `evaluate-final-acceptance` CLI subcommand (read-only inspector that prints a deterministic table and always exits 0, matching the Phase 7C `status` reporter pattern). Refuses fail-closed on missing / malformed loop-state, unsupported `contract_version`, loop-state.status != `phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval`, loop-state.last_verdict != `APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW`, missing / empty / non-string / over-bounded `--accepted-by`, non-string / over-bounded `--notes`, output-boundary violations via `FINAL_ACCEPTANCE_PROTECTED_OUTPUT_PATHS` (path outside `.agent-loop/`, protected sibling Phase 9 descriptor including the Phase 9F retry-state, memory subtree, directory), and an existing acceptance artifact on disk (Phase 9G refuses silent re-acceptance; the operator must explicitly delete the prior artifact to re-record, which leaves a manual audit trail). The Phase 9B / 9C / 9D / 9E / 9F protected sets were symmetrically extended to include `.agent-loop/final-acceptance.json` so no sibling Phase 9 slice can overwrite the acceptance artifact through its own output flag. `record_final_acceptance(...)` transitions `loop-state.status` from `phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval` to `phase_complete_final_human_accepted` (the new canonical accepted terminal) as part of recording so the canonical state model itself reflects accepted completion - not only the separate artifact path. The artifact is written first; if `save_loop_state` fails the artifact is on disk but loop-state still reads as the pre-acceptance terminal, and the evaluator reports `awaiting_final_human_acceptance` (torn state) so the gate fails loud rather than silently advancing on a partial signal. The recorder still does NOT touch any other loop-state field: recording acceptance is a gate, not an activation. The shipped Phase 4C activator + `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` human approval remain the only path to the next phase; Phase 9G never advances loop-state past the canonical accepted terminal. The Phase 9E `evaluate_phase_completion(...)` was extended to recognize EITHER the pre-acceptance terminal OR the canonical accepted terminal as a `completion_approved` signal so the long-run loop terminates cleanly across the recording transition without breaking existing Phase 9E callers (the signal vocabulary itself is unchanged; only the terminal-state coverage is widened). The `DISTILLATION_ELIGIBLE_STATUSES` frozenset (sister to the singular `DISTILLATION_ELIGIBLE_STATUS` preserved for backward compatibility) was widened the same way so phase-boundary memory distillation fires on either terminal. `evaluate_final_acceptance(...)` requires BOTH halves of the canonical accepted-state signal for the `recorded` result: the artifact must be present and match loop-state AND loop-state.status must be at `phase_complete_final_human_accepted`. A torn state (artifact present + matches but status not bumped, e.g. an operator hand-reset only one half) reports `awaiting_final_human_acceptance` with `acceptance_artifact_matches_loop_state=True` plus a "torn state" reason so the operator sees both halves of the reset are required. Re-recording requires resetting BOTH halves explicitly: delete the artifact AND reset loop-state.status back to the pre-acceptance terminal; both resets leave a manual audit trail and no `--force` flag is provided. Audit lines (`final acceptance: recorded ...`) land in `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`. The Phase 4 planner / Phase 4C activator boundary, the Phase 5 approval-mode runtime semantics, the Phase 5E reconciliation, the Phase 5F prompt bootstrap, the Phase 6 memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support behavior (including the Phase 6F token-exhaustion continuation primitive itself and the Phase 9F capacity-halt re-probe primitive), and the Phase 9B-9F surfaces are all preserved unchanged.

Phase 9F (Capacity-Halt Reprobe And Automatic Resume, complete) ships the bounded capacity-halt re-probe + automatic-resume layer for Claude/Codex token-quota or rate-limit exhaustion. The shipped runtime treats `halted_capacity_unavailable` as a resumable EXTERNAL-CAPACITY halt distinct from the Phase 6F token-exhaustion continuation seam (which writes a new continuation prompt and resumes within the same cycle); Phase 9F waits for the external capacity to return, re-probes through a configurable probe callable, then restores the pre-suspension loop-state `status` so the orchestrator resumes the exact suspended step the next time it runs. The library function `reprobe_capacity_and_resume(repo_root, *, max_attempts=None, initial_backoff_seconds=None, max_backoff_seconds=None, suspended_status=None, probe=None, sleep=None, output_path=None, log_path=None) -> Path` reads validated loop-state, requires `status == halted_capacity_unavailable`, loads or initializes the retry-state JSON at `.agent-loop/capacity-retry-state.json`, and runs up to `max_attempts` bounded retry attempts. Each attempt computes `backoff = min(initial_backoff_seconds * 2 ** (attempt_index - 1), max_backoff_seconds)` so the wait is exponentially bounded, invokes `sleep(backoff)` (default `time.sleep`; injected callable for tests), then invokes `probe(repo_root) -> bool`. The default probe is env-var-gated through `AGENT_LOOP_CAPACITY_PROBE`: it returns True only when the env var is set to a recognized truthy value (`available`, `1`, `true`, `yes`, `ok` - case-insensitive); any other value (or an unset env var) returns False so the bounded retry budget exhausts rather than silently auto-succeeding without a real availability check. Operators with a real capacity check inject a custom `probe` callable via the library entry point. On a successful probe the slice restores loop-state `status` from the retry-state's `suspended_status` field (default `claude_implementing`), deletes the retry-state file (canonical loop-state is the source of truth for the next orchestrator step), emits a `capacity reprobe: succeeded ...` audit line, and then dispatches the matching Claude prompt handoff so the original suspended step ACTUALLY RUNS rather than only restoring a non-terminal status string: `suspended_status == "claude_implementing"` dispatches `dispatch_prompt_handoff(repo_root, mode="implementation", ...)` (replays `.agent-loop/claude-prompt.md`); `suspended_status == "claude_fixing"` dispatches `dispatch_prompt_handoff(repo_root, mode="fix", ...)` (replays `.agent-loop/fix-prompt.md`). The supported allowlist is the constant `CAPACITY_RETRY_SUPPORTED_SUSPENDED_STATUSES = frozenset({"claude_implementing", "claude_fixing", "awaiting_codex_review"})`; the allowlist covers both Claude-side and Codex-side capacity exhaustion so the Phase 9F surface treats Claude AND Codex token / rate-limit exhaustion as resumable external-capacity halts. Claude-side resume dispatches the matching Claude prompt handoff (see above); the Codex-side resume for `awaiting_codex_review` actively dispatches the suspended Codex review via the shipped `make_codex_adapter().wait_for_review(.agent-loop/codex-review.md)` against the already-captured Claude summary + evidence so the suspended step actually runs (not merely restoring `loop-state.status`). The Codex adapter's reported `model_id` is persisted into `loop-state.codex_version` (mirroring the normal-cycle Codex review step contract); a non-zero adapter exit code is recorded into the orchestrator audit log but does NOT raise (matching the `dispatch_prompt_handoff` non-zero-exit pattern). Adapter invocation control is per-side: `invoke_claude_adapter` (default None -> falls back to `invoke_adapter`) gates the Claude-side dispatch (forwarded into `dispatch_prompt_handoff(...)`) and `invoke_codex_adapter` (default None -> falls back to `invoke_adapter`) gates the Codex-side `wait_for_review(...)` call. The global `invoke_adapter` (default True) remains as the back-compat single-toggle used by the standalone `run-capacity-reprobe` CLI's `--no-invoke-adapter` flag (skips BOTH dispatches at once); per-side overrides win when explicitly supplied. The Phase 9E long-run loop forwards both its `invoke_claude_adapter` / `invoke_codex_adapter` toggles (and the matching `--no-invoke-claude` / `--no-invoke-codex` CLI flags) into the reprobe call, so a Codex-side capacity resume correctly honors `--no-invoke-codex` (not `--no-invoke-claude`) and vice versa. `effective_invoke_codex=False` keeps the Codex-side branch descriptor-free / dispatch-free (audit records `handoff_mode='codex_review_pending'`) so tests and dry-run operators can preview the resume routing without consuming Codex capacity; `effective_invoke_claude=False` does the same for the Claude-side dispatch. The Phase 9F seam stops short of parsing the review or driving the verdict loop; `_handle_verdict_loop` remains the canonical owner of post-review routing, which the next orchestrator step (Phase 9E review/fix iteration via `run_internal_review_fix_cycle(...)` or the shipped Codex review continuation a normal cycle drives) consumes. Statuses outside the allowlist (e.g. `evidence_capture`) have no resume-continuation routing, so `record_capacity_halt(...)` refuses fail-closed when the resolved suspended status is outside this set (explicit override or inferred-from-current-loop-state alike) and `_validate_capacity_retry_state(...)` refuses fail-closed on a persisted retry-state that names an unsupported `suspended_status` (operator-tampered, legacy from before the allowlist landed, etc.). The dispatch step honors the new `invoke_adapter` kwarg (default True) which forwards into `dispatch_prompt_handoff(..., invoke_adapter=...)`: production callers actually invoke the shipped Claude adapter as part of the resume, while dry-run callers can pass `invoke_adapter=False` to write the descriptor-only handoff for inspection without consuming Claude capacity. The operator CLI surface exposes the same toggle via the new `--no-invoke-adapter` flag on `run-capacity-reprobe`. A second `capacity reprobe: resumed ...` audit line records the dispatched handoff mode together with the per-side effective flags `invoke_claude_adapter=...` and `invoke_codex_adapter=...` so the audit trail makes the per-side dispatch decision (Claude prompt handoff vs Codex review wait, dispatched vs descriptor-free) explicit on every resume. On a failed probe with budget remaining the slice persists the updated retry-state and continues to the next attempt within the same call. On budget exhausted the slice refuses fail-closed with `halted_input_missing`; the cumulative `attempt_count` field persists across operator invocations so re-running the CLI after a refusal does NOT reset the budget - deleting the retry-state file is the operator's explicit reset. The retry-state schema carries `retry_signal_version = "phase-9f-v1"`, `created_at`, `phase`, `sub_phase`, `task`, `cycle_count` (each loop-state-aligned for staleness refusal), `attempt_count`, `max_attempts`, `initial_backoff_seconds`, `max_backoff_seconds`, `suspended_status`, `history` (a list of per-attempt records with `attempt_index`, `attempted_at`, `backoff_seconds`, `probe_result`), `last_outcome`, and the literal `CAPACITY_RETRY_CANONICAL_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. Audit lines (`capacity reprobe: started ...`, `attempt_failed ...`, `succeeded ...`, `budget_exhausted ...`) land in `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`. The operator runtime entry is the new `run-capacity-reprobe` CLI subcommand (`python scripts/agent_loop.py run-capacity-reprobe [--max-attempts N] [--initial-backoff-seconds X] [--max-backoff-seconds Y] [--suspended-status S] [--output <repo-relative-path>]`). Refuses fail-closed on missing / malformed loop-state, unsupported `contract_version`, status not equal to `halted_capacity_unavailable`, out-of-bound `--max-attempts` (default `CAPACITY_RETRY_DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 5`, capped at `CAPACITY_RETRY_MAX_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 16`), out-of-bound `--initial-backoff-seconds` or `--max-backoff-seconds` (each must be > 0 and <= `CAPACITY_RETRY_MAX_MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS = 600`), `initial_backoff_seconds > max_backoff_seconds`, output-boundary violations via `CAPACITY_RETRY_PROTECTED_OUTPUT_PATHS` (path outside `.agent-loop/`, protected sibling Phase 9 descriptor, memory subtree, directory), a stale or malformed retry-state on disk (wrong `retry_signal_version`, mismatched `phase` / `sub_phase` / `task` / `cycle_count`, missing required field, wrong-typed required field), and an operator-supplied probe that raises an exception. The Phase 9B / 9C / 9D / 9E protected sets were symmetrically extended to include `.agent-loop/capacity-retry-state.json`. A second library function `record_capacity_halt(repo_root, *, suspended_status=None, reason=None, log_path=None) -> Path` and the matching `record-capacity-halt` CLI subcommand provide the production-path entry into the Phase 9F halt: the function saves the CURRENT loop-state `status` as the retry-state's `suspended_status` (unless overridden via `--suspended-status`), writes a fresh retry-state with `attempt_count = 0`, transitions loop-state `status` to `halted_capacity_unavailable`, and emits a `capacity reprobe: recorded ...` audit line. The function refuses fail-closed when loop-state is already at `halted_capacity_unavailable`, at any other `halted_*` status, or when a retry-state file already exists on disk. This is the seam a Claude/Codex adapter (or any runtime component that detects a capacity-exhaustion event) calls to enter Phase 9F without hand-editing `loop-state.json`. The Phase 9E long-run loop integrates the Phase 9F seam: when a hop encounters `halted_capacity_unavailable` (either pre-hop or after a Phase 9D iteration), the loop dispatches `reprobe_capacity_and_resume(repo_root, log_path=..., invoke_claude_adapter=invoke_claude_adapter, invoke_codex_adapter=invoke_codex_adapter)` (the long-run loop's per-side toggles forward directly into the reprobe call so a Claude-side resume honors `--no-invoke-claude` and a Codex-side resume honors `--no-invoke-codex`; previously the call only forwarded `invoke_claude_adapter` as a single `invoke_adapter` argument, which incorrectly routed Codex-side capacity resume through the Claude flag) rather than terminating on a generic `completion_halted`; on a successful Claude-side reprobe the loop continues to the next hop with restored loop-state AND the prompt-handoff descriptor at `.agent-loop/prompt-handoff.json` reflecting the resumed step (`mode = "implementation"` or `mode = "fix"`), on a successful Codex-side reprobe the loop continues with the restored `awaiting_codex_review` status plus a freshly-written `.agent-loop/codex-review.md` when `invoke_codex_adapter=True` (or the descriptor-free `handoff_mode='codex_review_pending'` routing decision when `invoke_codex_adapter=False`), on a refusal (budget exhausted, malformed retry-state, unsupported persisted suspended_status) the loop stops cleanly with `capacity_reprobe_rc` and `capacity_reprobe_halt_reason` captured in the hop record. The Phase 4 planner / Phase 4C activator boundary, the Phase 5 approval-mode runtime semantics, the Phase 5E reconciliation, the Phase 5F prompt bootstrap, the Phase 6 memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support behavior (including the Phase 6F token-exhaustion continuation primitive itself), and the Phase 9B-9E surfaces are all preserved unchanged. The Phase 9G final human acceptance + polish gate is shipped (see the Phase 9G paragraph above) and consumes the Codex-approved terminal Phase 9F's reprobe-and-resume flow produces.

Phase 9E (Long-Run Continuation And Completion Heuristics, complete) shipped the bounded long-run continuation layer that extends the shipped Phase 9B/9C/9D autonomous runtime across longer product-building runs with canonical-artifact "done enough" completion detection between hops. The library function `run_long_run_continuation(repo_root, *, max_hops=None, invoke_codex_adapter=True, invoke_claude_adapter=True, capture_evidence=True, output_path=None, log_path=None)` reads validated loop-state, validates `max_hops` (default `LONG_RUN_CONTINUATION_DEFAULT_MAX_HOPS = 2`, capped at `LONG_RUN_CONTINUATION_MAX_MAX_HOPS = 8`) and the output path, and runs up to `max_hops` hops. Each hop calls the new helper `evaluate_phase_completion(repo_root)` (which consults canonical `loop-state.status` + `last_verdict` only - no transcript or chat state) to determine whether the sub-phase is already complete; the helper returns one of three terminal completion signals (`completion_approved` when `status == phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval` and `last_verdict == APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW`, `completion_failed` when `status == halted_failed_requires_human` or `last_verdict == FAILED_REQUIRES_HUMAN`, `completion_halted` for any other `halted_*` status), or `None` when further work is required. On a pre-hop completion signal the hop is a no-op and the loop stops. Otherwise the hop invokes the shipped Phase 9D `run_internal_review_fix_cycle(repo_root, max_inner_cycles=1, ...)` (Phase 9E owns the hop counter; Phase 9D owns the per-iteration review/route/dispatch), captures the Phase 9D descriptor path (on success) or the `HaltError.status` + message (on a halt) into the hop record, then re-evaluates completion before continuing.
