A Phase 9D halt INSIDE a hop is recorded into the advisory descriptor but does NOT propagate out of Phase 9E - the operator inspects `.agent-loop/long-run-continuation.json` (and the Phase 9D `review-fix-loop.json` from prior hops) to determine what happened; Phase 9E never silently widens autonomy past a halt. Phase 6F token-exhaustion integration: when a hop entry (or a Phase 9D hop's post-completion check) finds loop-state at `halted_awaiting_token_exhaustion_continuation`, Phase 9E dispatches the shipped `run_token_exhaustion_resume(repo_root)` primitive (the same seam the `resume` / `auto-continue` CLI commands use) instead of treating the halt as a terminal stop; on rc=0 the resume restored loop-state and consumed one continuation-budget unit and the loop continues to the next hop, on rc!=0 the resume refused fail-closed (budget exhausted, malformed checkpoint, mismatching cycle identity, unsupported suspension reason) and Phase 9E stops cleanly with the refusal captured in the hop record's `token_exhaustion_resume_rc` field. The per-hop counter remains the upper bound on Phase 6F resume attempts. The advisory descriptor at `.agent-loop/long-run-continuation.json` carries `signal_version = "phase-9e-v1"`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `phase`, `sub_phase`, `task`, `hops` (each with `index`, `started_at`, `pre_hop_completion`, `action_taken` (`noop_already_complete` or `review_fix_iteration`), `phase_9d_descriptor`, `halt_status`, `halt_reason`, `post_hop_completion`), `hops_run`, `max_hops`, `completion` (the final completion determination), `capture_evidence`, `invoke_codex_adapter`, `invoke_claude_adapter`, `advisory_only = true`, and the literal `LONG_RUN_CONTINUATION_CANONICAL_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`. Audit lines (`long-run continuation: started ...`, `hop=N ...`, `completion_detected_at_entry ...`, `halted_in_hop ...`, `stop_after_hop ...`, `finished ...`) land in `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`. The operator runtime entry is the new `run-long-run-continuation` CLI subcommand (`python scripts/agent_loop.py run-long-run-continuation [--max-hops N] [--output <repo-relative-path>] [--skip-evidence] [--no-invoke-codex] [--no-invoke-claude]`); the three escape hatches forward into each Phase 9D hop. The output-write boundary mirrors the Phase 9B/9C/9D boundaries via `LONG_RUN_CONTINUATION_PROTECTED_OUTPUT_PATHS`: the slice refuses output paths outside `.agent-loop/`, paths targeting any orchestrator-owned runtime / Codex-owned planning / Phase 4 planner / Phase 5B routing / Phase 6N runtime-config artifact, paths under the Phase 6 `.agent-loop/memory/` subtree, paths that resolve to a directory, the Phase 9B intake artifact, the Phase 9C handoff descriptor, and the Phase 9D review-fix-loop descriptor. The Phase 9B/9C/9D protected sets were symmetrically extended to include `.agent-loop/long-run-continuation.json`. Canonical loop-state, prompt, summary, review, fix, checkpoint, and `claude-done.json` artifacts remain the source of truth; Phase 9E's descriptor is advisory routing/timing only. The Phase 4 planner / Phase 4C activator boundary (`APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` human approval remains the only path to the next phase), the Phase 5 approval-mode runtime semantics, the Phase 5E reconciliation, the Phase 5F prompt bootstrap, the Phase 6 memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support behavior, and the artifact-ownership boundaries are all preserved unchanged. Both Phase 9F (capacity-halt re-probe + automatic resume) and Phase 9G (final human acceptance + polish gate) have shipped in their own implementation slices; see the Phase 9F and Phase 9G paragraphs above.

Phase 9D (Autonomous Internal Review/Fix Loop, complete) shipped the runtime layer that consumes the Phase 9B intake plus Phase 9C prompt-handoff surfaces and lets the orchestrator drive the internal Codex review and Claude fix loop across bounded cycles without manual routing between agents. The library function `run_internal_review_fix_cycle(repo_root, *, max_inner_cycles=None, invoke_codex_adapter=True, invoke_claude_adapter=True, capture_evidence=True, output_path=None, log_path=None)` reads validated loop-state, structurally validates the Phase 9C handoff descriptor at `.agent-loop/prompt-handoff.json` (the descriptor must be valid JSON, carry `handoff_signal_version = "phase-9c-v1"`, and match the current loop-state's `phase` / `sub_phase` / `task` / `cycle_count`), validates `.agent-loop/claude-summary.md` against the active phase / sub-phase, and runs up to `max_inner_cycles` review/fix iterations (default `INTERNAL_REVIEW_FIX_LOOP_DEFAULT_MAX_INNER_CYCLES = 3` so the autonomous path actually continues past the first NEEDS_FIXES dispatch and reviews the result; capped at `INTERNAL_REVIEW_FIX_LOOP_MAX_MAX_INNER_CYCLES = 5`). Each iteration runs evidence capture (unless `capture_evidence=False`), invokes the shipped Codex adapter via `make_codex_adapter().wait_for_review(...)` (unless `invoke_codex_adapter=False`), parses the review via `parse_codex_review` (owner-routed through `_parse_review_issue_block`), persists `last_verdict` / `last_verdict_phase`, and branches: `APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW` sets `status=phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval` and stops; `FAILED_REQUIRES_HUMAN` halts; `NEEDS_FIXES` calls `_prepare_needs_fixes_follow_up` (regenerating `.agent-loop/fix-prompt.md` ONLY when Claude-owned issues remain), enforces the Phase 3A cycle threshold, increments `cycle_count`, and dispatches the next fix prompt through the shipped Phase 9C `dispatch_prompt_handoff(mode="fix", invoke_adapter=invoke_claude_adapter)`. Writes a single advisory descriptor at `.agent-loop/review-fix-loop.json` capturing per-iteration verdict, owner classification, fix-prompt refresh, and next-handoff dispatch. The operator runtime entry is the `run-internal-review-fix-cycle` CLI subcommand with `--max-inner-cycles`, `--output`, `--skip-evidence`, `--no-invoke-codex`, and `--no-invoke-claude` flags. The output-write boundary protects the Phase 9B intake artifact and Phase 9C handoff descriptor via `INTERNAL_REVIEW_FIX_LOOP_PROTECTED_OUTPUT_PATHS`. Canonical review and fix-prompt artifacts remain the source of truth; the slice never authors `codex-review.md` itself (Codex adapter's job) and only authors `fix-prompt.md` through the shipped owner-routing helper. The shipped Phase 4 planner / Phase 4C activator, Phase 5 approval-mode runtime, Phase 5E reconciliation, Phase 5F prompt bootstrap, Phase 6 memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support behavior, the cycle-threshold hard stops, and the artifact-ownership boundaries are all preserved unchanged.

Phase 9B (PRD Intake And Decomposition, complete) is the first runtime-building slice under the approved Phase 9A autonomy contract. The slice ships a canonical PRD-intake and decomposition surface that accepts either a structured PRD (JSON with `prd_kind = "structured_prd"`, `title`, `summary`, and a non-empty `requirements` list whose entries carry `id` / `title` / `description` and optional `tasks` / `risks` / `acceptance_criteria` fields) or a looser product brief (`prd_kind = "product_brief"` with `title` / `summary` / `narrative`) and decomposes it into bounded internal phases / tasks / risks / acceptance criteria that fit the shipped phase/task model rather than inventing a parallel control plane. The library function `intake_and_decompose_prd(repo_root, *, input_path, output_path=None, max_phases=None, max_tasks_per_phase=None, log_path=None)` validates inputs, decomposes, and writes a single canonical advisory artifact `.agent-loop/prd-intake.json` carrying `intake_signal_version = "phase-9b-v1"`, `created_at`, `source_input_kind`, `source_input_path` (repo-relative), `normalized_title`, `normalized_summary`, an ordered `decomposition.phases` list (each with `label` / `objective` / `tasks` / `risks` / `acceptance_criteria`), `decomposition.total_phases`, `decomposition.max_phases_applied`, `decomposition.max_tasks_per_phase_applied`, `advisory_only = true`, and the literal `PRD_INTAKE_CANONICAL_PRECEDENCE_NOTE` reminding consumers that the shipped Phase 4 planner and Phase 4C activator remain the only writers of canonical phase activation artifacts. The operator runtime entry is the new `intake-prd` CLI subcommand wired through `HANDLERS` (`python scripts/agent_loop.py intake-prd --input <repo-relative-path> [--output <repo-relative-path>] [--max-phases N] [--max-tasks-per-phase N]`); the handler resolves the operator-provided `--input` and `--output` paths against the repo root (refusing absolute paths and any path that resolves outside the repo via `..`), calls the library function, routes any `HaltError` through `_halt` so the structural refusal vocabulary lands on disk, and on success prints the written artifact path plus a pointer to the `prd intake:` audit-log line in `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`. Refusal modes (all fail-closed via `HaltError("halted_input_missing", ...)`): missing source file, source path is a directory, unreadable source, non-JSON source, top-level non-dict source, missing or unknown `prd_kind` (must be `"structured_prd"` or `"product_brief"`), missing / empty `title` or `summary`, structured PRD with a non-list / empty `requirements`, structured PRD with more requirements than `max_phases` (silent truncation is refused so the operator sees the boundary), per-requirement missing / empty / non-string `id` / `title` / `description`, per-requirement `risks` / `tasks` / `acceptance_criteria` that are not lists of non-empty strings, structured PRD with empty `acceptance_criteria` when the field is explicitly provided, product brief missing or empty `narrative`, product brief whose narrative produces no non-empty sections, product brief with more sections than `max_phases`, out-of-bound `max_phases` (zero, negative, non-int, bool, above `PRD_INTAKE_MAX_MAX_PHASES = 32`), out-of-bound `max_tasks_per_phase` (above `PRD_INTAKE_MAX_MAX_TASKS_PER_PHASE = 16`), repo-relative-path validation failures on `--input` / `--output`, and output-boundary violations (`--output` resolving outside `.agent-loop/`, `--output` targeting any path in `PRD_INTAKE_PROTECTED_OUTPUT_PATHS` such as `.agent-loop/loop-state.json` / `.agent-loop/current-task.md` / `.agent-loop/phase-plan.md` / `.agent-loop/claude-prompt.md` / the Phase 2A evidence files / the Phase 4 planner artifacts / the Phase 5B `claude-done.json` / the Phase 6N `runtime-config.json` / the orchestrator log, `--output` under the Phase 6 `.agent-loop/memory/` subtree, `--output` equal to the source input path, or `--output` resolving to a directory). The output-boundary check fires inside the library function so a direct Python caller using `intake_and_decompose_prd(...)` cannot bypass it through `output_path=...`. Canonical-precedence preservation: the slice never writes to `loop-state.json`, never writes to `phase-plan.md` / `TASK.md` / `current-task.md` / `current-phase.md`, never mutates the source input file, and only writes to the named output path plus a single audit-log line when `log_path` is supplied (no log file is created when `log_path` is omitted). The Phase 4 planner / activator boundary, the Phase 5 approval-mode runtime semantics, the Phase 6 memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support behavior, and the artifact-ownership boundaries are all preserved unchanged; autonomous review/fix execution (Phase 9D), automatic next-phase activation (Phase 9D / 9E), long-run completion heuristics (Phase 9E), capacity-halt re-probe (Phase 9F), and final human acceptance automation (Phase 9G) remain deferred.

Phase 9C (Orchestrator-Driven Prompt Handoff, complete) shipped the runtime handoff layer that dispatches the active Claude prompt from canonical repo artifacts on disk and captures the resulting handoff audit trail without requiring manual copy/paste. The library function `dispatch_prompt_handoff(repo_root, *, mode=None, output_path=None, log_path=None, invoke_adapter=True)` reads the validated loop-state, selects the active prompt artifact (`mode="implementation"` -> `.agent-loop/claude-prompt.md`; `mode="fix"` -> `.agent-loop/fix-prompt.md`; `mode=None` -> auto-detect from `last_verdict` so `NEEDS_FIXES` routes to the fix prompt and every other state routes to the implementation prompt), validates the selected prompt artifact via the shipped `validate_claude_prompt_present` (refuses fail-closed on missing or empty content), writes the structured handoff descriptor to `.agent-loop/prompt-handoff.json`, audits a `prompt handoff: dispatched ...` line to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`, and unless `invoke_adapter=False` (operator dry-run / planning mode) invokes the shipped Claude adapter via `make_claude_adapter().invoke(prompt_path, summary_path)`. The adapter call uses the same shipped factory `cmd_run` uses (the configured `SubprocessClaudeAdapter` when `AGENT_LOOP_CLAUDE_CMD` is set, else the `ManualHandoffClaudeAdapter` fallback) and the same canonical Claude-summary path `.agent-loop/claude-summary.md` `cmd_run` writes to. The adapter's `ExecutionResult` (`exit_code`, `model_id`, `duration_seconds`) is captured into the descriptor's `adapter_invocation` block (alongside the `adapter_invoked = true` flag) and a second `prompt handoff: invoked ...` audit line records the outcome. The descriptor's schema-stable keys are `handoff_signal_version = "phase-9c-v1"`, `dispatched_at`, `mode`, `source_prompt_path` (repo-relative), `source_prompt_byte_size`, `phase`, `sub_phase`, `task`, `cycle_count`, `approval_mode`, `last_verdict`, `advisory_only = true`, `canonical_precedence_note` (the literal `PROMPT_HANDOFF_CANONICAL_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`), `adapter_invoked`, and `adapter_invocation` (null when `invoke_adapter=False`; otherwise `{exit_code, model_id, duration_seconds, summary_path}`). The operator runtime entry is the `dispatch-prompt-handoff` CLI subcommand (`python scripts/agent_loop.py dispatch-prompt-handoff [--mode {implementation,fix}] [--output <repo-relative-path>] [--no-invoke]`); the `--no-invoke` flag flips to descriptor-only behavior so an operator can preview the dispatch without invoking Claude. The handler routes operator paths through `_resolve_prd_intake_path` (refusing absolute paths and `..` escapes), calls the library function, routes any `HaltError` through `_halt`, and on success prints the written descriptor path plus a pointer to the audit-log lines in `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`. The output-write boundary mirrors the Phase 9B intake boundary via `PROMPT_HANDOFF_PROTECTED_OUTPUT_PATHS`: the handoff refuses output paths outside `.agent-loop/`, paths targeting any orchestrator-owned runtime / Codex-owned planning / Phase 4 planner / Phase 5B routing / Phase 6N runtime-config artifact, paths under the Phase 6 `.agent-loop/memory/` subtree, paths that resolve to a directory, and the Phase 9B intake artifact `.agent-loop/prd-intake.json` (cross-slice protection so a handoff dispatch cannot overwrite an intake artifact). The Phase 9B intake's protected set was symmetrically extended to include `.agent-loop/prompt-handoff.json`. The library function enforces the boundary so direct Python callers cannot bypass it. The canonical prompt artifacts on disk remain the source of truth: the slice never modifies them, never updates `loop-state.json`, never runs evidence collection, never waits for Codex review, and never advances cycle_count. Those responsibilities continue to live in the shipped `cmd_run` / `cmd_resume` / `cmd_auto_continue` cycle drivers. A non-zero adapter exit code is recorded into the descriptor and audit log but does NOT raise (matching the shipped pattern where the adapter's exit code is a signal that subsequent validators act on). The Phase 4 planner / Phase 4C activator boundary, the Phase 5 approval-mode runtime semantics, the Phase 5B `claude-done.json` routing signal, the Phase 5E post-review reconciliation, the Phase 5F phase-start prompt bootstrap, and the Phase 6 memory / checkpoint / continuation / runtime-adapter / LangChain support behavior are all preserved unchanged; automatic next-phase activation (Phase 9E), long-run completion heuristics (Phase 9E), capacity-halt re-probe (Phase 9F), and final human acceptance automation (Phase 9G) remain deferred.

Phase 9A (Autonomous Mode Contract And Safety Policy, complete) shipped the documentation-only contract for the future selectable fully autonomous PRD-to-product mode without implementing the runtime that drives it. The contract lives at `docs/autonomy-contract.md` and explicitly states that the runtime is NOT yet implemented; the runtime work is deferred to Phase 9B (PRD intake), Phase 9C (orchestrator-driven prompt handoff), Phase 9D (autonomous review/fix loop), Phase 9E (long-run continuation), Phase 9F (capacity-halt re-probe), and Phase 9G (final human acceptance and polish gate). The contract distinguishes the future Phase 9 mode from the shipped Phase 5D `autonomous` approval mode (a narrow intra-phase strict-gate bypass that runs Codex / Claude implementation and fix cycles within a single already-active phase, without planning or advancing the next phase) so the two are not collapsed into one mode name; the contract names the three Phase 5C gates (`pre_claude_prompt`, `pre_fix_prompt`, `pre_codex_review`) that Phase 5D bypasses so a reader sees what the shipped narrow autonomous behavior actually does. The contract documents (a) allowed actions inside the future Phase 9 mode (PRD intake, orchestrator-driven prompt handoff, automatic Codex review and Claude fix cycles within the shipped `cycle_count` / `max_cycles` threshold, automatic next-phase activation on a terminal approved verdict, token-exhaustion continuation via the shipped Phase 6F/6G primitives, capacity-halt re-probe and resume, final human acceptance / polish stop), (b) preserved hard stops (never commits to Git, never pushes, `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` gate preserved unchanged until Phase 9D ships an internally-derived path, immediate halt on `FAILED_REQUIRES_HUMAN` / `halted_failed_requires_human`, immediate halt on `cycle_count >= max_cycles` for `NEEDS_FIXES` / `halted_max_cycles_reached`, every structural halt preserved, operator SIGINT preserved as `halted_human_stop`, final human acceptance / polish gate required), (c) audit expectations (every internal phase advance recorded in `phase-plan.md` and `loop-state.json`, every Codex-to-Claude handoff recorded via the shipped `claude-done.json` routing signal, every review verdict recorded in the corresponding `codex-review.md`, every checkpoint hop recorded under `.agent-loop/memory/checkpoint/`, every capacity-halt re-probe recorded with bounded metadata, every Phase 5E auto-fix application recorded with the existing `review reconciliation:` audit line, final human acceptance recorded as an explicit artifact write), and (d) safety boundaries the mode must refuse to cross (Git mutation, modification of `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` / `ROADMAP.md`, skipping the final acceptance gate, silently widening autonomy across runs, retrying capacity-halts forever, claiming completion without objective signals). The Phase 9A slice shipped no new runtime, planner, activator, evidence-collection, review-routing, checkpoint, continuation, memory, runtime-adapter, LangChain, or VS Code feature work; the only code change was an extension of `tests/test_documentation_consistency.py` that adds focused-test guardrails proving the contract doc exists, is ASCII-only, and is non-empty; every `python scripts/agent_loop.py <subcommand>` mention resolves to a real `agent_loop.HANDLERS` entry; the contract explicitly states the runtime is "NOT yet implemented" so a clean-clone reader cannot mistake it for shipped behavior; the contract names Phase 5D (shipped reference) plus the three Phase 5C gates so the distinction from the shipped narrow `autonomous` mode is discoverable; the contract names Phase 9B / 9D / 9F / 9G so the locus of each future runtime piece is discoverable; the contract preserves the no-Git-automation boundary, the `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` token, both human-required terminal halts, and the final human acceptance gate by literal name.

Phase 8C (Final README Alignment And Clean-Clone Polish, complete) was the final documentation/polish slice. It aligned the README, examples, and clean-clone getting-started path with the shipped CLI/runtime behavior and the already-delivered Phase 8A/8B docs without collapsing roadmap-only capabilities into present-tense product behavior. Phase 8B (Safety, Approval, And Operational Playbooks, complete) ships three new operator-facing playbook documents that explain the shipped safety model, approval semantics, halt reasons, recovery paths, and troubleshooting guidance from a clean clone. `docs/safety-rules.md` covers the repo-artifact source-of-truth model, the no-git-automation boundary, the human-gated `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` token at phase transitions, the recovery-preserving `HaltError` pattern, the artifact-ownership partition (orchestrator-owned / Codex-owned / Claude-owned), the Phase 2A Evidence Collection Contract state vocabulary (`Passed` / `Failed` / `Not run` / `Inconclusive`), the orchestrator audit trail, and an explicit "what the safety model does NOT promise" section so operators do not over-trust the system. `docs/approval-modes.md` is a per-mode playbook for `review` (default), `strict`, and `autonomous`: what each mode does, the Phase 5B `claude-done.json` handoff artifact, the three Phase 5C strict-mode gates (`pre_claude_prompt`, `pre_fix_prompt`, `pre_codex_review` with two halt-status flavors for routing), an explicit note that strict-mode halts do NOT write strict-gate checkpoint artifacts (only the Phase 6F token-exhaustion path does), the `autonomous`-mode bypass-with-audit-note pattern, the hard stops every mode preserves, and a disclaimer that `autonomous` is NOT the Phase 9 fully-autonomous PRD-to-product mode. `docs/halt-and-recovery.md` is the operator-facing mirror of `STATUS_RECOVERY_HINTS` in `scripts/agent_loop.py`: every shipped status (in-flight cycle states, the four Phase 5C strict-mode halt-status flavors covering the three shipped gates `pre_claude_prompt` / `pre_fix_prompt` / `pre_codex_review` with two halt-status flavors on `pre_codex_review` so resume can route correctly, the Phase 6F token-exhaustion halt, the operator-SIGINT halt, the seven structural halts, the two human-required terminal halts, and the `phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval` success terminal) gets a dedicated section naming the meaning and the matching recovery path. The `halted_failed_requires_human` and `halted_max_cycles_reached` sections explicitly state that the shipped planner refuses to propose the next phase from those states, so no operator CLI command unblocks them. All three playbooks distinguish shipped behavior from `ROADMAP.md` and refuse to present Phase 9 / Phase 10 capabilities as currently shipped. The slice ships no new runtime, planner, activator, evidence-collection, review-routing, checkpoint, continuation, memory, runtime-adapter, LangChain, or VS Code feature work; the only code change is an extension of `tests/test_documentation_consistency.py` that adds focused-test guardrails proving (a) each playbook exists, is ASCII-only, and is non-empty, (b) every `python scripts/agent_loop.py <subcommand>` mention in any playbook resolves to a real `agent_loop.HANDLERS` entry, (c) the safety doc names the load-bearing safety boundaries by literal name, (d) the approval-modes doc names every shipped approval mode (via `APPROVAL_MODE_*` constants), every Phase 5C strict-mode gate, both `pre_codex_review` halt-status flavors, the Phase 5B `claude-done.json` artifact, and the strict-mode "no strict-gate checkpoint artifacts" boundary, (e) the halt/recovery doc has a section for every status in the canonical `STATUS_RECOVERY_HINTS` map (the doc fails closed if it lags the canonical map), names the three Codex verdicts, and explains that the planner refuses to propose the next phase from the human-required terminal halts, and (f) no playbook presents Phase 9 / MCP / external UI work as a current shipped capability.

Phase 8A (Architecture And Usage Docs, complete) ships two new operator-facing documentation files describing the system as it actually behaves today from a clean clone: `docs/architecture.md` covers the three-actor agent model (Codex planner/reviewer, Claude implementer, local orchestrator), the per-cycle loop lifecycle (Phase 3A Orchestrator Contract), the phase model and the human-gated Phase 4C activation step, the three shipped approval modes (`review` / `strict` / `autonomous`), the full operator-visible halt vocabulary (in-flight, terminal, strict-mode gates, token-exhaustion, operator-interrupt, structural), the Phase 6N runtime adapter seam with the local default and the opt-in LangGraph mirror, the Phase 6A-6L memory and checkpoint layers, the Phase 6J/6K optional-context and Phase 6O LangChain support layers, the artifact-ownership boundary mirroring `CLAUDE.md` (orchestrator-owned vs Codex-owned vs Claude-owned), and the load-bearing safety boundaries (no auto-commit, no auto-push, recovery-preserving halts, repo-artifact source-of-truth). `docs/usage.md` walks a new operator from a clean clone through the shipped CLI surface (grouped into inspection / cycle-execution / planning-activation / configuration-advisory subsets), the clean-clone setup with the operator-side env vars (`AGENT_LOOP_CLAUDE_CMD` / `AGENT_LOOP_CODEX_CMD` / `AGENT_LOOP_*_MODEL` / `AGENT_LOOP_*_CMD` for evidence), the first-cycle walkthrough, the common operator flows (drive a cycle, recover from each halt family, generate a proposal, activate, inspect, reset runtime config), and the VS Code operator entrypoints. Both docs distinguish shipped behavior from the future `ROADMAP.md` scope (Phase 9 Fully Autonomous PRD-To-Product Mode and Phase 10 Future Product Features are explicitly marked roadmap-only). The slice ships no new runtime, planner, activator, evidence-collection, review-routing, checkpoint, continuation, memory, runtime-adapter, LangChain, or VS Code feature work; the only code change is `tests/test_documentation_consistency.py`, a focused-test file that asserts the new docs exist, are ASCII-only, only name `agent_loop.HANDLERS` subcommands the shipped CLI actually exposes (so the docs cannot drift into claiming non-existent commands), cover the load-bearing operator subcommands by invocation, name every orchestrator-owned artifact called out by `CLAUDE.md`, name the three approval modes, the three Codex verdicts, the shipped runtime adapter ids, the five memory categories, and the four strict-mode halt vocabulary entries, and do not present unshipped behavior (MCP, external UI, fully autonomous PRD-to-product) as current shipped capabilities. Phase 8B (Safety, Approval, And Operational Playbooks, complete) added the operator-facing playbooks for halt reasons, approval semantics, recovery paths, and troubleshooting guidance while preserving the shipped CLI-first workflow and artifact source-of-truth boundaries. The later Phase 9 contract work is now complete through Phase 9F, and the current active slice is Phase 9G final human acceptance and polish gate.

Phase 7B (Artifact Inspection And Review Workflow, shipped) adds a thin read-only artifact-inspection layer plus a workspace-settings layer on top of the shipped Phase 7A VS Code task entrypoints. The slice consists of (1) a new `inspect_artifacts(repo_root) -> list[dict]` library function and `cmd_inspect_artifacts` CLI handler in `scripts/agent_loop.py` that report the on-disk presence, byte size, and ISO-8601 UTC last-modified timestamp of the active review artifacts (`.agent-loop/codex-review.md`, `.agent-loop/claude-prompt.md`, `.agent-loop/fix-prompt.md`), the active planning artifacts (`.agent-loop/current-task.md`, `.agent-loop/current-phase.md`), and the six shipped Phase 2A/2B evidence files (`.agent-loop/git-status.log`, `git-diff.patch`, `test-output.log`, `lint-output.log`, `typecheck-output.log`, `build-output.log`); (2) supporting constants `INSPECTION_SIGNAL_VERSION = "phase-7b-v1"`, four `INSPECTION_GROUP_*` labels (`review`, `prompt`, `planning`, `evidence`), the ordered `INSPECTION_ARTIFACTS` tuple of `(group, repo-relative-path)` rows, and the ordered `INSPECTION_GROUPS` tuple; (3) the new `inspect-artifacts` CLI subcommand wired through `HANDLERS` so the same dispatch surface drives `python scripts/agent_loop.py inspect-artifacts` and the matching VS Code task; (4) a new `Agent Loop: inspect artifacts` task in `.vscode/tasks.json` that delegates to the shipped CLI under the same thin-process-wrapper pattern as the rest of the Phase 7A task set; (5) a new `.vscode/settings.json` workspace settings file that improves artifact-inspection ergonomics with a `files.associations` entry mapping `*.patch` files to the `diff` viewer (so the captured `git-diff.patch` renders as a diff rather than plain text) and an `explorer.fileNesting.patterns` block that groups related artifacts under their parents (`fix-prompt.md` + `claude-summary.md` + `claude-done.json` under `claude-prompt.md`, `current-task.md` under `current-phase.md`, `codex-summary.md` under `codex-review.md`, `runtime-config.json` under `loop-state.json`, and the five other evidence files under `git-status.log`); and (6) a checked-in operator acceptance checklist at `docs/vscode-artifact-inspection-checklist.md` that covers the remaining live-editor behaviors a headless test run cannot prove (terminal linkification, Explorer nesting, and diff-viewer rendering). The inspector is purely read-only: it never mutates a canonical artifact, never creates the orchestrator log, never writes anywhere on disk, and exits 0 regardless of which artifacts are present. The output is a deterministic plain-text table whose paths are repo-relative so a VS Code task terminal auto-linkifies every row. The workspace settings file is a thin operator convenience layer: it declares no `python.defaultInterpreterPath`, no terminal-shell override, and no other runtime-influencing key (locked in by a focused test), so VS Code cannot silently change how the shipped CLI runs through it. The CLI-first contract is preserved: every Phase 7B surface delegates to existing repo artifacts and shipped commands, the repository remains fully usable without VS Code, and the inspection workflow is not a control plane.

Phase 7C (Status, Reset, And Recovery UX, shipped) makes the active loop state, the recovery path for each halt, and the persisted-runtime reset surface easy to drive from VS Code through a thin read-only status reporter, a per-status recovery hint map, and operator-facing task wrappers around the already-shipped reset/recovery CLI surfaces, all without changing the orchestrator's recovery semantics.

Phase 7A (VS Code Task Entrypoints, shipped) adds `.vscode/tasks.json` with thin VS Code task wrappers around the most common shipped CLI operator flows so the loop is easier to drive from the editor without changing the underlying CLI-first runtime contract. The slice ships nine tasks - `Agent Loop: check state` (`check-state`), `collect evidence` (the Phase 2B evidence producer `scripts/run_checks.sh`), `validate artifacts` (`validate-artifacts`), `run cycle` (`run`), `resume strict-mode gate` (`resume`), `auto-continue` (`auto-continue`), `plan next phase` (`plan`), `activate approved phase` (`activate`), and `bootstrap claude prompt` (`bootstrap-prompt`). Eight of the nine wrap the shipped `agent_loop.py` CLI: `type` is `"process"` (no shell parsing, no inline scripting), `command` is `"python"`, `args[0]` is `scripts/agent_loop.py`, `args[1]` is a subcommand that exists in `agent_loop.HANDLERS`, and `cwd` is `${workspaceFolder}` so the tasks do not depend on the operator's terminal CWD. The ninth is the dedicated evidence-collection task: it wraps the shipped Phase 2B evidence producer at `scripts/run_checks.sh` rather than the `validate-artifacts` gate which only inspects already-produced evidence; structurally `type` is `"process"`, `command` is `"bash"`, `args` is `["scripts/run_checks.sh"]`, and `cwd` is `${workspaceFolder}` (on Windows hosts VS Code resolves `bash` through Git Bash; on macOS / Linux through the system bash; the task type stays `process` so no shell parsing of args happens either way). Every task corresponds to an existing documented CLI surface (the shipped `agent_loop.py` HANDLERS set or the shipped `scripts/run_checks.sh` Phase 2B producer); the tasks file never inlines a second interpreter inside args, never adds positional file arguments the underlying CLI does not document, and never introduces editor-owned runtime behavior beyond delegating to the shipped command. Canonical repo-artifact truth, halt and refusal vocabulary, approval-mode behavior, checkpoint/continuation behavior, and artifact ownership boundaries are all preserved by virtue of the tasks layer being a thin wrapper rather than an alternative orchestrator. The repository remains fully usable without VS Code: the orchestrator surface is byte-equivalent regardless of the presence of the tasks file, and `agent_loop.HANDLERS` continues to be the single source of truth for the operator CLI.

Phase 7C (Status, Reset, And Recovery UX, shipped) builds on the shipped Phase 7A task entrypoints and Phase 7B inspection layer with a thin operator-facing status and recovery UX surface. The slice consists of (1) the new constants `STATUS_SIGNAL_VERSION = "phase-7c-v1"`, the `STATUS_RECOVERY_HINTS` dict mapping every operator-visible shipped status to a one-line actionable next-step hint (the strict-mode gates, the Phase 6F token-exhaustion halt, the Phase 5B/D terminal `phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval` state, the structural halts, the operator-SIGINT `halted_human_stop`, the in-flight cycle states `claude_implementing` / `claude_fixing` / `evidence_capture` / `awaiting_codex_review` that an operator can observe between adapter handoffs, plus the terminal / threshold halts `halted_evidence_incomplete` / `halted_evidence_script_unavailable` / `halted_failed_requires_human` / `halted_max_cycles_reached`). The hints for `halted_failed_requires_human` and `halted_max_cycles_reached` describe the contract-accurate manual recovery path (human triage of `.agent-loop/codex-review.md` and `.agent-loop/claude-summary.md`, followed by a fresh Codex-owned activation prompt) rather than directing the operator at `agent_loop.py plan` / `activate` directly, because the shipped planner contractually refuses to propose the next phase from any `halted_*` status, from `last_verdict == FAILED_REQUIRES_HUMAN`, and from `cycle_count >= max_cycles` on `NEEDS_FIXES`, and the `STATUS_RECOVERY_FALLBACK` catch-all string for any unmapped status; (2) a new `compute_status_summary(repo_root) -> dict` library function that always returns a dict (never raises) carrying the active phase / sub_phase / task / status / approval_mode / cycle_count / max_cycles / last_verdict / awaiting_human_for / active-checkpoint presence plus a `recovery_hint` field on every code path, with an explicit `load_error` field surfacing missing or malformed loop-state instead of crashing; (3) the new `_render_status_summary(summary)` helper producing a deterministic plain-text table (signal-version header, one row per state field, trailing `Recovery hint: ...` line); (4) the new `cmd_status` CLI handler wired through `HANDLERS` so the same dispatch surface drives `python scripts/agent_loop.py status` and the matching VS Code task, always returning 0 even on missing or malformed state; (5) a new `Agent Loop: status` task in `.vscode/tasks.json` that delegates to the new subcommand; and (6) a new `Agent Loop: reset runtime config` task that wraps the already-shipped Phase 6N `python scripts/agent_loop.py set-runtime-config --clear` recovery surface (idempotent rc=0; returns the orchestrator to the default `local` runtime). The status reporter is purely read-only: it never mutates a canonical artifact, never creates the orchestrator log, never writes anywhere on disk. The reset task is the only operator-facing reset surface in this slice and it delegates byte-for-byte to the existing Phase 6N CLI; no new write-side recovery command is introduced. Halt and refusal vocabulary, approval-mode behavior, checkpoint/continuation behavior, and artifact ownership boundaries are all preserved by virtue of the status reporter being a reporter (not a controller) and the reset wrapper delegating to the already-shipped recovery surface. Artifact dashboards, broader Phase 7+ editor integration scope, and MCP support remain deferred.

Phase 7B (Artifact Inspection And Review Workflow, shipped) makes the active review artifacts (`.agent-loop/codex-review.md`, `.agent-loop/fix-prompt.md`, `.agent-loop/claude-prompt.md`), the active planning artifacts (`.agent-loop/current-task.md`, `.agent-loop/current-phase.md`), and the shipped Phase 2A/2B evidence files easy to open and inspect from VS Code through a thin read-only inspector, a workspace settings layer, and a checked-in operator acceptance checklist - all without changing the orchestrator's ownership, halt, approval, or artifact-truth rules.

Phase 6O (LangChain Support Layer, shipped) adds a narrow optional support layer for prompt construction, selective retrieval, and tool abstraction on top of the shipped Phase 6N runtime-adapter seam. The layer is NOT a runtime owner: the shipped local orchestrator remains the default runtime, the Phase 6N LangGraph mirror remains the only opt-in alternate runtime, and the 6O helpers never drive `cmd_run` / `cmd_resume` / `cmd_auto_continue`. The layer consists of (1) the constants `LANGCHAIN_SUPPORT_SIGNAL_VERSION = "phase-6o-v1"`, `LANGCHAIN_SUPPORT_ENV_VAR = "AGENT_LOOP_LANGCHAIN_SUPPORT"`, `LANGCHAIN_SUPPORT_AUDIT_NOTE_PREFIX = "langchain support:"`, `LANGCHAIN_SUPPORT_TRUTHY_VALUES = frozenset({"1", "true", "yes", "on"})`, `LANGCHAIN_SUPPORT_FALSY_VALUES = frozenset({"0", "false", "no", "off", ""})`, `LANGCHAIN_SUPPORT_TOOL_NAMES = ("read_loop_state", "list_memory_entries", "load_active_checkpoint", "retrieve_memory_entries")`, `LANGCHAIN_SUPPORT_OUTPUT_REL = ".agent-loop/langchain-support.json"`, and `LANGCHAIN_SUPPORT_CANONICAL_PRECEDENCE_NOTE` (literal advisory-precedence reminder consumers compare by equality); (2) `is_langchain_support_enabled(cli_flag, env_value)` resolving the opt-in with CLI-flag precedence over env, case-insensitive recognition of the truthy / falsy sets, default off, and fail-closed refusal via `HaltError("halted_input_missing", ...)` on any unrecognized env value (so a typo surfaces as a refusal rather than a silent default); (3) the `_ensure_langchain_support_enabled(*, enabled)` guard that refuses fail-closed when a helper is invoked without opt-in (importing or instantiating helpers is safe; only calling a helper method raises); (4) `LangChainPromptHelper` building a structured advisory-only `build_prompt_payload(...)` dict from canonical loop-state with a `support_signal_version`, `built_at`, `advisory_only=True`, the literal canonical-precedence note, a `canonical_state` subset of loop-state, and a deterministic `system_prompt_block`; re-reads loop-state on every call (no caching) and re-raises every shipped-validator `HaltError` verbatim (no new halt status); (5) `LangChainRetrievalHelper.retrieve(...)` wrapping the shipped Phase 6C `retrieve_memory_entries(...)` and preserving the `MEMORY_RETRIEVAL_ADVISORY_FIELD = True` marker on every returned entry (refuses fail-closed if any entry is missing the marker - defense in depth against a hand-edited memory entry); (6) `LangChainToolRegistry` exposing a static set of four named READ-ONLY tools (no write-side tools in this slice; `list_tools()` returns one dict per tool; `invoke(tool_name, **kwargs)` dispatches to the matching shipped inspector or refuses on an unknown name); (7) the `langchain-support-eval` CLI subcommand with `--enable-langchain-support` flag (default off, refuses with rc=2 via `_halt` when not opted in, also consults the `AGENT_LOOP_LANGCHAIN_SUPPORT` env var; on opt-in writes the combined `{prompt_payload, tools_registered, support_signal_version, advisory_only, canonical_precedence_note, evaluated_at}` artifact to `.agent-loop/langchain-support.json` and emits a `langchain support: eval written ...` audit note). Default-runtime preservation: the support layer never modifies the Phase 6N runtime selection, never instantiates the LangGraph mirror, never mutates the persisted runtime-config artifact, and never invokes the cycle drivers (proven by mock spies in the focused test suite). Canonical-precedence preservation: the layer is read-only against canonical state and writes only to `.agent-loop/langchain-support.json` (an audit artifact regenerable from canonical state) and to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` (audit notes only). Refusal vocabulary: every refusal uses the existing `halted_input_missing` halt status; no new halt status is introduced. The layer is a pure-stdlib structural support layer; the real `langchain` package is intentionally NOT a runtime dependency in this slice, mirroring the Phase 6N pure-stdlib LangGraph emulation pattern. A future slice can swap the helper implementations behind the same interface to wire the real `langchain` package without changing the contract surface. Promotion of the support layer into the runtime control plane, CrewAI evaluation, write-side tools in the registry, broader multi-framework orchestration, and any LangChain-managed state overriding canonical state remain deferred (refused fail-closed; the layer is structurally subordinate per the Phase 6M contract).

Phase 6N (Experimental LangGraph Runtime Mirror, shipped) adds a narrow opt-in runtime-adapter seam that exercises the Phase 6M contract in code while preserving the existing local orchestrator at `scripts/agent_loop.py` as the default runtime. The seam consists of (1) the constants `RUNTIME_ADAPTER_DEFAULT = "local"`, `RUNTIME_ADAPTER_LANGGRAPH = "langgraph"`, `RUNTIME_ADAPTERS_SUPPORTED = frozenset({"local", "langgraph"})`, `RUNTIME_ADAPTER_ENV_VAR = "AGENT_LOOP_RUNTIME"`, `RUNTIME_ADAPTER_AUDIT_NOTE_PREFIX = "runtime adapter:"`, `LANGGRAPH_RUNTIME_ID = "langgraph_experimental"`, and `LANGGRAPH_NODE_NAMES = ("load_state", "validate_state", "consult_memory", "consult_checkpoint", "evaluate")`; (2) `resolve_runtime_adapter_id(cli_value, env_value)` which returns the default `local` when both inputs are unset / empty, lets CLI override env, and refuses fail-closed via `HaltError("halted_input_missing", ...)` on any id outside the supported set; (3) `LocalRuntimeAdapter` (the default sentinel - the shipped in-process flow IS the local runtime; selecting `local` and calling `evaluate(...)` returns `{"runtime_id": "local", "outcome": "local_default_unchanged"}` and emits one `runtime adapter: local local_default_sentinel` audit note when `log_path` is supplied, never reading or mutating any canonical artifact); (4) `LangGraphExperimentalRuntimeAdapter` - the opt-in mirror, a pure-stdlib structural emulation of LangGraph's StateGraph pattern that walks `LANGGRAPH_NODE_NAMES` in fixed order, re-loads loop-state from disk on every node entry (no caching across hops per the 6M framework-state subordination rule), inspects canonical state through the shipped `load_loop_state` / `validate_loop_state` / `check_contract_version` / `list_memory_entries` / `_load_active_checkpoint` validators, emits a `runtime adapter: langgraph_experimental ...` audit note per node plus begin / complete book-end notes, never writes to canonical artifacts, and re-raises every shipped-validator `HaltError` verbatim (no new halt status); (5) `make_runtime_adapter(runtime_id)` factory that returns the matching adapter or refuses fail-closed; (6) the `runtime-adapter-eval` CLI subcommand with `--runtime` flag that resolves the runtime id, instantiates the matching adapter, calls `evaluate(...)`, routes refusals through `_halt` (rc=2), and on success prints a summary plus a pointer to the audit log; (7) the persisted default-off runtime-selection artifact at `.agent-loop/runtime-config.json` (signal_version `phase-6n-v1`; absence = default off; written through `write_runtime_config(repo_root, selected_runtime)` and cleared through `clear_runtime_config(repo_root)`; the structural reader `read_runtime_config(repo_root)` returns the selected id on success or refuses fail-closed via `HaltError("halted_input_missing", ...)` on every malformed shape (non-file, unreadable, non-JSON, top-level not dict, missing required key, unrecognized signal_version, `selected_runtime` outside the supported set)); (8) the `set-runtime-config` CLI subcommand with a mutually-exclusive `--runtime <id>` / `--clear` flag pair (operators MAY pin a long-running selection without re-typing the CLI flag on each invocation; clearing is idempotent rc=0; both paths emit `runtime adapter:` audit notes); (9) the new `_resolve_runtime_with_persisted(repo_root, cli_value, env_value)` precedence resolver that chains CLI > env > persisted config > default `local`; (10) wiring through `_apply_runtime_selection(repo_root, args, *, hop_kind)` in the actual operator entry points `cmd_run` / `cmd_resume` / `cmd_auto_continue` plus a new `--runtime` flag on each, so the alternate runtime is genuinely opt-in and runtime-backed rather than evaluation-only - when `local` (or unset) is resolved the helper returns None immediately and the shipped in-process flow runs byte-equivalent to the pre-6N path; when `langgraph` is resolved the helper instantiates the mirror, emits a `runtime adapter: langgraph_experimental dispatch hop_kind=<run|resume|auto-continue>` audit note, runs the mirror's `evaluate(...)` pre-pass (which inspects canonical state, refuses fail-closed on contradictions, and emits the per-node audit notes), then returns None so the cycle work still runs through the shipped writers (canonical-artifact precedence preserved). Default-runtime preservation: when `--runtime` is absent, `AGENT_LOOP_RUNTIME` is unset, AND no persisted runtime-config exists, the default `local` runtime is selected and no LangGraph code runs; the shipped local orchestrator's in-process flow is unaffected by any of the wired entry points. Refusal vocabulary: every refusal goes through `HaltError` with the existing halt-status vocabulary (`halted_input_missing`, `halted_contract_version_mismatch`) and is routed through `_halt`; no new halt status is introduced. The mirror is a pure-stdlib structural emulation; the real `langgraph` package is intentionally NOT a runtime dependency in this slice, so the new code path is stdlib-only and adds no third-party requirement. A future slice can swap the implementation behind the same adapter interface to wire the real `langgraph` package without changing the contract surface. LangChain support-layer work, CrewAI evaluation, broader multi-framework orchestration, driving an actual Claude/Codex cycle THROUGH the mirror (the mirror currently runs as a pre-pass and then delegates to the shipped writers), and any promotion of the alternate runtime to default remain deferred.

Focused tests live in:
- `tests/test_documentation_consistency.py` covering the Phase 8A architecture/usage docs: existence + ASCII coverage proves `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/usage.md` both exist at the repo root, are non-empty, and contain no non-ASCII characters; CLI-surface coverage proves every `python scripts/agent_loop.py <subcommand>` mention in either doc resolves to a real `agent_loop.HANDLERS` entry (the docs cannot drift into claiming non-existent commands) and that `docs/usage.md` names every load-bearing operator subcommand by invocation (`status`, `run`, `resume`, `auto-continue`, `inspect-artifacts`, `plan`, `activate`, `set-runtime-config`) so a clean-clone reader can act without cross-referencing `--help` output; ownership-boundary coverage proves `docs/architecture.md` names every orchestrator-owned artifact called out by `CLAUDE.md` (the eight-entry orchestrator-owned list), the three approval modes (`review` / `strict` / `autonomous`), the three Codex verdicts (`APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW` / `NEEDS_FIXES` / `FAILED_REQUIRES_HUMAN`), every runtime adapter id in `agent_loop.RUNTIME_ADAPTERS_SUPPORTED`, the five Phase 6A memory categories, and the four Phase 5C strict-mode halt vocabulary entries; future-behavior coverage proves neither doc presents Phase 9 fully-autonomous PRD-to-product mode as a shipped capability and that MCP / external-UI mentions appear only in disclaiming contexts (roadmap / future / deferred / not implemented), so a new operator cannot read these as current product behavior. Phase 8B extension: the suite was broadened to cover the three operator-facing playbooks (`docs/safety-rules.md`, `docs/approval-modes.md`, `docs/halt-and-recovery.md`); each playbook is asserted to exist + be ASCII-only + non-empty; the same CLI-surface guardrail applies (any `python scripts/agent_loop.py <subcommand>` mention in any playbook must resolve to a real `agent_loop.HANDLERS` entry); content invariants per playbook are: safety doc names the no-git-automation boundary, the recovery-preserving `HaltError` pattern, the `APPROVED_FOR_ACTIVATION` token, the artifact-ownership roles, and every `EVIDENCE_STATES` vocabulary entry; approval-modes doc names every shipped `APPROVAL_MODE_*` value, the three Phase 5C strict-mode gates, both `pre_codex_review` halt-status flavors, the Phase 5B `claude-done.json` handoff artifact, the strict-mode "no strict-gate checkpoint artifacts" boundary (with line-wrap-tolerant whitespace collapse), and the explicit `autonomous` vs Phase 9 disclaimer; halt-and-recovery doc has a section for every status in the canonical `STATUS_RECOVERY_HINTS` map (the doc fails closed if it lags the canonical map), names the `phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval` success terminal, names the three Codex verdicts, and explicitly notes the planner refuses to propose the next phase from the human-required terminal halts; the same Phase 9 / MCP / external-UI no-future-claims guard applies to all three playbooks
- `tests/test_status_recovery.py` covering the Phase 7C status / recovery UX layer: constants coverage proves `STATUS_SIGNAL_VERSION = "phase-7c-v1"`, that `STATUS_RECOVERY_HINTS` is a non-empty dict, that `STATUS_RECOVERY_FALLBACK` is a non-empty string, and that the required recovery points are covered (`HALTED_PRE_CLAUDE_PROMPT`, `HALTED_PRE_FIX_PROMPT`, `HALTED_PRE_CODEX_REVIEW_NORMAL`, `HALTED_PRE_CODEX_REVIEW_FIX`, `HALTED_TOKEN_EXHAUSTION`, `awaiting_claude_implementation`, `phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval`, the in-flight states `claude_implementing` / `claude_fixing` / `evidence_capture` / `awaiting_codex_review`, and the terminal / threshold halts `halted_evidence_incomplete` / `halted_evidence_script_unavailable` / `halted_failed_requires_human` / `halted_max_cycles_reached`); a defense-in-depth assertion proves no shipped status (the same union plus the structural halts and the operator-SIGINT halt) silently resolves to the generic fallback; a contract-accuracy assertion proves the `halted_failed_requires_human` and `halted_max_cycles_reached` hints describe human/manual intervention and do NOT direct the operator at the planner / activate CLI as the immediate next step (the planner refuses from those states; the test catches a regression that would tell operators to run a contractually-blocked command); `_status_recovery_hint` coverage proves every known status returns its mapped hint, an unknown status returns the fallback, and `None` or the empty string returns a dedicated "no status set" hint; `compute_status_summary` coverage proves the function returns a dict on a missing loop-state (with a `load_error` field carrying the `halted_input_missing` halt status and a non-null `recovery_hint`), on a JSON-malformed loop-state (with a `load_error` and a non-null `recovery_hint`), and on a healthy loop-state (surfacing every state field with `load_error=None`), the `recovery_hint` field matches `STATUS_RECOVERY_HINTS[status]` for a known status, `active_checkpoint_present` flips True when a Phase 6D checkpoint is planted via the shipped writer and stays False otherwise, and the call does not mutate loop-state or create the orchestrator log on any code path; render coverage proves `_render_status_summary` carries the signal version header, surfaces a load error when present, omits the load-error line when absent, renders every required row label (Phase, Sub-phase, Task, Status, Approval mode, Cycle, Last verdict, Last verdict phase, Awaiting human for, Active checkpoint), the trailing line is the recovery-hint line carrying the matching hint text, and unset fields render as the literal `(unset)`; CLI coverage proves the `status` subcommand routes through `main(argv)` HANDLERS dispatch end-to-end and returns 0 on missing loop-state, on malformed loop-state, and on healthy loop-state, does NOT mutate loop-state on any code path, does NOT create the orchestrator log on any code path, and is wired into HANDLERS under the literal `"status"` key pointing at `cmd_status`
- `tests/test_artifact_inspection.py` covering the Phase 7B artifact-inspection layer: constants coverage proves `INSPECTION_SIGNAL_VERSION = "phase-7b-v1"`, that `INSPECTION_ARTIFACTS` is a non-empty ordered tuple, that the required Phase 7B set (codex-review.md, fix-prompt.md, current-task.md, current-phase.md, and the six evidence files) is present, and that every row's group label is one of the four known `INSPECTION_GROUPS` (`review`, `prompt`, `planning`, `evidence`); library coverage proves `inspect_artifacts(repo_root)` returns one record per artifact in the same order as `INSPECTION_ARTIFACTS`, a missing artifact carries `present=False` with `size=None` and `modified=None`, a present artifact carries an integer byte size matching the on-disk file and an ISO-8601 UTC `modified` timestamp matching the `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ` shape, mixed presence is reported per-record (not aggregated), the call does NOT mutate any artifact (snapshot-and-compare across the call returns byte-equivalent state), and the call does NOT create the orchestrator log; render coverage proves `_render_inspection_table` carries the signal version and "Phase 7B artifact inspection" header, renders one line per record (every `rel_path` appears in the output), present lines carry `present`, `size=N bytes`, and `modified=` fields, missing lines carry the literal word `missing`, and the trailing summary line carries `total=`, `present=N`, and `missing=N` totals; CLI coverage proves the `inspect-artifacts` subcommand routes through `main(argv)` HANDLERS dispatch end-to-end, exits 0 when no artifacts are present, exits 0 when every artifact is present, does NOT create `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` on either path, does NOT mutate any planted artifact (snapshot-and-compare on a populated tree), is wired into `HANDLERS` under the literal `"inspect-artifacts"` key pointing at `cmd_inspect_artifacts`, and prints repo-relative paths only (never the absolute repo-root path) so a VS Code task terminal can auto-linkify the rows
- `tests/test_vscode_tasks.py` covering the Phase 7A VS Code task entrypoint layer: structural coverage proves `.vscode/tasks.json` is present at the repo root, is valid JSON, declares schema `version = "2.0.0"`, and contains a non-empty list of tasks each carrying `label` / `type` / `command` / `args` with unique labels; delegation coverage proves every task is `type = "process"` (no shell parsing), every python-wrapping task is `command = "python"`, `args[0] = "scripts/agent_loop.py"`, `args[1]` exists in `agent_loop.HANDLERS`, and every task (python-wrapping or evidence-collection) carries `options.cwd = "${workspaceFolder}"` so tasks do not depend on the operator's terminal CWD; no-scope-widening coverage proves no task uses `type = "shell"`, no arg contains a shell metacharacter (`&&`, `||`, `;`, `|`, `>`, `$(`, `\``), no arg invokes a second interpreter inside the arg list (`bash`, `sh`, `zsh`, `powershell`, `pwsh`, `node`, `deno`, `ruby`, `perl`), and the only extra args allowed past a python-wrapping task's `<subcommand>` are `--flag-style` arguments documented by the shipped CLI; coverage-of-flows assertions prove the python-wrapping task set covers every documented common operator subcommand (`check-state`, `validate-artifacts`, `run`, `resume`, `auto-continue`, `plan`, `activate`, `bootstrap-prompt`) and introduces no subcommand outside the shipped HANDLERS surface, AND a dedicated evidence-collection assertion proves the slice ships the `Agent Loop: collect evidence` task that wraps the Phase 2B evidence producer (`scripts/run_checks.sh`) rather than only the `validate-artifacts` gate; dedicated `EvidenceCollectionTaskTests` coverage proves the evidence-collection task is `type = "process"`, `command = "bash"`, `args = ["scripts/run_checks.sh"]` with no extra positional args, the referenced `scripts/run_checks.sh` file actually exists on disk so the task does not silently dead-link, the task uses the same `${workspaceFolder}` cwd convention as the python-wrapping tasks, and exactly one evidence-collection task is declared; a repo-remains-usable-without-VS-Code smoke check proves `agent_loop.HANDLERS` is populated and the wrapped handlers are callable independently of the tasks file
- `tests/test_planner.py` covering the Phase 4A refusal paths, bounded-scope validation, and proposal-generation success path
- `tests/test_planner_activation.py` covering the activation success path, approval-signal refusals, required-input refusals, and activation rollback behavior
- `tests/test_planner_integration.py` covering the Phase 4D post-approval hook: a successful planner invocation after terminal approval, a planner-refusal path that still leaves the orchestrator returning 0, an unexpected-planner-exception path that is logged and still leaves the orchestrator returning 0, and a proof that the integration path performs no activation-owned writes
- `tests/test_planner_adapter.py` covering the Phase 4E adapter seam: the default factory returns the in-process `LocalPlannerAdapter`, the adapter delegates to `run_planner` and preserves the success behavior, the `plan` path and the post-approval refresh both dispatch through the seam, the post-approval path still contains an adapter exception, and the adapterized `plan` path performs no activation-owned writes and does not touch `loop-state.json`
- `tests/test_planner_adapter_selection.py` covering the Phase 4F adapter-selection behavior: local fallback selection, alternate adapter selection, planner-boundary enforcement with rollback on out-of-bound writes, and the continued separation between planning and activation
- `tests/test_langchain_support.py` covering the Phase 6O LangChain support layer: opt-in resolution coverage proves `is_langchain_support_enabled(cli, env)` returns False by default when both inputs are unset; True when the CLI flag is set; True when the CLI flag overrides a falsy env; True on `"1"` and case-insensitive `"TRUE"`; False on `"off"` and on `""`; refuses fail-closed via `HaltError("halted_input_missing", ...)` on an unrecognized env value (e.g. `"garbage"`); guard coverage proves `_ensure_langchain_support_enabled(enabled=True)` passes and `_ensure_langchain_support_enabled(enabled=False)` refuses; `LangChainPromptHelper` coverage proves instantiation without opt-in is safe (no exception until a method is called); `build_prompt_payload()` refuses fail-closed when not enabled; returns a payload with the required schema-stable keys (`support_signal_version`, `built_at`, `advisory_only`, `canonical_precedence_note`, `canonical_state`, `system_prompt_block`); the `advisory_only` flag is `True`; the `canonical_precedence_note` is the literal named constant (`is`-identity); the `canonical_state` subset mirrors loop-state phase / sub_phase / task / cycle_count / approval_mode; refuses on missing `loop-state.json` (the shipped `load_loop_state` `HaltError` is re-raised verbatim); refuses with `halted_contract_version_mismatch` on an unsupported `contract_version`; does NOT mutate loop-state across the call; emits a `langchain support: prompt_payload built ...` audit note when `log_path` is supplied; emits no log writes when `log_path` is omitted (byte-equivalent log); `LangChainRetrievalHelper` coverage proves `retrieve(...)` refuses when not enabled, returns the empty list when no memory matches, preserves the advisory marker on every returned entry from the shipped writer (planted via `write_memory_entry` and retrieved through the wrapper), delegates to the shipped `retrieve_memory_entries(...)` (spy-verified), and refuses fail-closed via `HaltError("halted_input_missing", ...)` when a mocked retrieval returns an entry missing the `MEMORY_RETRIEVAL_ADVISORY_FIELD` marker (defense in depth); `LangChainToolRegistry` coverage proves `list_tools()` and `invoke(...)` both refuse when not enabled, `list_tools()` returns the four named tools (each carrying `kind="read"` and the support-signal version), `invoke("read_loop_state")` returns the loaded state, `invoke("list_memory_entries")` returns repo-relative paths matching planted entries, `invoke("load_active_checkpoint")` returns None when no checkpoint is on disk, `invoke(...)` on an unknown tool refuses with "not in the registry", and every name in the static tool set is read-only (no "write" or "delete" substring); CLI coverage proves the `langchain-support-eval` subcommand routes through `main(argv)` HANDLERS dispatch end-to-end, refuses with rc=2 via `_halt` when not opted in (no output artifact created), writes the output artifact when `--enable-langchain-support` is passed (the artifact carries the required schema-stable keys plus `prompt_payload` and `tools_registered`), honors the `AGENT_LOOP_LANGCHAIN_SUPPORT` env var when the flag is omitted, refuses with rc=2 on a garbage env value (no output artifact), lands a `langchain support: eval written ...` audit note in the orchestrator log, and refuses with rc=2 when loop-state is missing on disk even after opt-in; default-runtime-preservation coverage proves the `langchain-support-eval` invocation does NOT call `run_normal_cycle`, `run_strict_resume`, or `run_auto_continue` (spy-verified), does NOT instantiate the Phase 6N `LangGraphExperimentalRuntimeAdapter` (constructor spy never called), does NOT mutate a planted `.agent-loop/runtime-config.json` (byte-equivalent across the call), library-level exercises of every helper do NOT mutate loop-state, and the eval writes only to the named output artifact and the orchestrator log (every other file under repo-root is byte-equivalent across the call, verified by a snapshot-and-compare)
- `tests/test_runtime_adapter.py` covering the Phase 6N experimental LangGraph runtime mirror layer: runtime-selection coverage proves `resolve_runtime_adapter_id` returns the default `local` when both inputs are None or empty string, lets CLI override env, treats empty CLI as 'unset' and falls through to env, refuses fail-closed via `HaltError("halted_input_missing", ...)` on any unsupported runtime id from either CLI or env (e.g. `crewai`, `openai_swarm`), and accepts explicit `local` selection; factory coverage proves `make_runtime_adapter("local")` returns a `LocalRuntimeAdapter`, `make_runtime_adapter("langgraph")` returns a `LangGraphExperimentalRuntimeAdapter` with `runtime_id = LANGGRAPH_RUNTIME_ID`, and refuses on any other id; `LocalRuntimeAdapter` coverage proves the sentinel returns the local-default outcome dict, does NOT read loop-state (proven by succeeding when no `loop-state.json` is planted), does NOT mutate canonical state, emits one `runtime adapter: local local_default_sentinel` audit note when `log_path` is supplied, and writes nothing to the log when `log_path` is omitted (byte-equivalent); `LangGraphExperimentalRuntimeAdapter` success-shape coverage proves the returned dict carries `runtime_id`, `node_names` (matching `LANGGRAPH_NODE_NAMES`), and `node_results` (one entry per node in the fixed order), the per-node results capture the expected canonical-state subset (`load_state` records phase / sub_phase / cycle_count from loop-state, `validate_state` reports `valid=True`, `consult_memory` reports `memory_entries_seen=0` when no memory is planted, `consult_checkpoint` reports `active_checkpoint_present=False` when no checkpoint is planted, `evaluate` records approval_mode / awaiting_human_for / last_verdict); audit-note coverage proves the adapter emits an `evaluate_begin` book-end note, one `node=<name>` note per node, and an `evaluate_complete` book-end note, and that every emitted line carries the `runtime adapter:` prefix and the `langgraph_experimental` runtime id; when `log_path` is omitted the orchestrator log is byte-equivalent across the call; refusal coverage proves the adapter refuses fail-closed on a missing `loop-state.json` (the shipped `load_loop_state` `HaltError("halted_input_missing", ...)` is re-raised verbatim), on an unsupported `contract_version` (`halted_contract_version_mismatch`), and on loop-state missing a required field, AND proves a refusal at the first node does NOT run later nodes (spies on `list_memory_entries` and `_load_active_checkpoint` confirm they are not called); canonical-precedence coverage proves the adapter does NOT mutate `loop-state.json`, does NOT mutate any planted source memory entry, writes only to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` (every file outside the log is byte-equivalent across the call, verified by a snapshot-and-compare), and re-loads loop-state on every node entry (a spy on `load_loop_state` confirms a call count equal to `len(LANGGRAPH_NODE_NAMES)`, proving the 6M framework-state subordination rule that no caching crosses hops); checkpoint compatibility coverage proves the `consult_checkpoint` node routes through the shipped `_load_active_checkpoint` selector (spy-verified) and reports `active_checkpoint_present=True` when a checkpoint planted through the shipped `write_checkpoint_entry` exists on disk; CLI coverage proves the `runtime-adapter-eval` subcommand routes through `main(argv)` HANDLERS dispatch end-to-end, that the default invocation with no `--runtime` and no env var selects the local runtime (the log contains the local-sentinel note and no `langgraph_experimental` lines), that `--runtime langgraph` selects the mirror (the log contains the begin / complete book-ends), that the `AGENT_LOOP_RUNTIME` env var is honored when `--runtime` is omitted, that `--runtime` overrides the env var, that an unsupported `--runtime` value returns rc=2 via `_halt`, and that the langgraph adapter run without loop-state on disk returns rc=2 via `_halt`; default-runtime-preservation coverage proves the local-runtime selection path never instantiates the `LangGraphExperimentalRuntimeAdapter` (constructor spy never called), that the default-resolution path does not touch any canonical artifact (proven by succeeding when no `loop-state.json` is planted), and that running the local-sentinel CLI against a planted `loop-state.json` leaves it byte-equivalent across the call
- `tests/test_repeated_failure_synthesis.py` covering the Phase 6L repeated-failure memory synthesis layer: success-shape coverage proves the call writes a new `failure` memory entry under `.agent-loop/memory/failure/` whose body carries the `synthesis_signal_version = "phase-6l-v1"` marker, an ordered `source_memory_entries` list of repo-relative paths matching the planted failure entries in `(cycle_count, created_at)` order, `source_count`, `source_cycle_counts`, the active loop-state `phase` / `sub_phase` / `task` / `cycle_count` (so the synthesis is scoped to the current state, not the source entries' state), the `knowledge_type = "failure"` field, the literal `REPEATED_FAILURE_CANONICAL_PRECEDENCE_NOTE`, the applied `min_entries_applied` and `max_source_entries_applied` bounds (proven on a custom override), an audit-log line lands when `log_path` is supplied (carrying the signal version, phase, sub_phase, cycle_count, source count, and bounds), and the orchestrator log is byte-equivalent across the call when `log_path` is omitted; bounds validation coverage proves the synthesis refuses fail-closed on `min_entries` below 2, above `REPEATED_FAILURE_MAX_MIN_ENTRIES`, bool, and non-int; on `max_source_entries` below 2, above `REPEATED_FAILURE_MAX_MAX_SOURCE_ENTRIES`, and bool; on the contradictory `max_source_entries < min_entries` case; and that when more than `max_source_entries` matching sources exist, the newest entries by `(cycle_count, created_at)` are kept (proven by planting 5 entries and capping to 3); source-set selection coverage proves the synthesis refuses when no failure entries are on disk, when only 1 matches under the default `min_entries = 2`, when only 2 match under a custom `min_entries = 3`, that entries from a different phase are excluded, that entries from a different sub_phase are excluded, that prior 6L synthesis entries are skipped (the synthesis layer stays flat - proven by planting two regular failure entries + one 6L-marked entry and asserting source_count == 2 with only the two regulars), AND that Phase 6I distillation `failure` entries (which carry the 6I `distillation_signal_version` marker but NOT the 6L marker) ARE included in the source set; loop-state refusal coverage proves the synthesis refuses on a missing `loop-state.json`, on an unsupported `contract_version` (`halted_contract_version_mismatch`), and on loop-state missing a required field; canonical-precedence preservation proves the call does NOT mutate `loop-state.json`, does NOT mutate any of the source memory entries (byte-equivalent across the call), and writes only under `.agent-loop/memory/failure/` (every file outside that directory is byte-equivalent across the call); CLI coverage proves the `synthesize-repeated-failures` subcommand routes through `main(argv)` HANDLERS dispatch end-to-end and writes the synthesis entry, honors the `--min-entries` override (refuses with rc=2 when only 2 sources are on disk but `--min-entries 3` is requested), honors the `--max-source-entries` override (the recorded `source_count` on the on-disk synthesis entry reflects the operator's cap on a 5-entry candidate set), refuses via `_halt` on insufficient sources with rc=2, and refuses via `_halt` with rc=2 on an invalid `--min-entries` flag
- `tests/test_optional_context_prompt.py` covering the Phase 6K optional-context prompt-integration layer: success-shape coverage proves the returned dict carries every schema-stable top-level key (`integration_signal_version`, `integrated_at`, `source_artifact`, `source_signal_version`, `source_loaded_at`, `max_files_applied`, `max_bytes_per_file_applied`, `advisory_only`, `canonical_precedence_note`, `source_canonical_precedence_note`, `declared_paths`, `files`, `prompt_block`), the top-level `advisory_only` flag is `True`, the two signal-version fields equal the shipped constants (`phase-6k-v1` for the integration, `phase-6j-v1` for the source), both the Phase 6K and the passed-through Phase 6J canonical precedence notes are the literal named constants (consumers can rely on equality), `declared_paths` and `files` preserve declaration order, every file entry carries `advisory_only = True` as an `is`-True structural marker, the bound fields are passed through from the source payload (no integration-side widening), the rendered `prompt_block` contains every source path AND every excerpt verbatim, the audit log line lands when `log_path` is supplied and the log is byte-equivalent across the call when `log_path` is omitted, and a structurally-valid 6J payload with zero declared files renders an explicit "no optional-context files" block instead of an empty / silent prompt block; contradictory / out-of-bound refusal coverage proves the integration refuses on an unsupported source `context_signal_version` (e.g. `phase-6z-vX`), on a `canonical_precedence_note` that drifts from the shipped Phase 6J literal, on a `files` list whose length contradicts `declared_paths`, on a `files` length above `max_files_applied`, on `max_files_applied` above `OPTIONAL_CONTEXT_MAX_MAX_FILES`, on `max_bytes_per_file_applied` above `OPTIONAL_CONTEXT_MAX_BYTES_PER_FILE`, on `max_files_applied` zero, on `max_bytes_per_file_applied` negative, on a file entry whose `advisory_only` is False (the integration refuses any entry that is not explicitly advisory), on an `excerpt_byte_size` above `max_bytes_per_file_applied`, and on a `source_path` that does not match the matching `declared_paths` index; malformed / missing refusal coverage proves the integration refuses on a missing source artifact, a directory at the source path, an unreadable source (mocked `OSError` on `Path.read_text`), a non-JSON source, a top-level non-dict source, a missing required top-level key, a non-list `files`, a non-dict file entry, a file entry missing a required key, a non-list `declared_paths`, and a non-string `declared_paths` entry; canonical-precedence preservation proves the library function does NOT create the default output artifact (only the CLI handler does), does NOT mutate the source 6J artifact across the call, does NOT re-read any of the declared source files (the integration succeeds even when the underlying repo files referenced by `declared_paths` do not exist on disk - the bounded 6J excerpts are the only data consumed), and does NOT mutate a planted `.agent-loop/loop-state.json`; bounded-inclusion coverage proves the per-entry `truncated` flag is preserved from the 6J source, the per-entry `excerpt_byte_size` is preserved, the per-entry `excerpt` bytes equal the 6J source excerpt verbatim (the integration adds no expansion), and the rendered `prompt_block` reports the per-entry truncation flag explicitly; CLI coverage proves the `integrate-optional-context` subcommand routes through `main(argv)` HANDLERS dispatch end-to-end and writes the JSON payload to `.agent-loop/optional-context-prompt.json` by default, honors a repo-relative `--output` override, honors a repo-relative `--source` override (the default source path remains absent), refuses fail-closed via `_halt` on an absolute `--output` path (rc=2, no artifact written), refuses on a `--output` path that resolves outside the repo root via `..`, refuses on an absolute `--source` path, refuses on a `--source` path that resolves outside the repo root via `..`, refuses on a missing source artifact via `_halt` leaving no output artifact behind, overwrites an existing output artifact rather than appending (it is an audit artifact regenerable from the 6J payload), and refuses fail-closed via `_halt` on a contradictory source payload
- `tests/test_optional_context.py` covering the Phase 6J declared optional-context file loading layer: success-shape coverage proves the returned dict carries every schema-stable top-level key (`context_signal_version`, `loaded_at`, `max_files_applied`, `max_bytes_per_file_applied`, `declared_paths`, `files`, `canonical_precedence_note`), the order of declared paths is preserved in both `declared_paths` and `files`, the `canonical_precedence_note` is the literal named constant (consumers can rely on equality), and the default limits land on the payload when the caller does not override them; canonical-precedence preservation proves the library function does NOT create the default output artifact (only the CLI handler does), does NOT mutate any source file across a load call, marks every loaded file with `advisory_only = True`, and does not append to the orchestrator log when `log_path` is omitted; canonical-precedence preservation proves the call does NOT mutate `loop-state.json`, does NOT mutate the canonical source artifacts, and writes only inside `.agent-loop/memory/<category>/` (every file outside that directory is byte-equivalent across the call), and an idempotency refusal does NOT add additional memory entries beyond the original write; a clean single-hop dispatch (the dispatched continuation does not re-record exhaustion) terminates the chain after one hop with the transitional `evidence_capture` left on disk; a multi-hop chain (the dispatched continuation records a fresh token-exhaustion event for the first N hops via the orchestrator's own writer) walks exactly N+1 hops and then terminates cleanly; canonical-precedence preservation - a successful chain does NOT advance `cycle_count`, does NOT mutate `phase` / `sub_phase` / `task` / `approval_mode`, and does NOT log any autonomous-mode bypass (the chain does not widen Phase 5 autonomy); - `tests/test_token_exhaustion_continuation.py` covering the Phase 6F token-exhaustion continuation layer: `record_token_exhaustion(...)` writes a token-exhaustion checkpoint capturing the PRE-suspension cycle context (phase / sub_phase / task / cycle_count / approval_mode / status / awaiting_human_for plus the active prompt path and the bounded continuation budget) and transitions loop-state to `HALTED_TOKEN_EXHAUSTION` + `awaiting_human_for = "token_exhaustion_continuation"` so the halt is auditable from the canonical state artifacts, refuses when loop-state is already at any `halted_*` status (no clobbering an existing recovery point), when the cycle is terminally complete (`phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval`), when the `active_prompt_path` is out of vocabulary, and when `loop-state.json` is missing, accepts both `.agent-loop/claude-prompt.md` and `.agent-loop/fix-prompt.md` as valid prompts, and emits a `token exhaustion recorded:` audit note; `run_token_exhaustion_resume(...)` consumes the active checkpoint by writing a new superseding checkpoint with `continuation_budget - 1` and restoring loop-state to the pre-suspension `status` / `awaiting_human_for`, emits a `token-exhaustion resume consumed:` audit note showing `budget N -> N-1`, refuses with `_halt` only on the "status is not the token-exhaustion halt" case (where no recovery point can be destroyed) and uses the recovery-preserving stderr+log+exit-2 pattern for every other refusal (no checkpoint on disk, malformed checkpoint, suspension_reason mismatch, cycle-identity mismatch, exhausted budget, saved status outside `TOKEN_EXHAUSTION_SUPPORTED_RESUME_STATUSES`), proves two consecutive consumptions take a budget of 2 to 0 and a third refuses;
