CLI coverage of the `record-token-exhaustion` subcommand proves the orchestrator runtime actually enters `HALTED_TOKEN_EXHAUSTION` end-to-end (the HANDLERS dispatch chain), proves the optional `--continuation-budget` override is honored, proves both `.agent-loop/claude-prompt.md` and `.agent-loop/fix-prompt.md` are accepted while an unknown `--active-prompt-path` is rejected by argparse before the handler runs, proves a pre-existing halt is refused via `_halt` (no recovery point silently clobbered), and proves the full `record-token-exhaustion` then `resume` sequence reaches the token-exhaustion continuation entry-point end-to-end without advancing `cycle_count`; `cmd_resume(...)` routing inspects the persisted halt status BEFORE dispatch so `HALTED_TOKEN_EXHAUSTION` routes to `run_token_exhaustion_resume` (mocked) and a strict-gate halt or a missing loop-state file routes to `run_strict_resume` unchanged (the load-bearing safety property that prevents the strict-resume status check from clobbering the token-exhaustion recovery point); canonical-precedence preservation - a refused token-exhaustion resume does not mutate the on-disk checkpoint bytes, a successful resume does NOT advance cycle_count (no phase progression, no autonomy widening), the active phase / sub_phase / task / approval_mode are preserved across the resume, and a strict-gate halt with a token-exhaustion checkpoint on disk still refuses via the Phase 6E suspension_reason mismatch path without clobbering the strict-gate state
- `tests/test_checkpoint_resume.py` covering the Phase 6E checkpoint-backed resume layer: `_load_active_checkpoint(...)` returns None when no checkpoint directory exists, returns the single payload when one exists, selects the active checkpoint by filesystem mtime (the most recently WRITTEN file wins, which actually tracks write order rather than filename order - the Phase 6D filename `{YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ}-{sha256_short(body)[:8]}.json` has a second-granularity timestamp and a body-hash tail uncorrelated with write order, so same-second writes could otherwise pick the wrong file by lexical order; lexical filename remains a deterministic secondary tie-breaker only when two files share an identical mtime), reads ONLY the active checkpoint so a malformed historical entry does not block resume when a newer valid checkpoint exists, and still propagates the Phase 6D fail-closed `HaltError` when the chosen active checkpoint is itself malformed; `_validate_checkpoint_against_loop_state(...)` accepts a fully matching checkpoint silently and refuses fail-closed with `halted_input_missing` for every individual mismatch dimension (`phase`, `sub_phase`, `task`, `cycle_count`, `approval_mode`, `awaiting_human_for`, and - when the loop is paused at a strict-gate halt - `suspension_reason`); `run_strict_resume(...)` integration coverage proves backward compatibility (no checkpoint on disk still dispatches the existing Phase 5C strict-gate continuation), proves the matching-checkpoint case dispatches and writes a `checkpoint validated for resume:` audit note, proves the most-recently-written-checkpoint-among-many is the one consulted, proves a malformed older checkpoint alongside a newer valid one still dispatches (only the active entry is read), refuses fail-closed when a stale `cycle_count` / `task` / `sub_phase` checkpoint exists, refuses fail-closed when the checkpoint's `suspension_reason` contradicts the active strict-gate halt, refuses fail-closed when the active on-disk checkpoint is malformed (missing required field, unrecognized `checkpoint_signal_version`) and writes a `strict resume refused:` audit note; canonical-precedence preservation - every refusal leaves the saved strict-gate `status` / `awaiting_human_for` / `approval_mode` intact so a corrected operator state allows a fresh resume to dispatch, never calls any of the four strict-gate dispatch helpers, and a successful resume does not mutate the on-disk checkpoint bytes
- `tests/test_checkpoint_storage.py` covering the shipped Phase 6D checkpoint artifact storage layer: a valid `write_checkpoint_entry(...)` call creates a file under `.agent-loop/memory/checkpoint/`, populates every Phase 6A required memory-metadata field plus every Phase 6D required checkpoint-body field (`checkpoint_signal_version`, `task`, `status`, `approval_mode`, `awaiting_human_for`, `active_prompt_path`, `suspension_reason`, `continuation_budget`), accepts each of the eight allowed `suspension_reason` values (the four base reasons `token_exhaustion` / `human_interrupt` / `process_killed` / `orchestrator_restart` plus the four Phase 5C strict-gate halt statuses), accepts both allowed `active_prompt_path` values (claude-prompt.md and fix-prompt.md), accepts a `continuation_budget` of zero, and accepts both `None` and a non-empty string for `awaiting_human_for`; malformed-checkpoint write refusal for empty `task` / `status` / `approval_mode`, wrong-type or empty-string `awaiting_human_for`, out-of-vocabulary `active_prompt_path` or `suspension_reason`, and `continuation_budget` that is bool / non-int / negative; read-side schema validation refuses a non-checkpoint category, a missing or non-JSON or non-dict body, a body missing any required checkpoint field, an unrecognized `checkpoint_signal_version`, an out-of-vocabulary `active_prompt_path` or `suspension_reason`, a bad `continuation_budget`, and (mirroring the write-side guards so a hand-edited artifact cannot bypass the checkpoint contract on read) empty / non-string `task` / `status` / `approval_mode` and an `awaiting_human_for` that is the wrong type or an empty string (None remains accepted); write-boundary enforcement - checkpoint entries live only under `.agent-loop/memory/checkpoint/`, a write does not mutate a planted `.agent-loop/loop-state.json`, and a forced filename collision is refused rather than overwritten (the Phase 6B append-mostly guarantee is inherited); `list_checkpoint_entries(...)` returns `[]` when the checkpoint subdirectory is absent and returns only checkpoint entries when other memory categories are also present; the writer still emits the Phase 6B `memory write:` audit note with `category='checkpoint'` when a `log_path` is supplied (and creates no log file when it is omitted)
- `tests/test_memory_retrieval.py` covering the shipped Phase 6C selective durable memory retrieval layer: relevant-entry filtering across phase / sub_phase / category dimensions and their combined intersection (task-level scoping is implicit in the (phase, sub_phase) match per the 6C contract narrowing because the canonical state model assigns one active task per active (phase, sub_phase) pair); bounded result limits including the default limit, custom-limit-below-max, and fail-closed refusals for limits that are zero, negative, non-int, bool, or above `MEMORY_RETRIEVAL_MAX_LIMIT`; malformed-entry refusal that halts the whole retrieval call on malformed JSON, missing required fields, unrecognized `signal_version`, and unknown on-disk category (no silent skipping of bad entries); unknown-category refusal for caller-supplied category filters, both alone and mixed with a valid category; advisory-only marker - every returned payload structurally carries `MEMORY_RETRIEVAL_ADVISORY_FIELD = True` so a consumer can assert advisory status without relying on docstrings, the marker is present on entries returned for every one of the five Phase 6A categories, and the marker is never written back to disk; canonical-precedence preservation - retrieval never mutates `.agent-loop/loop-state.json`, never creates any new file on disk, mutating a returned payload does not write through to disk, and empty-memory / no-matching-phase return an empty list rather than halting; newest-first ordering by `created_at`; audit-note signature when `log_path` is supplied that names phase / sub_phase / categories / limit / matched / returned (and no log file created when it is omitted, and no `task=` field in the audit signature); and a required non-empty active phase string at the boundary
- `tests/test_memory_storage.py` covering the shipped Phase 6B structured durable memory storage layer: a valid write for each of the five Phase 6A categories (`decision`, `failure`, `preference`, `summary`, `checkpoint`) populates every required metadata field (`signal_version`, `category`, `phase`, `sub_phase`, `cycle_count`, `source_artifact_path`, `created_at`) and writes the entry under `.agent-loop/memory/<category>/`; missing-metadata refusal for empty `phase` / `sub_phase` / `source_artifact_path` and non-int `cycle_count`; unknown-category refusal at write time and at `list_memory_entries` time so the five-category set cannot be silently extended at runtime; append-mostly behavior - two writes create two distinct files and a `supersedes` reference is recorded on the new entry without mutating the prior entry's bytes; a file-collision at the deterministic filename target is refused rather than overwritten; write-boundary enforcement - the scope guard refuses any path that resolves outside `.agent-loop/memory/` while accepting in-scope paths, and a full write+list cycle never mutates any canonical artifact like `.agent-loop/loop-state.json`; read-side schema validation refuses missing-field, unknown-category, unrecognized-`signal_version`, empty-or-malformed-JSON, and missing-file entries; `list_memory_entries` returns an empty list when the memory dir is absent and a category-filtered list when `category` is provided; the writer appends a `memory write:` audit note to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` when a `log_path` is supplied
- `tests/test_review_follow_up.py` covering the Phase 5E post-review reconciliation: path-based inference returns Codex for all-Codex-path issues and Claude for all-non-Codex-path issues; parse-time refusal when the file list mixes Codex-owned planning paths with Claude-owned implementation paths and no explicit `Owner:` field is provided; an explicit `Owner:` line overrides path-based inference (including the Claude-overrides-Codex-path direction); supported Codex-owned auto-fixes apply when File(s) is a subset of the action's allow-list and the same action is refused at reconciliation time when File(s) extends outside that allow-list; a reconciliation classification summary lands in `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log` before any side-effect; the per-action note and the fix-prompt synchronization note still land in the success path; and the pre-Phase-5E behavior on the existing mixed-review and codex-only-review tests is preserved
- `tests/test_approval_modes.py` covering the Phase 5B review-mode initial slice, the Phase 5C strict-mode pauses, AND the Phase 5D autonomous-mode initial slice: activation defaults `approval_mode` to `"review"` and resets `awaiting_human_for` to null while preserving a pre-existing non-review mode; the normal-cycle backfill applies the same defaults to pre-Phase-5B state; the verdict handler sets `awaiting_human_for = "phase_complete_awaiting_human_approval"` on `APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW` and clears it on `NEEDS_FIXES` / `FAILED_REQUIRES_HUMAN`; `.agent-loop/claude-done.json` writes carry every required Phase 5A field for both implementation and fix completion and reject an invalid mode without leaving a partial file; a stale `claude-done.json` is superseded by a new implementation cycle and cleared by a new fix-prompt issuance; the baseline pre-Phase-5B terminal status / verdict writes still hold. Strict-mode coverage proves each of the three gates fires at the right point (no Claude adapter call before `pre_claude_prompt`, no Codex adapter call before `pre_codex_review` in either the normal or fix cycle), the resume subcommand refuses from a non-strict-halt status, resume after `pre_claude_prompt` dispatches into the cycle_count-increment continuation, resume after `pre_codex_review` skips straight to Codex review without re-invoking Claude or re-incrementing the counter, resume after `pre_fix_prompt` runs one fix cycle, and `review` mode never fires any strict gate. Autonomous-mode coverage proves none of the three Phase 5C strict-mode gates (`pre_claude_prompt`, `pre_fix_prompt`, `pre_codex_review` - the last with two halt-status flavors so resume can route) fires under `autonomous` (Claude/Codex adapters are reached without a halt) and each bypass is logged to `.agent-loop/orchestrator.log`; the autonomous continuation still halts on `FAILED_REQUIRES_HUMAN`, on the `cycle_count >= max_cycles` threshold for `NEEDS_FIXES`, on a missing-evidence halt, and on the `APPROVED_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW` phase-complete gate (autonomy never auto-progresses phases); NEEDS_FIXES within threshold runs exactly one fix cycle (one Claude invocation, one Codex review) and terminates correctly on the subsequent APPROVED verdict

Deferred to later phases generally: later Phase 6 implementation slices that build on the shipped Phase 6O LangChain support layer (including broader framework evaluation, write-side tools, and CrewAI-style multi-agent work), later Phase 7 editor-integration slices that build on the shipped Phase 7C status/reset/recovery layer (Phase 7D / 7+: dashboards, MCP support, broader UX polish), and post-Phase-9 roadmap work after the active Phase 9C orchestrator-driven prompt-handoff slice is complete. Phase 4D was the first slice that intentionally extended the Phase 3 orchestrator surface with the contained post-approval planner hook; Phases 4E and 4F completed the planner-adapter seam and one alternate adapter path while keeping the planner write boundary and the separate human-controlled approval/activation steps unchanged; Phase 5B shipped the default `review` runtime path plus the machine-readable completion handoff artifact, Phase 5C shipped the strict-mode pauses plus the `resume` subcommand, Phase 5D shipped the narrow `autonomous` continuation path with auditable gate-bypass logging while preserving the existing `review` and `strict` behavior, Phase 5E shipped deterministic ownership-aware post-review reconciliation with per-action allow-list enforcement and audit-log classification summaries while preserving every shipped runtime mode, Phase 5F shipped automatic phase-start Claude prompt generation from the canonical phase/task artifacts, Phase 6A documented the durable memory rules later 6x implementation slices must follow, Phase 6B shipped the storage foundation for that memory layer, Phase 6C shipped selective advisory-only retrieval, Phase 6D shipped checkpoint artifact storage, Phase 6E shipped checkpoint-backed resume handling, Phase 6F shipped token-exhaustion continuation handling, Phase 6G shipped automatic continuation chaining, Phase 6H shipped continuation-prompt construction, Phase 6I shipped phase-boundary memory distillation, Phase 6J shipped declared optional-context file loading, Phase 6K shipped the prompt-integration surface that consumes that payload into a deterministic advisory-only prompt block, Phase 6L shipped the repeated-failure memory synthesis surface that distills recurring failure patterns from prior `failure`-category entries into a new append-mostly advisory `failure` entry, Phase 6M shipped the runtime adapter contract future framework-backed runtimes must satisfy, Phase 6N shipped the first opt-in experimental LangGraph runtime mirror under that contract as a pure-stdlib structural emulation that preserves the local runtime as the default and exercises every 6M contract dimension without adding a third-party runtime dependency, and Phase 6O shipped the narrow optional LangChain support layer for prompt construction, selective retrieval, and tool abstraction as a default-off, opt-in, structurally-subordinate helper surface that never owns the runtime, never overrides canonical state, and adds no third-party runtime dependency.
